The foreign key stays. A normalization checks again for its version's record after taking the filer's lock, so two concurrent ones don't both run.
- **A failed normalization is recorded; the ingest still succeeds.** The documents an ingest fetched are recorded independently of the financials derived from one of them, so a normalization error (a companyfacts shape change, a bug like the one above) no longer fails the job and hides the fetched filings behind retries. Its transaction rolls back (nothing partial is stored), then an insert-only, audited `financial_normalization_failure` row (migration `0037`: version, company, normalizer version, error class and message, job) is written, the job's artifacts carry `financial_normalization: {"status": "failed", ...}`, and `atlas_financial_normalizations_total{outcome="failed"}` counts it (alert `AtlasFinancialNormalizationFailing`). The version stays unnormalized, so the next ingest tries again; a success is the usual `financial_normalization` row. A database outage (`OperationalError`, `InterfaceError`) still fails the attempt, which the queue retries. Rejected: failing the ingest (the previous behaviour), which retried a deterministic failure until the job failed and marked coverage as missing when only the financials were.
- **Not done here:** strict replay (research §4.1: also require the companyfacts Source Version's `ingested_at <= as_of`) belongs to Phase 6a replay; the observations keep `source_version_id` for it.

## 2026-09-29: Investigator Claims → Assertions (Phase 3-6a ticket 10)

- **The Investigator is a role on the role-call foundation** (`atlas/roles/investigator.py`, prompt `investigator.v1.md`), run by the `extract_claims` job (`atlas.claims`) over the Source Versions its payload names (`{"source_version_ids": [...], "question"?, "run_id"?}`; at most 25, the spec's per-run document bound). The job starts its own run (`kind = claim_extraction`) unless given an unfinished `run_id` (for ticket 14's investigations, which then finish the run themselves).
- **Passages, not whole sections.** Each Source Version is split by the retention sectioner (`sec-items-v1`), so a passage's section is a memory document's section; sections are cut into windows of at most 3,000 characters at line breaks. A window is sent when it names a known company other than the document's own (**entity tag**: display or legal name, or the legal name without corporate suffixes, case-sensitive and whole-word, so "coherent optics" never tags Coherent) or when a recall for the job's `question`, scoped by `company:` tags to the documents' companies, resolves to its section (**recall hit**). Entity-tagged windows first, then recall-only ones; at most `ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGES` (24), `ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_PASSAGES_PER_CALL` (6) per call. Both are starting values, not measured.
- **A Claim names a passage and offsets in it.** The model answers `passage_id`, `quote`, `quote_start`, `quote_end` (code points into the passage's text); Atlas adds the passage's start to get the Source Version span (the spec's "`source_version_id`, char offsets"). The span is then checked by **the existing Assertion span check** (`check_quote`): exact text at exact offsets, no search. A Claim whose quote occurs elsewhere is rejected (`quote_mismatch`, the reason saying where it does occur), never moved. **Open risk:** models are poor at counting characters, so live runs may reject many true quotes on offsets alone; the live suite should measure it before deciding whether to let Atlas locate a quote that occurs exactly once in its passage. *(Measured and decided: see "Claim quotes are located, not trusted" below; the no-search rule is superseded.)*
- **Checks, in order** (the first failure is the rejection reason): whitelisted predicate; known layer; passage sent in that call; known subject, known company object or named product object, subject ≠ object; offsets inside the passage; the span check; the quote names both parties (a company by name, or the document's own company as "we"/"our"/"the Company"); the quote uses directional language for its predicate (`no_directional_language`). Rejected Claims are `claim` rows with `outcome = rejected`, a `reason_code` and a message, visible at `GET /api/v1/claims` and counted in `atlas_claims_rejected_total{reason}`.
- **The whitelist and direction.** Build plan §5.5's nine predicates, each with a stated direction (`supplies`: the object is the subject's customer; `buys_from`: the object is the subject's supplier) and object kind (a company, or a product/material/technology in `object_text`). Nothing is ever inferred from a Claim: one accepted Claim is one Assertion with the proposed predicate. "Works with", "partners with" and "collaborates with" map to no predicate; the response schema leaves `predicate` a string so such a proposal is a rejected Claim with that reason rather than a failed call.
- **The layer taxonomy** as slugs: `substrate`, `epi`, `chip-laser`, `dsp`, `module`, `contract-manufacturing`, `system` (spec: substrate → epi → chip/laser → DSP → module → system, plus contract manufacturing). The same vocabulary as the universe config and the company table (`atlas.companies.Layer`).
- **Co-mention guard (the gate).** Two companies named in one sentence carry no relation: a quote must contain a predicate-specific directional cue (a conservative pattern list in `atlas/claims/predicates.py`), or the Claim is rejected and no Assertion is created. So every Assertion an extraction creates is Relationship-eligible, and a co-mention-only passage (the Coherent 10-K's peer group naming Lumentum) produces none, whatever the model claims. The cue proves only that directional language is present; whether its direction is right is the reviewer's job (ticket 12), which extends this list.
- **Resumable batches.** Progress is stored per batch with the passages, so a job requeued by an LLM quota or outage (`extract_claims` is pausable) resumes at its next batch in the same run. A quarantined answer is a batch with no Claims; a spent run budget ends the extraction `budget_exhausted` with what it found, and the job succeeds. Accepted Claims' Assertions are created inside the batch's transaction (`Assertions.create_within`), so a crash never leaves an Assertion without its Claim.
- **Epistemic types.** An Investigator Claim is `direct_source_statement`, `company_claim` or `third_party_report` (the response schema's enum); an agent's own inference is never a Claim.

## 2026-09-29: Scout and leads (Phase 3-6a ticket 08)

- **The Scout is a role** (`atlas/roles/scout.py`, prompt `roles/prompts/scout.v1.md`) on the ticket-07 role caller: request `{theme_id, theme_title, theme_description, research_question, max_queries}`, response `{queries: [{query, purpose}]}`. The open gaps are the **Bottlenecks mental model's content**, sent as one quoted low-trust `retrieved_data` item (`source: mental-model:bottlenecks`), and only once Hindsight has refreshed the model (`last_refreshed_at` set): before that it holds 0.10.1's placeholder text, which isn't gaps. The model's text isn't parsed into gaps by code; the Scout reads it. The discovery records `gaps_source = mental-model:bottlenecks@<last_refreshed_at>`.
- **At most 10 queries, decided by code.** The schema doesn't bound the list (a `maxItems` MiniMax might not enforce would only cost a repair); the discovery keeps the first `ATLAS_DISCOVERY_MAX_QUERIES` (default 10, validated ≤ 10) distinct queries, compared case- and whitespace-insensitively, and records how many were proposed.
- **SearXNG:** `GET /search?q=...&format=json&engines=bing,brave` (`ATLAS_SEARXNG_ENGINES`; always named, as the owner's instance's defaults are partly broken). Only the first result page is read (no `pageno`). `unresponsive_engines` is stored per query as `[{engine, reason}]`. A failed search (HTTP error, connection failure, a body that isn't SearXNG JSON) is recorded on its query and the others proceed; only when every query failed does the attempt fail, and the retry reuses the Scout's queries (no second LLM call) and searches the failed ones again. **SearXNG failures never pause the queue**: it isn't a Hindsight/LiteLLM dependency, and pausing would hold back retains for a search outage. The `discover` job kind is pausable for the Scout's LiteLLM call (429/outage pause as for every role).
- **Leads are Tier C metadata, deduplicated by canonical URL** globally (not per theme): scheme and host lowercased, a leading `www.` and default ports dropped, fragment dropped, tracking parameters (`utm_*`, `gclid`, `fbclid`, `msclkid`, `mc_cid`, `mc_eid`, `igshid`) removed and the rest sorted, a trailing `/` dropped from a non-root path; path case and the scheme kept (`http` and `https` stay distinct). Non-web URLs (e.g. `ftp:`) make no lead. A lead keeps its first sighting's URL, title, snippet, published date (the day of SearXNG's `publishedDate`) and query; its `engines` are the union over sightings; every result is also kept as a `lead_sighting`. Syndicated copies at different URLs stay separate leads; grouping them is Evidence Families' job (ticket 11).
- **Leads are never Evidence nor retained:** nothing links a lead to the source ledger, Assertions or memory documents, and discovery enqueues no job. Proposing Candidates from leads is ticket 09.
- **The per-run lead budget (spec §7.4 "≤ 10 new web leads per question") isn't applied here**: ticket 08 bounds queries, not leads. The investigation orchestration (ticket 14) applies its lead budget when it picks leads to investigate.
- **Metrics:** `atlas_discovery_queries_total{status=searched|failed}`, `atlas_discovery_unresponsive_engines_total{engine}`, `atlas_leads_total`. Scout tokens appear in `atlas_llm_tokens_total{kind="discovery"}` once the run finishes.


## 2026-09-29: Pacing to subscription windows; archive backup; staged rollout

- **Pacing:** the owner's ChatGPT/Codex and MiniMax subscriptions each renew in rolling 5-hour windows. LLM-backed work is spread across those windows, not confined to the 01:00–07:00 night window, and each provider gets a budget per window (build ticket 27). The 429 pause remains the backstop.
- **Rollout:** the ten companies not yet ingested are rolled out one at a time under that budget, each with an ingest plan and a retain cap first. The unbounded bootstrap of 2026-09 must not recur.
- **Archive backup:** the archive PVC (`atlas-archive`, ceph-block) is backed up by the owner's VolSync, with MinIO as the backup target. The owner sets this up in home-ops. Until it exists and a restore has been checked, snapshots (Phase 6a) are not a durability guarantee. WORM (MinIO object lock) and the restore drill stay in Phase 7.
- **Before building on it:** a small live smoke test of claim extraction runs (build ticket 28), because exact quote spans from a live model are the main untested risk.

## 2026-09-29: Non-SEC sources after the terms check

Checked robots.txt and the terms pages on 2026-09-29 (no filings downloaded):
- **Innolight (HKEX 03308):** no automation. The HKEX Terms of Use prohibit bots and scrapers on hkexnews; the routes are a licensed Issuer Information feed Service (IIS) or written consent (info@hkex). Until then the owner imports documents fetched by hand (`manual_import`, audited, with the origin URL and publication time).
- **IQE:** the FCA National Storage Mechanism, whose terms allow lawful use subject to the AUP. LSE RNS is not allowed (robots disallow and personal-use terms), nor is IQE's Brighter IR iframe (`Disallow: /`).
- **Soitec:** the AMF info-financière open API (Licence Ouverte / etalab-2.0, 10k calls per IP per day). Euronext is not allowed (its terms prohibit robots and systematic retrieval), and soitec.com's terms forbid reuse without permission.
## 2026-09-29: Entity resolution (Phase 3-6a build ticket 02)

- **One deterministic pipeline** (`atlas/identity/resolver.py`, from docs/research/identity-apis.md §4 on branch `research/identity-apis`): validate (ISIN Luhn, LEI mod-97, CIK padding; an invalid identifier is dropped, never repaired), then one registry per strong identifier (CIK → SEC submissions; LEI → GLEIF; ISIN → GLEIF issuer + OpenFIGI composite; ticker on a US venue → SEC ticker file + OpenFIGI), then cross-registry checks, then name + jurisdiction links, then name-only candidates. Names are equal only after `normalize_name` (upper-case, dots dropped, trailing legal forms INC/INCORPORATED/CORP/CORPORATION/LTD/LIMITED/PLC/AG/NV/SA/CO/COMPANY dropped), never by similarity. **HOLDINGS stays significant**: dropping it would equate a registrant with its operating subsidiary (MACOM).
- **Tiers are per identifier** (a `Proposal` for each CIK, LEI and listing), and a resolution's tier is its worst proposal's: `exact` (a strong identifier, all others agree), `corroborated` (joined on name + jurisdiction: SEC `stateOfIncorporation` `PA` ↔ GLEIF `US-PA`, or for a non-US issuer GLEIF's country against the company's), `candidate` (name only, a ticker without a venue, an ADR, several matches, a name the entity doesn't have, jurisdictions that differ or can't be compared, an LEI that is RETIRED/MERGED or has a successor) and `conflict` (two sources disagree: nothing is proposed, both chains are returned). A LAPSED LEI still identifies.
- **Only exact and corroborated commit automatically, and a CIK↔LEI link never does**: no source links them, so every such link is `owner_confirmation` and waits in the review queue (`GET /api/v1/identity-mappings?review_state=pending`, `POST …/{id}/confirm|reject`). The database enforces it (`identity_mapping_owner_never_auto`). For the universe (whose CIKs come from config) every LEI therefore waits for the owner, once per company; confirming one answers the company's other pending LEIs.
- **Listings:** `exchange_mic` stays the operating MIC; the resolver asks OpenFIGI with the segment MICs (`XNAS` → `XNGS`/`XNMS`/`XNCM`, never `XNAS` itself, which doesn't match) plus the country composite (`exchCode US`) for US venues, and stores the composite FIGI, the share-class FIGI and the matched `segment_mic`. Currency comes from the venue. SEC's `exchange` labels map only `Nasdaq` → XNAS and `NYSE` → XNYS; OTC, CBOE and null listings are skipped with a note. An ADR (`securityType2 = Depositary Receipt`) is a candidate: its underlying line and ratio are the owner's (no source gives the ratio).
- **A listing's start date:** no source gives one, so a listing entity resolution finds and config doesn't name starts on the day Atlas observed it (`valid_from`). Config listings keep their primary-source dates.
- **Unsponsored-ADR CIKs** (seed-list finding 3) are never proposed: the universe passes each company's `ignored_ciks`, and a CIK whose recent forms are only depositary F-6 registrations is reported as a shell.
- **Sources and limits:** SEC shares the process-wide 10 requests/s limiter and needs `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT`; GLEIF 60/min (`fuzzycompletions` is never called); OpenFIGI keyless 25/min, at most 10 jobs per request, 429s wait for `ratelimit-reset`. Base URLs are settings (`ATLAS_SEC_FILES_URL`, `ATLAS_SEC_DATA_URL`, `ATLAS_GLEIF_URL`, `ATLAS_OPENFIGI_URL`). The undocumented `efts.sec.gov` endpoints and OpenFIGI's keyword search are not used.
- **The universe first:** `resolve_mention` (what Candidates from leads call) looks up the universe before any network call: CIK, LEI, a listing by ISIN or ticker on its venue valid at `as_of`, then a legal, display or alias name (former names only inside their interval). Name and bare-ticker hits are `candidate`. Its result's `company_id` is set for a universe company and None for an unseeded one.
- **Aliases:** SEC's current and former names (with dates) are stored once the CIK is committed or confirmed; GLEIF's legal and other names when the LEI is confirmed.
## 2026-09-29: Relationships and review (Phase 3-6a ticket 12)

- **An edge is (subject, predicate, object, layer).** *(Superseded on 2026-10-01: the layer is optional and no longer part of the identity; see "A layer only when the quote supports one" below.)* The object is a company, or for the product predicates the Assertion's `value_json.object_text` (compared casefolded, whitespace collapsed). The layer is part of the identity, so "Coherent supplies NVIDIA" at `chip-laser` and at `module` are two edges and a layer confusion shows up as its own edge. The product is not: an edge lists the products its Assertions name. `competes_with` is stored in the direction proposed (no merging of A–B with B–A).
- **The `review_relationships` job** machine-reviews Assertions (named in its payload, or a sweep of every open one with a whitelisted predicate and no review yet, oldest first, at most 100). An Assertion is reviewed once (`relationship_review`, unique per Assertion, insert-only). It forms a Relationship only if its predicate is whitelisted, it is open (not rejected or superseded), `value_json.layer` is a known layer, its object is present and differs from the subject, **and its quote has directional language for the predicate**; otherwise it is recorded `not_eligible` with the reason and forms no edge. So a researcher's manual Assertion quoting a co-mention is never a Relationship either (the spec's gate), not just the Investigator's.
- **Machine-reviewed = every check passes:** the verbatim span (the archived parse re-read at review time, hash-checked by the archive); a Tier A source document; explicit language (the Investigator's cue list plus a **hedge list**: "may" (lower case only; "May" is a month), "might/could/would", "potential(ly)", "expects/plans/intends/anticipates/aims/seeks/hopes to", "in discussions/negotiations/talks", "non-binding", MOU/LOI, "reported(ly)", rumours, "allegedly", speculation); and the Reviewer's answer: verdict `confirmed`, direction `as_proposed`, layer `correct`. Patterns can't tell who "we" is, so they never judge the direction; that part of "explicitly directional language" is the Reviewer's classification. Anything else is `needs_human_review` (the exceptions queue) with reason codes.
- **The Reviewer is asked only about Assertions that pass the deterministic checks** (span, Tier A, unhedged): the others go to a human whatever it would say, so they cost no tokens (`reviewer_status = skipped`). It is a separate role (`roles/reviewer.py`, prompt `reviewer.v1.md`) with its own strict schema, called `ATLAS_REVIEWER_ASSERTIONS_PER_CALL` (5) Assertions at a time. Each item's quote and ±600 characters of context go as quoted low-trust `retrieved_data` (`<item>:quote`, `<item>:context`), never in the request. The role runs on the configured role model (MiniMax-M3); the map's "the Skeptic model" is read as "a separate call with its own prompt", not a separate model deployment.
- **Failures.** A quarantined answer or an item the answer leaves out sends that edge to humans (`reviewer_quarantined`, `reviewer_no_answer`). An LLM quota or outage pauses the queue (the kind is pausable); the retry keeps the job's run (`relationship_review_job`) and reviews only what is left. A spent token budget stops the job `budget_exhausted` and leaves the rest unreviewed for a later job (they are not sent to humans for a reason that isn't theirs).
- **Edge state.** A new edge takes its first review's outcome. Later Evidence is added (`relationship.evidence_added`); a passing witness lifts an exception to `machine_reviewed` (`relationship.machine_reviewed`), but a failing one never demotes a machine-reviewed edge (its reasons stay visible in `review_reasons`). **Machine review never changes an owner's decision.** The owner may approve or reject any edge in any state (`POST /relationships/{id}/review`, `{review_state: approved|rejected, note?}`); repeating the current decision is 409 `invalid_transition`. Every change is audited (`docs/data-model.md` §3.1e). Owner approval is what ticket 20's publish gate will require of the Relationships a Hypothesis depends on.
- **Counts.** `evidence_count` counts supporting Assertions not rejected or superseded; `family_count` the distinct Evidence Families of their Source Versions (a version in no family counts as its own), i.e. independent witnesses.
- **Not automatic after extraction.** `extract_claims` doesn't enqueue a review: an investigation's final stop chains one for its Assertions (ticket 16; in its own run, see below); by hand, `atlas jobs enqueue review_relationships --key K --payload '{}'`.
## 2026-09-29: Investigation orchestration (Phase 3-6a ticket 14)

- **The plan is fixed and visible from the start.** Round 1 is `scout` → one `investigator:<slug>` per seed company → `skeptic` ∥ `financial_analyst` → `editor`, stored as `investigation_task` rows (spec "task rows per role") with their dependencies and premises. The Skeptic and Financial Analyst are **skipped slots** (tickets 15 and 19 fill them); the Editor depends on them, and a skipped dependency counts as done. One Investigator task per seed company (not one for all) so that a company's premise can be disproven on its own. **Changed since** ("Multi-hop Investigators: the plan grows after the Scout", 2026-10-01): once a round's Scout has succeeded, the plan gains an Investigator for each other researched company its reading pointers name, while the company budget has room.
- **Tasks run as `investigation_task` jobs** (pausable) on the existing queue, not as `discover`/`extract_claims` jobs: each task's job runs the role's existing service in-process (`Scout.discover(..., run_id=)`, `ClaimExtractor.extract(job, payload)`) within the investigation's run, and in the transaction that records the outcome enqueues the next ready tasks (`JobQueue.enqueue_within`). So the DAG advances without a scheduler and a crash never leaves a task marked queued without its job. The discovery and extraction records are still created (keyed by the task's job) and readable at their own endpoints. `discover` jobs also accept a `run_id` now.
- **One run per investigation**, started by the first task (the API doesn't call LiteLLM or Hindsight). The per-run token ceiling is the investigation's `token_budget` (default `ATLAS_RUN_TOKEN_BUDGET`), enforced by the role caller before each call; the `atlas` key's `maxBudget` backstops it (a `budget_exceeded` answer is a quota failure, so it pauses).
- **Budgets.** Leads: the discovery's leads in the order found (query position, then result position), up to `max_leads` (≤ 10); the rest are counted as dropped (`lead_budget_reached`). Queries stay bounded by `ATLAS_DISCOVERY_MAX_QUERIES`. Documents: each Investigator task takes its company's **latest parsed version of each Source Document available at `as_of`**, newest first, up to what is left of `max_documents` (≤ 25, shared by all tasks), split fairly across a round's Investigator tasks since pilot fix 01 ("The document budget split and the card with no finding", below). "Fetched documents" are read here from the archive: an investigation doesn't fetch leads (leads are never Evidence; ticket 09 turns them into Candidates). Rounds: `max_rounds` ≤ 2 is recorded and enforced by a check constraint; only round 1 exists until the follow-up (ticket 17).
- **Stops**, each with its reason in the investigation and a `stopped` event: the Editor's verdict (`answered` only when the Editor says so, at least one finding cites accepted Claims, and no finding was dropped as unsupported; else `needs_review`); `no_new_independent_evidence` when the round's accepted Claims bring no Evidence Family that an earlier round's didn't (in round 1: no accepted Claim), decided by code; since pilot fix 01 the Editor is still called when no Claim was accepted at all, and writes a card with no finding (below), but the stop reason is the same; `premise_disproven` when the Editor was cancelled because its premises or all its inputs were; `needs_review` when a task fails on its job's last attempt; `budget_exhausted` when a role call is refused for the token budget.
- **A budget stop is resumable; other stops are final.** A final stop cancels the tasks that hadn't started and finishes the run with its token totals (`run.tokens_in/out`). A `budget_exhausted` stop leaves the run open and the unfinished tasks as they were: `POST /investigations/{id}/resume` with a larger `token_budget` re-queues them (a new generation, so a new job) in the same run. A resumed Investigator task **continues** its budget-exhausted extraction (`claim_extraction.continues_id`): only the passages from the next batch on are sent, so nothing is proposed twice. Until resumed, a budget-stopped investigation's tokens are shown from its role calls, not the run row.
- **An LLM quota or outage is not a stop.** The role caller raises `TransientFailure`; the task records `task_paused` and the worker pauses the queue and requeues its job without using an attempt; `GET` shows `paused: true`. When the pause lifts, the task resumes (`task_resumed`) where it was (the extraction at its next batch, the discovery at its searches).
- **Premises and cancellation.** Every task depends on the `question` premise; each Investigator task also on `company:<slug>`. `POST /investigations/{id}/premises/{key}/disprove` (audited) cancels only the **unstarted** tasks that name the premise, then those whose every non-skipped dependency was cancelled; a running task finishes, and its findings stay in the record. Accepted Claims from a disproven company's documents are left out of the Editor's input and the disproven premises are listed on the card. Who disproves a premise besides the researcher (the Skeptic) is ticket 15.
- **The Editor adds no facts.** It is sent the accepted Claims (resolved subject, predicate, object, source; each quote as quoted low-trust data) and the leads (snippets as quoted data, never evidence). A finding must cite at least one accepted Claim of the investigation and nothing else; otherwise it is kept only as an `unsupported_finding` and the stop can't be `answered`. Each kept finding is shaped as the §7.2 claim: `claim_text` is the Editor's; `epistemic_type` is `agent_inference` (with the cited Claims' types listed); spans, Source Versions, Evidence Families (a family ID, or a lone version's ID), entity IDs and `validity_dates.evidence_available_at` are filled in by code; `needs_review` stays true until every cited Assertion is corroborated; `counterevidence_ids` is empty until the Skeptic. The card is always a draft; Hypotheses are ticket 16.
- **Metrics:** `atlas_investigation_stops_total{reason}` from the insert-only event log (a resumed budget stop stays counted). Investigation tokens appear in `atlas_llm_tokens_total{kind="investigation"}` once the run finishes.
## 2026-09-29: Hypotheses (Phase 3-6a ticket 16)

- **Saving an investigation is a Hypothesis draft by the Editor.** `POST /api/v1/hypotheses {investigation_id}` (202) needs a stopped investigation with a research card with at least one finding (a `premise_disproven` stop has no card, and a `no_new_independent_evidence` card has no finding: 409), one Hypothesis per investigation (409 names the existing one). It records the Hypothesis (`draft`, author = the actor, theme, related companies = the seed companies whose premise wasn't disproven) and, in the same transaction, a `draft_hypothesis` job (pausable). The API calls no LLM. The job is the **same Editor role** (calls recorded as `editor`) with its own prompt `editor-hypothesis.v1.md`, in its own run (kind `hypothesis_draft`, kept across retries on `hypothesis.draft_run_id`; the investigation's run is finished by then) under `ATLAS_RUN_TOKEN_BUDGET`. It is sent the question, the research card's findings and open questions, the investigation's accepted Claims (the card's set: none from a task whose premise was disproven) with each quote as low-trust `retrieved_data`, and the disproven premises; it proposes the §5.6/§8.1 fields (thesis statement, mechanism as §8.1's demand driver / possible constraint / economic capture question, measurable predictions, catalysts, falsifiers, required Evidence, alternative explanations) plus **unresolved questions** and **findings**.
- **Only findings state facts, and code decides what they may cite** (`atlas.hypotheses.findings`, the research card's rule): a finding must cite at least one accepted Claim of the investigation and nothing else, and its spans, Source Versions, Assertions, Evidence Families and validity dates are filled in by code (the §7.2 claim shape). Anything else is kept only as an `unsupported_finding`: **no unsupported claim is promoted.** A researcher's correction whose findings cite anything else is refused (422 `unsupported_finding`) rather than recorded. The other fields are proposals, not findings, and cite nothing.
- **Versions never change** (`hypothesis_version`): the content is a JSON object with its SHA-256 (`audit.content_hash`) beside it and its provenance (investigation, investigation run and research-card Editor call, draft run and Editor call). A trigger (`ENABLE ALWAYS`) refuses every update except the one publication stamp on an unpublished version, and every delete, so a published version is immutable in the database, not just in code. Unpublished versions don't change either: **every edit is a new version** (`POST /hypotheses/{id}/versions`, based on the latest, with a required note; audited `hypothesis.corrected` when the base is published, `hypothesis.version_created` otherwise). This is simpler than mutable drafts and makes every version diffable.
- **The §5.6 lifecycle** (`TRANSITIONS`): `draft → researching → evidence_ready`, `needs_more_evidence ↔ researching`, `evidence_ready → (publish) → reviewed → paper_tracking → closed`, `rejected` from anything before `paper_tracking`, `reviewed → needs_more_evidence | closed`; `closed` and `rejected` are final. `POST /hypotheses/{id}/transitions` (409 `invalid_transition` otherwise); `evidence_ready` needs a drafted version. **`reviewed` is reached only by publishing**: `POST /hypotheses/{id}/publish-version {version}` publishes the latest (unpublished) version from `evidence_ready` (→ `reviewed`); a correction's publication while `reviewed` or `paper_tracking` leaves the status. Every change is an insert-only `hypothesis_transition` row and an audit event (`hypothesis.transitioned`, `hypothesis.version_published` with the content hash).
- **The publish gate is pluggable** (`Hypotheses(gate=..., hooks=...)`): a check reads what it needs in the publishing transaction and returns a `GateFailure(code, message)` or None; all failures are reported (422 `publish_gate_failed`, the codes in the message). Today's gate: `no_falsifier`, `no_unresolved_question`; ticket 20 added the Relationship checks and the Research Snapshot hook (below).
- **The version diff** (`GET /hypotheses/{id}/diff?from_version=&to_version=`, default the latest against the one before): a claim is a finding's statement (casefolded, whitespace collapsed) with the Assertions it cites. `new` (only later), `unchanged` (both), `contradicted` (in the earlier version, and the later one lists new counterevidence for it, or it was dropped and one of its Assertions is now disputed, rejected or superseded), and **`removed`** (dropped with no contradiction). `removed` is a fourth class beyond the spec's three: a claim a researcher drops isn't contradicted, and calling it so would misreport. Contradiction reads the Assertions' review state when the diff is read, not when the version was written (ticket 21's proposed-update flag will record contradictions as they happen). `fields_changed` names the other fields that differ.
- **The dossier export** (`GET /hypotheses/{id}/export?format=json|markdown&version=`): format `atlas.hypothesis-dossier` v1. Citations are numbered in order of first use: the Claim's exact quote and span, its Assertion (review state at the version and now), the Source Version and Document (title, publisher, canonical URL, form, accession, tier, `available_at`). Run metadata: the investigation (question, as-of, stop, budgets, run), each run behind the version (code, Hindsight and template versions, routed models, tokens) and each Editor call (prompt name, version, SHA-256, model, status). Markdown renders the same content; it includes the export time, so two exports differ in that line.
- **Relationship review is chained by the investigation** (ticket 12 left it unchained): an investigation's **final** stop enqueues `review_relationships` for the Assertions of the run's accepted Claims on its documents (≤ 100 a job, keyed `investigation:<id>:<n>`), in the stop's transaction, with a `relationship_review_queued` event before `stopped`. A `budget_exhausted` stop doesn't (the run isn't over; the resumed stop does). The review runs in **its own** `relationship_review` run, not the investigation's: the stop finishes the investigation's run in the same transaction, and a review job can call only within an unfinished run. So the review's tokens aren't counted against the investigation's token budget (the `atlas` key's `maxBudget` still backstops them). Assertions from a disproven company's documents are reviewed too: the edge's truth doesn't depend on the question.

## 2026-09-30: The follow-up round and the research workbench (Phase 3-6a ticket 17)

- **One follow-up round, on an open question of the research card.** `POST /api/v1/investigations/{id}/follow-up {question}` takes only a question the card lists (its own open questions or a finding's; 422 `unknown_open_question` otherwise): the researcher pushes on an uncertainty the investigation surfaced, not a new question (that is a new investigation). It is refused (409) while the investigation runs (`follow_up_not_allowed`), after a `budget_exhausted` stop (resume it instead) or a `premise_disproven` stop, when `max_rounds` is reached (`round_budget_spent`; `max_rounds` ≤ 2, so one follow-up at most) or the run's tokens are spent, and once the investigation is saved as a Hypothesis (`hypothesis_saved`: the Hypothesis is drawn from the card, which the round could replace).
- **The round is the fixed plan again, as round 2, in the same run and budgets.** New `investigation_task` rows for round 2 (same DAG, same premises: a premise disproven in round 1 cancels its round-2 tasks at once). The investigation runs again and its **run is reopened** (`finished_at` cleared; the next stop finishes it with the new totals), so the round spends what is left of the token, lead and document budgets rather than getting fresh ones. The Scout searches for the open question and the Investigators use it for recall; the Skeptic and Editor keep the investigation's question (the card still answers it).
- **A follow-up reads only what the investigation hasn't.** An Investigator task (in any round) takes only Source Versions not yet in `investigation_document`, so round 2 finds new independent Evidence only when new sources exist (e.g. imported or ingested since round 1). The Editor's existing rule decides: no new Evidence Family in the round's accepted Claims means `no_new_independent_evidence`, without an Editor call, and round 1's card stands.
- **Recorded, insert-only:** `investigation_follow_up` (migration `0034`) holds the round, the question, who asked and when, and **the card before the round** (`card_before`), so a redrafted card never hides what the earlier one said; plus the `follow_up_started` event and the `investigation.follow_up_started` audit event. A final stop's relationship review takes only its round's Assertions (round 2's jobs are keyed `investigation:<id>:round-2:<n>`; round 1's keys are unchanged).
- **The workbench reads the investigation, not the claims API.** `GET /investigations/{id}` gained `evidence` (the Evidence tray: each accepted Claim with its subject and object names, its Assertion and source span, the source's title and availability, its Evidence Family, round and task, and `excluded` when its task's premise was disproven), `follow_ups`, and `request.hypothesis_id` filled from the saved Hypothesis. `GET /investigations` lists them newest first. The page (`/investigation/?id=`) polls every 2 s while the investigation runs; the Financial Analyst slot, like every task, shows its status, detail and whatever its artifacts record, so ticket 19's output needs no workbench change.

## 2026-09-29: HKEXnews and the fetch gate (Phase 3-6a ticket 04)

- **Atlas does not fetch HKEXnews (lead's decision, 2026-09-29).** The HKEX Terms of Use (<https://www.hkex.com.hk/Global/Exchange/Terms-of-Use?sc_lang=en>, updated 19 Aug 2025, covering hkexnews.hk) say: "You are not permitted to conduct, facilitate, enable, authorise or permit any text or data mining or web scraping in relation to this Website … for any purpose", and forbid "any 'robot', 'bot', 'spider', 'scraper' or other automated device, program, tool, algorithm, code, process or methodology to access, obtain, copy, monitor or republish any portion of the Website" and "systematic retrieval to create collections, compilations, databases or directories". hkexnews.hk serves no robots.txt (404). The official automated route is HKEX's licensed **Issuer Information feed Service (IIS)**; HKEX's prior written consent (info@hkex.com.hk) also lifts a restriction. Neither is held, so the site register records HKEXnews as `automation: forbidden` with the block reason "blocked: HKEX Terms of Use prohibit automated access (IIS licence or written consent required)": an `exchange:hkex` ingest records a `blocked` gate decision with that reason for the title search and requests nothing. **Innolight's HKEXnews documents come in by manual import** (below). The HKEXnews adapter is kept, fixture-tested, for the day a licence or consent is recorded (`consent: {reference, granted_on, scope}` in `configs/sources/sites.yaml`). Tickets 05/06 use the same path for the FCA National Storage Mechanism (IQE) and the AMF info-financière open API (Soitec).
- **Manual import** (`atlas sources import --company <slug> --file <path> --origin-url <URL> --published-at <ISO time with offset> [--title] [--publisher] [--media-type]`; `atlas.ledger.manual_import`): a document the owner downloaded by hand is recorded through the same ledger write as a fetch: a Source Document of provider `manual_import` (`source_type = manual_import`, tier A, `public_regulatory`) identified by its origin URL, publisher from the site register (else the host); a Source Version with `available_at` = `published_at` = the given time, basis `publisher_timestamp`; parsed (PDF via ticket 03), given its Evidence Family, retained when English, and audited under `ATLAS_ACTOR`. The same bytes again are `unchanged` (no new version, no retain); changed bytes at the same origin URL are a superseding version available from the import time (`observed_revision`). The time must carry its offset (HKEXnews prints Hong Kong time); a naive one is refused. No request is made, so its observation has no gate decision. Whether keeping hand-downloaded HKEXnews documents in Atlas's archive fits HKEX's terms (they permit download and temporary storage for personal use) is the owner's call; the import records who imported each one.
- **The fetch gate** (`atlas.sources.gate`) decides before each request of an exchange adapter, in order: (1) the **register**: the host must belong to a site in `configs/sources/sites.yaml` (`ATLAS_SOURCE_SITES_CONFIG`), else `blocked_by = register`; (2) the **terms**: `allowed` passes, `forbidden` passes only with a recorded consent, `unchecked` is blocked (`blocked_by = terms`); a site blocked here gets no request at all, not even for robots.txt; (3) **robots.txt**, read once per origin per ingest and obeyed. Every decision, allowed or blocked, is a `fetch_gate_decision` row (audited `fetch_gate.allowed|blocked`), and each fetch observation names the allowed decision it was made under (a trigger refuses a blocked one). Blocked decisions are listed at `GET /api/v1/companies/{id}/fetch-gate-decisions?status=blocked`; the ingest job still succeeds and lists them under `blocked`, because a policy block isn't an error and retrying can't help. SEC EDGAR fetches aren't gated (NULL `gate_decision_id`): its fair-access policy explicitly permits declared automated access.
- **robots.txt is RFC 9309, self-implemented** (`atlas.sources.robots`), not `urllib.robotparser`, which applies the first matching rule rather than the longest and treats 401/403 as disallow-all. Atlas's product token is `AtlasResearch`: it obeys the groups naming it (merged), else the `*` groups; the longest matching path wins and `allow` wins a tie; `*` and a final `$` are wildcards; percent-encoding is normalized; `/robots.txt` is always allowed. A 4xx means no restrictions, a 5xx or network failure means disallow-all, and an HTML page served as robots.txt has no directives. The body is archived and its hash, status, the obeyed group and the deciding rule (with its line) are recorded on the decision. A redirect to another host is followed by the HTTP client without a second gate check; no pilot source redirects.
- **Innolight's own IR site is never fetched.** `ir.zj-innolight.com` (robots.txt `Disallow: /`) and `www.zj-innolight.com` (`Disallow: /uploads/`) are in the register as `unchecked`, so the gate blocks them without a request; nothing in the ingest points at them anyway.
- **One exchange ingest path.** The `ingest` job dispatches on the company's `source_path`: `sec` runs the SEC ingest; `exchange:<name>` runs `atlas.ledger.exchange_ingest` if `EXCHANGE_SOURCES` has that exchange (today `exchange:hkex`), else it is refused before seeding or fetching (CLI exit 2; a job fails). An `ExchangeSource` is the register site of the feed, its provider ID and a factory building the adapter over the gated client from the company's config (`exchange: {issuer_code, feed_id}` in the theme config). Tickets 05/06 add an adapter and one entry; the gate, the ledger identity (`SourceCandidate.announcement`: publisher, feed ID, issuer code, category, publication time as printed and its zone) and the `publisher_timestamp` availability are shared. Exchange documents are Source Documents with `source_type = exchange_announcement`, the feed's category as `document_type`, tier A, `public_regulatory`; `published_at` is the feed's time too. Live requests need `ATLAS_EXCHANGE_LIVE=true` and `ATLAS_EXCHANGE_USER_AGENT` (a name and contact); otherwise the adapter replays `ATLAS_EXCHANGE_FIXTURES_DIR/<company>/`. The gate applies to replays as to live requests.
- **HKEXnews adapter** (`atlas.sources.hkexnews`): discovery is the title search `GET /search/titleSearchServlet.do?…&stockId=<HKEXnews stockId>&fromDate&toDate&rowRange&lang=E` (Innolight's stockId 1000311764, from the seed-list research). Its JSON's `result` is the rows serialized into a string; each row's `DATE_TIME` (`DD/MM/YYYY HH:MM`, Hong Kong time, minute precision) is `available_at` (basis `publisher_timestamp`), converted to UTC; `FILE_LINK` is the document. PDF and HTML rows only. It asks for 100 rows and, while `hasNextRow`, 100 more, up to 500; beyond that discovery fails rather than silently dropping announcements. The adapter declares no language: the English search also lists Chinese-only documents (overseas regulatory announcements), so the parse decides and only English is retained. The response shape comes from public descriptions (the seed-list research; <https://github.com/carrotly-ai/disclosures/blob/main/HKSG-FEASIBILITY.md>), not from a request to HKEXnews; that `DATE_TIME` is Hong Kong time is HKEXnews' convention, unverified against a live response.
- **Fixtures are hand-written**, not recorded (`scripts/make_hkexnews_fixtures.py` writes `tests/fixtures/hkexnews/innolight/`): the documented search shape with three rows, and synthetic PDFs that say so. `FixtureReplay` gained `ignore_query` for a search whose query carries today's date. The integration tests use a copy of the register with a **synthetic** HKEXnews consent to exercise the allowed path, and the committed register to show the block.

## 2026-09-29: Claim quotes are located, not trusted

- **Why (measured).** Ticket 28's live smoke run (2026-09-29T18:25Z, MiniMax-M3 via LiteLLM, 4 Investigator calls, 18,979 tokens in / 918 out) over the recorded Coherent FY2026 10-K, Lumentum's 10-K Item 1 and an EX-99.1: **3 Claims proposed, 3 rejected `quote_mismatch`, 0 accepted**. Of the 3 quotes, **2 occur verbatim exactly once in their passage** (at other offsets than the model gave) and would have passed every later check once placed; **1 was a paraphrase**, in no passage. The Lumentum 10-K Item 1 and the EX-99.1 produced no Claims. A small sample, but offsets alone rejected every true quote, well past ticket 28's ~20% threshold. Owner decision, 2026-09-29.
- **The rule.** The model's offsets are a hint; the quote is the evidence.
  1. If the quote is exactly the passage text at the model's offsets, it is placed there (`claim.offset_source = model`).
  2. Otherwise the passage is searched for the quote as an **exact** substring: no folding of whitespace, case, quote marks or dashes. **Exactly one** occurrence: the Claim takes that span (`offset_source = located`) and every later check runs unchanged. **None**: `quote_mismatch`, as before (a paraphrase is never "found"). **More than one**: a new rejection, `quote_ambiguous` (the reason lists the occurrences); Atlas never picks one. *(From 2026-10-01 a quote with no exact occurrence is tried once more through a one-to-one fold of typographic hyphens, quotation marks and spaces, and the stored quote is the archived text at the span: see "Claim checks" below. Whitespace runs, case and words are still never folded.)*
  3. The **Assertion span check** (`check_quote`) still runs on the final span, so an accepted Claim's Assertion is always an exact span of the archived parsed text.
- **Recorded.** `claim.offset_source` (migration 0024; null when the quote was never placed, and for Claims recorded before 0024), shown by `GET /api/v1/claims[/{id}]`. The model's own offsets stay in `claim.proposed`, so how often it counts right stays measurable (`offset_source` counts `model` vs `located`).
- **Scope.** Only within the one passage the Claim names: a quote is never moved to another passage or elsewhere in the document. The earlier out-of-passage check (`quote_outside_passage`) still runs first on the model's offsets.
- **Not changed:** researcher-created Assertions (`POST /api/v1/assertions`) still need exact offsets; the viewer computes them from the selection.
## 2026-09-29: FCA National Storage Mechanism for IQE (Phase 3-6a ticket 05)

- **IQE comes from the FCA National Storage Mechanism (NSM), not LSE RNS** (ticket 05 re-scoped by the lead after the terms check). `source_path` `exchange:lse-rns` is replaced by `exchange:fca-nsm` (migration 0025 moves any company still on it and narrows the check constraint); IQE's config gives its LEI as `exchange.feed_id` (the NSM searches by LEI) and its TIDM `IQE` as `issuer_code`.
- **The NSM's terms allow it.** Terms of Use (<https://data.fca.org.uk/artefacts/NSM_Terms_of_Use.pdf>, updated 19 March 2020, read 2026-09-29): "You may use the material and documents contained in the NSM for any lawful purpose not otherwise excluded by these terms and conditions or the Acceptable Use Policy"; uploaders grant "other users of this site a limited licence to use, store and copy that content and to distribute and make it available to third parties including for any lawful commercial purpose"; the FCA's name, logo and marks may not be used "for any purpose". The Acceptable Use Policy (<https://data.fca.org.uk/artefacts/NSM_General_AUP.pdf>, 18 February 2020) adds "Not to reproduce, duplicate, copy or re-sell any part of our site in contravention of the provisions of our terms of website use" and "Not to access without authority, interfere with, damage or disrupt … any part of our site". Neither host serves a robots.txt (403 on `data.fca.org.uk` and `api.data.fca.org.uk`, which RFC 9309 reads as no restrictions); the gate still reads it on every ingest. The user guide (<https://www.fca.org.uk/publication/primary-market/nsm-investor-user-guide.pdf>, v10.0, June 2026) documents the search fields, that information is "generally … publicly available within an hour of it being submitted", corrections (latest version by default) and CSV export (≤ 4,000 rows, a download link per line). The site register records the NSM as `automation: allowed` with those clauses; **Atlas's conditions**: a low fixed rate (`rate_per_s: 0.5`, one request every 2 s, live only), no republishing of the site, no use of the FCA's marks.
- **LSE RNS and IQE's Brighter IR iframe are blocked.** `www.londonstockexchange.com` robots.txt: `User-agent: *` / `Disallow: /en-gb/`; its disclaimer (<https://www.londonstockexchange.com/disclaimer>) provides RNS "to the general public for personal use and to RNS customers in a professional capacity" and allows storage "on disk (but not on any server or other storage device connected to a network) for personal use". The register records `lse-rns` as `automation: forbidden` with the reason "blocked: LSE disclaimer limits RNS to personal use stored off any network, and robots.txt disallows /en-gb/ (an RNS data licence or written consent required)". `polaris.brighterir.com` (the regulatory-news iframe on IQE's site) serves `Disallow: /` and its terms are unchecked, so it is `unchecked`. The gate blocks both without a request (unit-tested with the committed register).
- **The search is a JSON POST**, so `SecHttpClient` gained `post_json` (retried like a GET; used only for the idempotent search) and the gated client `post_json`, gated on the URL like a GET. The NSM's search API isn't documented: the adapter sends the exact query the NSM's own search page sends (`POST https://api.data.fca.org.uk/search?index=nsm-search`, `company_lei` = `["", <LEI>, "disclose_org", "related_org"]`, `latest_flag` `Y`, `dateCriteria` up to now with no fractional seconds, `sort: submitted_date desc`, `from`/`size` 100), as described by <https://github.com/TomHyde10/RNS> (`nsm.js`) and confirmed by a handful of requests on 2026-09-29 (two robots.txt, three searches, one HEAD of a document). It narrows the answer itself rather than varying the query:
  - **Only the issuer's own disclosures:** the LEI search also returns other organisations' documents naming IQE as related issuer (of the 400 most recent hits, 82 were IQE's; the rest mostly holders' Form 8.3s during the 2026 offer period). A row whose `lei` isn't IQE's is skipped.
  - **`available_at`** is `publication_date` (the PIP's release time, or the time the submitter says it was published), else `submitted_date` (NSM receipt), basis `publisher_timestamp`, also stored as `published_at`; with neither, the discovery time (`observed_discovery`, `published_at` null: `ExchangeAnnouncement.published_at`/`published_local` became optional for this). Times are UTC, sometimes with nanoseconds (truncated to microseconds).
  - HTML and PDF documents only (`https://data.fca.org.uk/artefacts/<download_link>`; RNS announcements are HTML, directly uploaded reports often PDF); pages of 100 newest first until a row submitted at or before `since` (default lookback 730 days) or the end; more than 2,000 rows fails discovery. Each document is a Source Document identified by its URL; a correction has its own `seq_id` and URL, so it is a separate document (only the latest version is listed).
- **Fixtures are hand-written** in the observed shapes (`scripts/make_fca_nsm_fixtures.py` → `tests/fixtures/fca-nsm/iqe/`); identifiers, times and documents are synthetic. IQE's real NSM entries (checked 2026-09-29) are all RNS HTML, including its FY2025 results; the fixture's PDF annual report uses the `NSM/Portal/NI-…/NI-….pdf` link shape seen for another issuer's direct upload. Whether the live search keeps accepting non-browser clients is unverified beyond those requests.
## 2026-09-29: AMF info-financière for Soitec (Phase 3-6a ticket 06)

- **Soitec comes from the AMF's info-financière open API, not Euronext or soitec.com** (ticket 06 re-scoped by the lead after the terms check). `source_path` `exchange:euronext` is replaced by `exchange:amf` (migration 0029 moves any company still on it and narrows the check constraint); Soitec's config gives its LEI as `exchange.feed_id` (the API is searched by it) and its ticker `SOI` as `issuer_code`.
- **The API is open data.** The data.gouv.fr dataservice "API info-financière" (<https://www.data.gouv.fr/dataservices/api-info-financiere>, read 2026-09-29; producer Premier ministre / DILA) gives the licence "Licence Ouverte / Open Licence 2.0" (Etalab), the base URL `https://www.info-financiere.gouv.fr/api/explore/v2.0` and "10000 appels API par ip par jour". The licence allows reuse, commercial included, if the source and the date of its last update are credited: every announcement Atlas stores carries `attribution` ("Source: Autorité des marchés financiers (AMF), info-financiere.gouv.fr (DILA), dataset flux-amf-new-prod, record <id>, updated <record timestamp>. Licence Ouverte / Open Licence 2.0 (Etalab), <licence URL>"), kept in the Source Version's metadata. The register records the site as `automation: allowed`, 0.5 requests/s live, and a discovery makes at most 10 API calls (1,000 rows), so an ingest spends a few of the day's calls. The API answered `X-RateLimit-Limit: 5000` (per day) and deprecates v2.0 in favour of v2.1 (`ods-explore-api-deprecation` header); Atlas uses v2.0 as data.gouv.fr documents it.
- **robots.txt and the API client (lead's decision).** `www.info-financiere.gouv.fr/robots.txt` has `User-agent: *` … `Disallow: /api/` (and allows `/api/` to Googlebot); the files the API's records link to (`url_de_recuperation`) are on Opendatasoft's file host, whose robots.txt is `User-agent: *` / `Disallow: /`. robots.txt addresses crawlers; Atlas is a client of an API published for programs and never crawls the site's HTML, RSS or Atom. So the site register gained `api_client` (`url_prefixes`, `documentation_url`, `note`; only for `automation: allowed`, prefixes on the site's own hosts): the gate still reads, archives and records robots.txt for every request, and for a URL under one of those prefixes a robots disallow is recorded in the reason ("… documented API client, not a crawler (<documentation URL>): robots.txt Disallow: /api/ (line 13) disallows it") instead of blocking. The prefixes are exactly the `flux-amf-new-prod` records and `https://fr.ftp.opendatasoft.com/datadila/INFOFI/`; the site's pages and feeds, other datasets, exports and the rest of the file host stay blocked by robots.txt (unit-tested with the committed register). Applying the API-client reasoning to the file host is my extension of the lead's decision, since the documents are only reachable there.
- **The adapter** (`atlas.sources.amf`): `GET …/catalog/datasets/flux-amf-new-prod/records?where=identificationsociete_iso_cd_lei="<LEI>"&order_by=informationdeposee_inf_dat_emt desc&limit=100&offset=…`, newest first until a row transmitted at or before `since` (default lookback 730 days) or the end; more than 1,000 rows fails discovery. Only PDF and HTML files (`url_de_recuperation`; an ESEF `.zip` is skipped) of rows whose LEI is the issuer's. Rows the AMF itself publishes about the issuer (threshold-crossing decisions, diffuser `307`) are kept: they are regulated information filed under Soitec's LEI, and French.
  - **`available_at`** is the later of `uin_dat_amf` (sent to the AMF) and `informationdeposee_inf_dat_emt` (transmitted), which normally agree: basis `publisher_timestamp`, also stored as `published_at`; with neither, the discovery time (`observed_discovery`). The API prints them with `+00:00`.
  - **Language:** `informationdeposee_inf_lng_inf` "Anglais" → `en`, "Français" → `fr`, declared on the candidate (so it overrides the parse); anything else is left to the parse. Soitec files most releases twice, one row per language: the English file is retained, the French one archived with `language=fr` and never retained (ticket 03's rule).
  - `announcement_id` is `uin_idt_uin`; `issuer_code` the LEI; `category` `type_d_information`; `document_type` the English `subtype_of_information`, else the French `sous_type_d_information` (the API writes the string `NULL` for none).
- **Euronext and soitec.com are blocked** (`automation: forbidden`). Euronext's Terms of Use (<https://www.euronext.com/en/terms-use>, updated 29 April 2021): "Except if we give you prior written permission, use of any Web browsers (other than generally available third-party browsers), engines, software, spiders, robots, avatars, agents, tools or other devices or mechanisms to navigate, search or determine the Euronext Website is strictly prohibited", and no copying or compiling "through framing or systematic retrieval to create collections, compilations, databases or directories". (Its robots.txt, read 2026-09-29, disallows only site internals; the block rests on the terms.) Soitec's terms of use (<https://www.soitec.com/home/terms-of-use>, quoted from its public text via web search): "In no event may you use, distribute, copy, reproduce, modify, distort, or transmit the Web site or any of its elements … without the prior written permission of Soitec." The gate blocks both without a request.
- **Fixtures are hand-written** in the documented shapes (`scripts/make_amf_fixtures.py` → `tests/fixtures/amf/soitec/`); identifiers, times and documents are synthetic, the two robots.txt bodies are as served. The live requests made to confirm the shapes are listed in the implementation log.

## 2026-09-29: Candidates (Phase 3-6a ticket 09)

- **Mentions come from a small role call, not regexes.** The mention extractor (`atlas/roles/mentions.py`, prompt `roles/prompts/mention_extractor.v1.md`) reads leads' titles and snippets as quoted low-trust `retrieved_data` (ids `lead-1`…, `source` the canonical URL, text `title\n\nsnippet`), 10 leads per call, and answers `{leads: [{lead, companies: [{name, ticker, exchange}]}]}` (strict schema; ticker and exchange null unless the text gives them). Answers for ids it wasn't sent are ignored; a lead it omits named nothing. It never resolves or judges a company.
- **Resolution is deterministic.** Code maps the exchange name to a MIC from a fixed table (`exchange_mic`: Nasdaq → XNAS, NYSE → XNYS, HKEX → XHKG, …; an unknown exchange is dropped, leaving a ticker without a venue), then `resolve_mention` (the universe first, then SEC/GLEIF/OpenFIGI) with every configured company's ignored CIKs. A universe company is `in_universe`; `unresolved` (nothing identified it) proposes nothing; any other tier (exact, corroborated, candidate, conflict) is an unseeded match and proposes a Candidate. Resolutions are cached per job by (normalized name, ticker, MIC).
- **A Candidate is a (theme, company) pair** (CONTEXT.md), keyed by `cik:` when the resolution has an entity with a CIK, else `lei:`, else `name:` + the normalized name (a conflict, several matches, or a listing with no issuer record). A later lead naming the same company in the same theme joins it (`lead_mention`), whatever the Candidate's state; it is never re-proposed. The resolution is stored whole. A CIK-keyed Candidate doesn't carry the LEI the resolver linked by name: that CIK↔LEI link stays the owner's (ticket 02). Country: GLEIF's jurisdiction, else `US` for an SEC US state of incorporation, else none.
- **When:** each `discover` job enqueues `propose_candidates` (key `discovery:<id>`, same job class) when `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT` is set, since resolution calls SEC; otherwise nothing is enqueued (and `atlas jobs enqueue propose_candidates` refuses at run time with that reason). An investigation's Scout (ticket 14) calls the discovery directly and so enqueues no proposal; its leads can be proposed by hand. A lead is examined once (`lead_examination`), so a retried or repeated job reads only new leads. The role calls are one run of kind `candidates`, finished when the attempt ends. Pausable (LiteLLM); an identity-source failure is an ordinary failed attempt.
- **Nothing commits automatically.** The owner's commit (only from `lead`) creates the `company` row with the ID a config entry would get (UUIDv5 of the CIK, else the slug; `atlas.companies.company_id_from`), adds `universe_company` (theme, company, Candidate), and moves the Candidate to `investigating`, all audited in one transaction. The slug defaults to the name's lowercase words joined by `-`; the owner may set slug, display name, country (required when resolution found none: 409 `country_required`), layer, and for a name-only Candidate one of the CIKs its resolution offered (409 `cik_not_offered` otherwise). 409 `already_in_universe` when the CIK is a company's already, `slug_taken` when the slug is.
- **The universe's database extension:** `extend_universe(connection, universe)` adds each `universe_company` company that has a source path to the config's companies (config wins on a slug) and to its theme's members. The `ingest` job (for a slug the config lacks), `atlas ingest` and retention (theme tags) use it; other readers of the theme config (the research scope, discovery themes) are unchanged, since a commit adds no theme.
- **Source path at commit:** a resolved CIK makes the company `sec` and enqueues its `ingest` (key `candidate-commit:<id>`, looking back `ATLAS_INGEST_LOOKBACK_DAYS` like `atlas ingest`) in the commit's transaction. Without a CIK the company has no source path and the Candidate's `ingest_note` says no automated source exists yet: exchange feeds need a config entry (issuer code, feed ID) and a register decision, so a committed non-SEC company is added to the config by hand when its source is ready.
- **Rejected Candidates are kept** with their reason (`rejected`, CHECK: reason set exactly then). Candidates are never deleted: `candidate_no_delete`/`candidate_no_truncate` triggers, and the runtime role has no DELETE. Commit and reject apply only to a `lead`; the other §8.3 transitions (evidence_ready, needs_more_evidence, paper_tracking, closed, and rejecting a committed one) are later tickets' (the research workflow).
- **Metric:** `atlas_candidates{state}`, a gauge over all seven states.
## 2026-09-29: Quota-window pacing and the staged rollout (Phase 3-6a ticket 27)

- **Two providers, two units.** Job kinds are classified by whose subscription they spend (`atlas.jobs.budget.PROVIDER_KINDS`): the Hindsight-backed kinds (`retain`, `poll_operation`, `reprocess`, `refresh_mental_model`, `reflect`) are `codex`; the LiteLLM role kinds (`discover`, `extract_claims`, `review_relationships`, `investigation_task`) are `minimax`. `ingest` and `noop` spend neither. Codex is counted in **Hindsight operations submitted** (retain and reprocess batches, 1 each; `hindsight_operation` rows), because Atlas can't see the shared server's Codex tokens. MiniMax is counted in **recorded tokens** (`llm_call.tokens_in + tokens_out`), what the per-run budget already counts.
- **Rolling window, pacing clock.** Each unit counts from when the queue first sees its row until one window later (`ATLAS_BUDGET_WINDOW_HOURS`, default 5). Every claim first records unseen rows in `provider_usage` at the pacing clock's now (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so concurrent workers count each once), then sums each provider's window. Recording at the pacing clock rather than the rows' own database timestamps keeps the budget on the same injectable clock as the pause and the window. Migration 0028 records existing rows at their own times, so history isn't counted as spent on deploy. The queue view counts rows not yet seen as spent now, without writing.
- **Holding rule.** A job of a budgeted kind is claimed only while the window's usage is **below the limit for its class**: the budget for `interactive`, `floor(budget × (1 − ATLAS_BUDGET_INTERACTIVE_RESERVE))` for `backfill` (default reserve 0.3). The cost of the next job is unknown until it runs, so a job may overshoot by what it spends (one batch; at most `ATLAS_RUN_TOKEN_BUDGET` tokens for a role run). With several workers, each may overshoot once. `poll_operation` is never held: it spends nothing new and holding it would only delay observing a submitted batch (the same reason it's always interactive). Budgets apply whether or not a backfill window is set; the 429/outage pause stays the backstop for what the budget doesn't foresee.
- **Conservative defaults**, tuned by the owner: `ATLAS_CODEX_BUDGET_OPERATIONS=40` (backfill 28) and `ATLAS_MINIMAX_BUDGET_TOKENS=400000` (backfill 280,000) per 5 h. The 2026-09 bootstrap submitted 1,397 operations in minutes; 40 per window is about 3% of that. `docs/runbooks.md` ("Universe rollout and quota budgets") says how to read the budget view and when to raise them.
- **The backfill window is optional and may list ranges.** `ATLAS_BACKFILL_WINDOW` defaults to empty (any time) and accepts comma-separated `HH:MM-HH:MM` ranges (`01:00-07:00,13:00-15:00`). The next opening is the earliest range start after now.
- **Ingest plan.** A backfill SEC ingest of a company with no Source Version yet records an `ingest_plan` after discovery and before any fetch: every discovered document (after the lookback and 8-K selection), their count, and the estimated retain operations (one per `sec_filing_document`; companyfacts is normalized, not retained). One per job; audited. It isn't a gate: the ingest proceeds, bounded by the cap and the budget. Exchange ingests record no plan yet (Innolight is manual import; IQE and Soitec wait for tickets 05/06).
- **Retain cap.** `atlas ingest --max-retains N` (SEC filers only) retains at most N of the company's **not-yet-retained** versions (each document's latest version, retainable parse and language, no memory documents, no `retain` job), newest `available_at` first, ties by ingest order. The rest are listed as `retains_deferred` in the job's artifacts; the next capped ingest (a new `--key`) retains the next N, so a company's backfill can be rolled out over several windows without re-fetching.
- **Visibility.** `GET /api/v1/queue` adds `budgets` (per provider: unit, window, used, budget, backfill limit, whether each class is held, when each resumes, the kinds held) and `budget_held` per pending kind. `GET /api/v1/ingest-plans[?company=]` lists plans. Metrics: `atlas_budget_used{provider,unit}`, `atlas_budget_limit{provider,job_class}`, `atlas_budget_window_seconds`, `atlas_budget_usage_total{provider}` (`increase(...[5h])` gives usage per window) and `atlas_queue_jobs_held_by_budget{provider,kind,job_class}`.

## 2026-09-29: No personal data in the User-Agent of non-SEC sources

SEC's EDGAR access rules require a User-Agent with a contact email, so `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT` keeps one. Every other source (the exchange adapters, and any later non-SEC HTTP source) sends the generic `ATLAS_EXCHANGE_USER_AGENT` (default `AtlasResearch`). Settings validation refuses any value containing `@`, so an email address can't be configured there by mistake. This follows an incident during ticket 05: two NSM robots.txt requests carried the owner's email because the SEC contact string was reused. The owner delegated the choice ("You decide").

## 2026-09-30: Owner accepts the AMF API-client exception; TradingView findings

- **AMF (ticket 06):** the owner accepted option (a). Atlas uses the AMF info-financière dataset as a documented API client despite the hosts' crawler-oriented robots.txt: the dataset's API records and the `datadila/INFOFI/` document folder only, at 0.5 requests/s and at most 10 API calls per discovery. The robots verdict is recorded with every fetch. Everything else on those hosts stays blocked. The reasons: the data is under Licence Ouverte / Etalab 2.0, and the API is published for programmatic clients.
- **No further per-exchange adapters.** SEC EDGAR, the FCA NSM and the AMF cover the US, UK and France. Other markets go through manual import until a better route exists.
- **TradingView MCP, tested live 2026-09-30 in the owner's session:**
  - `get_documents` lists any listed company's filings, releases, slides and transcripts. Lumentum has 169; Innolight HKEX:3308 has 29, including English annual and interim reports. Provider: Quartr.
  - `get_document_view` returns full verbatim transcripts with speaker labels, but only AI summaries of filings; filing PDFs aren't retrievable.
  - `get_news` returns headlines with the original publisher and timestamps.
  - Consequence: TradingView is not a Tier A filing source, since Atlas has no original text to quote from. It is a filing and event catalog, a transcript source (candidate Tier B) and a news-lead source (Tier C). Whether Atlas may store any of it depends on the terms check (ticket 29).

## 2026-09-30: TradingView stays outside Atlas (terms check, ticket 29)

TradingView's Terms of Use (https://www.tradingview.com/policies/) license content and market data "for exclusive display-only use" and prohibit "any form of non-display usage", including "machine-driven processes that do not involve the direct, human-readable display of such data" and "any processing of TradingView's content". §25 "AI Features" allows only "personal, non-automated use". Quartr's standard redistribution terms forbid using its data "to build, populate, enrich or maintain any database archive". News belongs to the publishers ("Data Providers" include "news providers").

Decision:
- **Atlas is not a TradingView MCP client** and stores no TradingView output (transcripts, news text, summaries, fundamentals, prices), including into Hindsight.
- **The owner uses the MCP interactively only**, in their own assistant session, for reading and finding leads. For example: which filings exist, what a call said, which companies to look at.
- **Bringing something into Atlas means going to the primary source** under that source's own terms: SEC/NSM/AMF adapters, `atlas sources import` of documents the owner is entitled to keep, and SearXNG leads to the publisher's own URL.
- **Automation or storage would need TradingView's written consent** (a "separate agreement").
## 2026-09-29: Retention triage (Phase 3-6a ticket 30)

- **Read first, retain what adds durable value.** Owner direction: "everything seems like token bloat. We should be able to read and figure out its value?" With `ATLAS_RETENTION_TRIAGE` on (`auto`, the default, means on when LiteLLM is configured), a new Source Version's `retain` job hands the version to a `triage` job before anything is submitted to Hindsight. Only sections decided `retain` get memory documents and are submitted (still one batch per version). A version with every section skipped submits nothing. `off` retains every section, as before; the test harness and the live Phase 2 gate pin `off`.
- **Order of decision, per undecided section:** (1) *inherited*: its text's SHA-256 equals a section of the version's predecessor (the most recent earlier triaged version of the same Source Document, else of the same company, form and document type, available no later). It copies that section's effective decision and points at it. (2) *rule* (`triage-rules-v1`, on Item headings only; a chunk's first line is not a heading): the headings forward-looking statements, signatures, exhibit index, `Item … (Financial Statements and) Exhibits`, mine safety, `[Reserved]` and Form 10-K summary, or a section whose body is only `None.`/`Not applicable.`, is `skip`/`boilerplate` with no call. (3) *role*: the Triage role (`atlas.roles.triage`; its prompt `triage.v1.md` is the rubric, version `triage.v1`) reads each section's heading and first `ATLAS_TRIAGE_EXCERPT_CHARS` (1,500) characters, quoted as low-trust data, plus the document's metadata and its company's themes. It decides `ATLAS_TRIAGE_SECTIONS_PER_CALL` (15) sections per call. Inheritance comes before the rules, so an on-demand retain of a boilerplate section carries forward.
- **Fail open.** A section the role's answer leaves out, or whose call was quarantined or hit the run's token budget, is retained (`default`, category `unclassified`). Triage never loses evidence by failing. A quota or outage failure pauses the queue like any role call. Decisions are inserted as they are made, so the resumed job decides only what's left.
- **Insert-only record.** `triage_decision` (migration 0030) holds each decision with the section's offsets, sectioner version, content hash, decision, value category, one-line reason, method, rubric and rules versions, and the role call, inherited decision, requester or investigation. It can't be updated or deleted. A section's effective decision is its latest row. Each row is audited (`triage_decision.<method>`).
- **Hold and retry, not fail open** (owner decision 2026-09-30, replacing ticket 30's fail-open rule). A failed triage retains nothing and records no decision by default; the version's retain waits until every section is decided. An LLM outage or quota failure requeues the job without using an attempt and pauses the queue, as for any role call. The triage run's own token budget spent (`TokenBudgetExhausted`) is not a provider failure, so it pauses nothing: the handler raises `Requeue` (`atlas.jobs.pacing`), and the worker puts the job back in the queue without using an attempt (failure classification `requeued`). Its next attempt starts a fresh run once the `minimax` rolling-window budget (ticket 27) has room. A quarantined answer, or one that leaves sections out, leaves those sections undecided: the job's other batches still run, then the attempt fails (`TriageIncomplete`, an ordinary failure) and the queue retries it, failing the job visibly after its `max_attempts` (3). If the run's budget runs out after such a section, the attempt fails the same way. A requeue there would ask the same batch again forever without using an attempt. `atlas triage retry --source-version <id>` or `--failed` re-enqueues a version whose latest triage job failed (same class and `max_attempts`; refused for any other version). The gauge `atlas_triage_failed_jobs` counts versions waiting on such a retry. Decisions are inserted as they are made, so a retried or resumed job asks only about what's left and never duplicates a decision. Skipped or undecided sections stay archived and citable, so evidence is not lost, only not yet in memory. The `default` method stays in the schema for rows recorded before this change; nothing records it now.
- **Insert-only record.** `triage_decision` (migration 0033) holds each decision with the section's offsets, sectioner version, content hash, decision, value category, one-line reason, method, rubric and rules versions, and the role call, inherited decision, requester or investigation. It can't be updated or deleted. A section's effective decision is its latest row. Each row is audited (`triage_decision.<method>`).
- **Retain on demand.** `POST /api/v1/source-versions/{id}/sections/{anchor}/retain` (`{reason, investigation_id?}`) records an `on_demand` `retain` decision, with the actor as `requested_by` and the investigation when one asked, and enqueues a `retain`. The next retain records and submits any `retain`-decided section without a memory document, with triage on or off. 409 `already_retained`, `linked` or `not_retainable`. The Investigator reads parsed text directly, so it never depends on a section being retained. `atlas.retention.request_retain` is the call an investigation makes.
- **Skipped sections stay evidence.** They remain archived, in the parsed text, viewable and citable by Assertions; only recall and reflect (memory) don't see them.
- **Budgets.** `triage` is a `minimax` kind (its role calls' tokens); it is enqueued by a `retain` job, a `codex` kind. So triage waits whenever retains would wait anyway.
- **Visibility.** `GET /api/v1/triage?source_version_id=&company_id=&decision=&category=&method=&effective=`; metrics `atlas_triage_sections{decision,category}` (effective decisions), `atlas_triage_decisions{method}` and `atlas_triage_hindsight_operations_saved_estimate{unit}`. `documents` counts skipped sections never retained, each one Hindsight extraction not run; `batches` counts versions with every section skipped, each one retain operation not submitted.

## 2026-09-30: Owner override: TradingView as a source (ticket 31)

The owner decided to use their TradingView subscription through the MCP server as an Atlas source, despite the display-only terms recorded above. In the owner's words: "this is a hobby project as of now. Building the function does not mean this will be anything more then a PoC", and "Yes I want to proceed." The override is:
- off by default (`ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_ENABLED`);
- limited to transcripts (Tier B), the filing catalog and news-headline leads;
- marked on every stored item;
- paced gently;
- never committed to git as real content (fixtures are synthetic).

The owner makes the repository private before any real content could reach it. The account and terms risk is the owner's, accepted knowingly.
## 2026-09-30: Scenarios and the Financial Analyst (Phase 3-6a ticket 19)

- **The model** (`atlas.scenarios.model`, `scenario-v1`) is §8.2 with ticket 12's BOM share as an explicit input: `component_price = downstream_unit_price × bom_share`; `incremental_revenue = addressable_units × company_share × component_price`; `incremental_contribution = revenue × operating_margin`; `scenario_enterprise_value = contribution × ev_multiple`; `net_debt = total_debt − cash`; `illustrative_equity_value = EV − net_debt`; `equity_value_per_share = equity / diluted_shares`; and `revenue_exposure = incremental_revenue / reported_revenue` (the exposure against the company's size). §8.2's "scenario enterprise value − net debt" subtracts the whole company's net debt from the incremental EV; it is kept as written and labelled illustrative. It is a pure function: no LLM, database or clock. No PyMC.
- **Inputs.** Each of the ten inputs has low, base and high values (low ≤ base ≤ high, within the input's bounds: fractions 0–1, margin −1–1, divisors above 0) and is `sourced` (an XBRL observation or an Assertion), `estimated` (with a non-blank basis) or `missing`. **A value with neither a source nor a basis doesn't validate** (Pydantic, 422 `invalid_request`). An input left out is missing. A missing input blocks every line built on it, which names the missing inputs; nothing is filled in.
- **A source must stand at the cutoff** (`atlas.scenarios.sources`, 422 `invalid_scenario` listing each problem): an XBRL observation must be the company's **as-of** observation for its period key at the cutoff (ticket 18's selection: the latest periodic filing with `available_at ≤ as_of`; so a value a later filing restated or reaffirmed, or one not yet available, is refused); only a currency amount (revenue, debt, cash) or a share count may be XBRL-sourced; it must be in the table's currency (or `shares`), since currencies are never mixed without an FX basis; and its value must equal the input in every case (a sourced figure isn't a range). An Assertion must exist, be available at the cutoff (its Source Version's `available_at`) and not be rejected or superseded; its number can't be checked mechanically, so its quote is shown beside the value. The cutoff is the investigation's `as_of` unless the request gives one.
- **Determinism.** `Decimal` in a fixed context (precision 50, half-even); every line rounded half-even to 6 places; every number a plain decimal string (no exponent, no trailing zeros, no negative zero); outputs are canonical JSON (sorted keys, `,`/`:` separators, UTF-8), stored as that text and hashed (SHA-256).
