The same table, however it was written (JSON numbers or strings, trailing zeros, key order), gives the same bytes. Each read recomputes the stored table and reports `recomputes_identically`.
- **Sensitivity**: each present input of the base case moved −20% and +20% alone, every line recomputed; a fraction or margin pushed past its bound is clamped and marked `capped`.
- **Attached to a Hypothesis version**, insert-only (`scenario`, audited `scenario.created`). A scenario may be attached to a published version (the version itself doesn't change). The company must be one of the Hypothesis's related companies.
- **The Financial Analyst fills the plan's slot** (`roles/financial_analyst.py`, prompt `financial-analyst.v1.md`): after the Investigators, with the accepted Claims (by Assertion ID; quotes as low-trust retrieved data) and, per seed company an accepted Claim names, its as-of XBRL figures (per metric the latest *reported* value, an annual one for a flow metric where there is one; derived quarters aren't sent because a sourced input cites one observation). One call proposes a table per company. Code keeps an input only if it validates on its own and its source stands **and was sent** (an observation of that company's figures, an Assertion of the Claims); anything else becomes `missing` with `rejected: <reason>` and is listed in the task's `rejected_inputs`. With no accepted Claim naming a seed company it is skipped without an LLM call. The tables go in the task's artifacts; `POST /hypotheses/{id}/scenarios {version}` without `assumptions` turns them into scenarios (re-checked at the cutoff: a source that no longer stands becomes missing), with the task and role call as provenance. The researcher's own table (with `assumptions`) is the analyst override; it is refused rather than downgraded when a source doesn't stand.
- **Not built:** a market-data as-of (§5.7's "market-data as-of"): there's no market-data provider (Phase 6b), so a multiple is always an estimate; the metric `atlas_scenario_runs` (spec story 54) is left to the metrics ticket.

How Atlas implements the override (`atlas.tradingview`; the owner cleared live use on 2026-09-30):
- **Override text.** Every catalog entry, headline and transcript Source Version (`metadata.owner_override`) and every TradingView fetch observation (`fetch_observation.owner_override`) records `TradingView terms (display-only) overridden by the owner for private non-commercial use (2026-09-30)`.
- **Off means off.** With `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_ENABLED=false` (the default) the two jobs fail at once, and `atlas tradingview login`/`check` exit 1, all without a request. The site register and fetch gate don't apply: they govern crawling public sites, and this is an authenticated API used under the owner's own account. The override is the gate.
- **Three tools only.** `mcp-tv-get-documents`, `mcp-tv-get-document-view` (transcript views only; summaries are never fetched as evidence, PDFs aren't retrievable) and `mcp-tv-get-news`. Prices, fundamentals, the screener, alerts and watchlists have no code path.
- **Transcripts** are Tier B Source Documents: provider `tradingview`, source type `transcript`, publisher `<upstream> via TradingView` (e.g. Quartr), licence class `licensed:tradingview-owner-override`. The raw bytes are the tool's answer exactly as received (media type `application/x-tradingview-transcript+json`). They are parsed by `tradingview-transcript-v1`, which flattens the AST to one line per paragraph with its speaker label. `available_at` is the event's `reported` time (basis `publisher_timestamp`), as the ticket asks. The view's own `published` time is kept in the metadata. The Source Document URL `https://mcp.tradingview.com/documents/<id>/views/<view id>` is an identifier Atlas builds; nothing is served there.
- **Catalog** rows are metadata only (`tradingview_catalog_entry`, upserted per TradingView document ID). **News** headlines are Tier C leads with origin `tradingview_news`: no query, an empty snippet, the TradingView story URL (else the headline's link), and a `tradingview_headline` row with the publisher, published time and related symbols. They belong to the themes of the company they were listed for. Story text is never fetched.
- **Pacing.** At most 1 request/s (`ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_RATE_PER_S`, default 0.5; every HTTP request waits). At most `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_MAX_CALLS_PER_JOB` (20) tool calls per job. Each tool call is recorded (`tradingview_request`) and counted in a third rolling-window budget, provider `tradingview`, unit `requests` (`ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_BUDGET_REQUESTS`, 200 per window, far under TradingView's ~100/min). The jobs aren't pausable: a TradingView outage doesn't hold Hindsight or LiteLLM work.
- **Auth.** Atlas gets its own OAuth token (`atlas tradingview login`), following the MCP authorization spec: protected-resource metadata, then authorization-server metadata, dynamic client registration when offered, then authorization code + PKCE (S256) with the RFC 8707 `resource` and a loopback redirect. Tokens live in a gitignored token file (mode 0600) that carries its token endpoint and client, or are given as secrets. They are refreshed before expiry and once after a 401 (a rotated refresh token is written back to the file), and they are never logged.
## 2026-09-30: The publish gate and Research Snapshots (Phase 3-6a ticket 20)

- **Publishing needs the owner's approval of every Relationship the version depends on** (map ticket 07). A version depends on the Relationships its findings' Assertions support (`relationship_assertion`). Two gate checks, after `no_falsifier` and `no_unresolved_question`, and all failures are reported:
  - `relationship_not_approved`: an edge is `machine_reviewed`, `needs_human_review` or `rejected`. Machine review is not approval.
  - `relationship_review_pending`: a finding's Assertion hasn't been machine-reviewed yet, so it may still form an edge the owner hasn't seen. An Assertion reviewed `not_eligible` (no layer, co-mention) supports no edge and holds nothing.

  The investigation's final stop chains the machine review (ticket 16), so a drafted Hypothesis normally meets only the first. The refusal is typed: 422 `publish_gate_failed` with `failures: [{code, message, relationship_ids, assertion_ids}]`. The counterevidence's Assertions are not dependencies: it argues against the version.
- **The Research Snapshot is written in the publishing transaction** (`atlas.snapshots`, a publish hook that the API always installs). A version is published with its snapshot or not at all. If the archive write fails, the publication rolls back. An object already put stays in the archive, orphaned, which is harmless because it is content-addressed. **Contents** (format `atlas.research_snapshot.v1`):
  - the version (ID, number, content hash, who published it and when) and the investigation's question and as-of cutoff;
  - **the Source Versions considered**: every Source Version that a role of the version's runs was sent a passage or quote of (a `retrieved` item whose source is `<id>#<start>-<end>`), that an extraction named, that a finding or Assertion cites, or that a cited financial observation came from. Each one has its raw and parsed hashes, archive URIs and effective `available_at` (with the recorded one beside it);
  - **Memory exactly as returned to the run**: every retrieved item a role was sent whose source is a mental model or memory (the Scout's Bottlenecks gaps). Atlas doesn't store recall results. A recall only chose which sections the Investigator read, and the model was sent Source Version text, not memory text. So those choices are listed (`recall_selections`: extraction, passage, section, range) instead, and `used` says whether any Memory reached the run. (Since memory-directed reading ticket 01 the Scout's recalls are stored, and the snapshot carries them as `memory.reading_pointers`: "Memory as the reading index" below. Since ticket 05 the Investigator makes no recall: the passages a pointer chose are `memory.pointer_selections`, and `recall_selections` lists only the passages of extractions made before it);
  - the Assertions cited by the findings, the counterevidence and the scenario inputs, with their exact spans and review state, and the Relationships depended on, with the owner's decision;
  - the version's **scenarios** (canonical assumptions and their hash, outputs as the stored canonical text and its hash) and the **financial dataset**: the XBRL observations their inputs cite, with the SHA-256 of that list's canonical JSON;
  - **runs** (code, Hindsight and template versions, the LiteLLM deployments per alias) and **role calls**. The role calls come from the investigation run and each Editor draft run up to this version, plus the machine reviews of the version's Assertions and the scenario proposals. Each has its prompt name, version and SHA-256, the model alias, and each attempt's routed model. `hindsight` gives the bank and the distinct Hindsight versions of those runs;
  - `outputs`: the version's content as published.
- **Storage and verification** (map ticket 09): the canonical JSON (sorted keys, fixed separators, UTF-8) goes in the archive's new `snapshots` namespace, `archive://snapshots/sha256/<hex>`. That is `snapshots/sha256/<hex>` as a key, like `raw/` and `parsed/`, rather than the map's `snapshots/<sha256>`, so the archive keeps one key scheme. An insert-only `research_snapshot` row records the hash. Triggers (`ENABLE ALWAYS`) refuse UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE and an INSERT for an unpublished version, and a CHECK ties the URI to the hash. The audit event `research_snapshot.created` carries the hash as `new_hash`. **Every read verifies it**: `GET /api/v1/snapshots/{id}` re-hashes the archived bytes against the row and checks that they name the row's version. An altered or missing object is 500 `snapshot_integrity_failed`, and its content is never served. `GET /api/v1/snapshots?hypothesis_id=` lists the rows. On the filesystem archive, immutability is the app's and the database's, not the storage's, until MinIO object lock.
- **Metric:** `atlas_research_snapshots_total`, counted from the insert-only rows.

## 2026-09-30: Proposed updates (Phase 3-6a ticket 21)

- **A published version's dependencies are recorded when it is published** (`hypothesis_dependency`, insert-only, in the publishing transaction): its findings' Assertions, the Claims they cite, the Relationships those Assertions support, and the Source Versions of their spans. The counterevidence a version lists is not a dependency: it already argues against it. Versions published before migration 0038 get theirs from their content.
- **What contradicts a version** is decided deterministically (no LLM, so the `check_contradictions` job isn't pausable). Four events, each hooked where it is recorded, in its transaction. A hook enqueues the job only when one indexed query finds a published version the event touches, so ordinary ingest and review enqueue nothing:
  - `assertion_reviewed`: a dependent Assertion is reviewed `disputed`, `rejected` or `superseded` (the states the version diff calls contradicting). "A later contradictory Assertion" is expressed this way: the owner (or a reviewer) supersedes or disputes the cited Assertion with one on the later source. A new Assertion on its own never contradicts anything: Atlas can't tell, without a model, whether it disagrees.
  - `relationship_rejected`: the owner rejects a Relationship the version depends on. Approving it again later doesn't withdraw the update; the owner dismisses it.
  - `source_revised`: a new Source Version of a Source Document an earlier version of which a dependent Assertion quotes. **Contradicting means the revision's parsed text no longer contains the quote.** A revision that still states every cited quote proposes nothing, and neither does one without parsed text (it can't be compared). A new, separate document can't contradict by itself: its statements reach a version through Assertions (the first event) or the Skeptic (the last).
  - `counterevidence`: the Skeptic's counterevidence, **accepted and independent** (ticket 15's rules: its Evidence Family is none of the supporting Claims'), against a Claim whose Assertion states the same span (the same Source Version and offsets) as a dependent Assertion. Claims and their Assertions belong to one investigation, so the span is what identifies "the same statement" across investigations.
- **One proposed update per Hypothesis and event.** Only each Hypothesis's latest published version is flagged (an older one has been corrected already). It's unique per (version, event key), so a retried or re-enqueued job adds nothing. If the event no longer holds when the job runs (the Assertion or Relationship was reviewed back), nothing is proposed and the job's artifacts say why. An update lists the contradicting Evidence (where it is stated: the successor or counterevidence Assertion and its quote, or the revised Source Version and the one it revises), the findings it bears on, and the Candidates it concerns: open Candidates in the Hypothesis's theme whose committed company is a related company of the Hypothesis or a party to a contradicted Assertion. That is the Candidate "flag": `GET /api/v1/proposed-updates?candidate_id=`. The Candidate read model is unchanged.
- **The owner decides; nothing is changed automatically.** `accept` starts a correction (ticket 16's lifecycle): a new unpublished version from the latest one, whose findings resting on a contradicted Assertion get `needs_review: true` and the update's summary as a limitation. It's audited like a correction (`hypothesis.corrected`) plus `proposed_update.accepted`. The owner edits it further and publishes it behind the gate, which writes its own snapshot. The status doesn't change. A closed or rejected Hypothesis can only dismiss. `dismiss` needs a reason. Either resolves the update once (a trigger refuses any other change, and deletes).
- **The published version and its Research Snapshot are only read.** The gate test checks the snapshot row, the archived bytes (so its hash) and the verified read are identical before and after a contradictory revision is collected, as is the version row.
## 2026-09-30: Replay banks (Phase 3-6a ticket 22)

Resolved design: map ticket 09 and spec "Snapshots and replay". `atlas.replay`, `POST/GET /api/v1/replay-jobs`.

- **Local Hindsight only.** A replay runs against `ATLAS_REPLAY_HINDSIGHT_URL` (the Compose profile's Hindsight or a throwaway one), a setting separate from the research bank's `ATLAS_HINDSIGHT_URL`, so pointing production at the shared server never sends replay banks there. Without it, `POST /replay-jobs` is 503 `replay_not_configured`. Its bank is `atlas-replay-<replay UUID>`, and the gateway refuses to delete any bank without that prefix (`HindsightGateway.delete_bank`, a rule checked before any call).
- **Seeded by re-retaining only, never export/import** (map ticket 09): an export can carry extraction and entity resolution done after the cutoff. The bank is created by importing the bank template **without its mental models** (their import queues refreshes, LLM work the replay doesn't use, and #4532's loop risk); the application is recorded and audited like the research bank's.
- **What it may see:** Source Versions with `source_version_availability.available_at <= cutoff` (the corrected view: an EDGAR dissemination correction counts), the latest version of each Source Document at the cutoff, English and parsed, of the requested companies (default: every company). This is §9.2's `available_at` convention (historical reconstruction), not the stricter `ingested_at <= cutoff` one, and each replay records it (`availability_convention`). The cutoff may not be in the future. **Cost bound:** at most `ATLAS_REPLAY_MAX_SOURCE_VERSIONS` (default 3, the map's "~3 documents") of them, the latest; a request may lower it. They are chosen when the replay is requested and listed on it.
- **Retention triage is bypassed:** every section of a chosen version is retained, through production retention's own functions (`version_sections`, `version_tags`, `retain_item`: same document IDs, tags, metadata and timestamps). Triage decisions are recorded after the fact and would make the replay depend on later work; the version cap bounds the cost instead. Zero-fact sections aren't reprocessed. Versions are retained **one at a time in chronological order** (availability, then ID), each waited for before the next is submitted.
- **The replay's own ledger:** sections go into `replay_document`, never `memory_document` (whose readers aren't bank-filtered everywhere, e.g. `unretained_versions`), and the provenance resolver takes a pluggable section lookup (`SectionLookup`), so a replay's citations resolve only to Source Versions it retained.
- **Consolidation:** after the retains, an explicit `POST …/consolidate`, waited for up to `ATLAS_REPLAY_CONSOLIDATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` per attempt and `ATLAS_RETAIN_POLL_ATTEMPTS` attempts; then it is recorded `timed_out` and the questions run anyway (the replay discloses it rather than failing).
- **Questions:** a fixed, versioned set from `configs/replay/question-sets.yaml` (name, version and SHA-256 recorded). Each question gets a recall and a reflect scoped like production research (company tags, else every theme's tag; `any_strict`), each memory and citation resolved and stored.
- **Leakage is counted independently of the ledger** (`leakage`, per answer and in total, `future_accepted` their sum): retained versions whose availability is now after the cutoff, recalled memories whose document ID, metadata or resolved provenance names a version the replay may not see, and resolved citations or quotes that lead to one. The gate test requires 0 for a future-dated fixture that the research bank does hold.
- **One bounded step per attempt:** the `replay` job (pausable, `codex`-budgeted: its retain batches and consolidation count as operations, via `replay_operation`) does one step per attempt (create bank, one retain, consolidation, one question, delete) and raises `Requeue`, so no attempt outlives the 5-minute lease. A quota or outage failure pauses the queue and the replay resumes where it was, its bank kept. Any other failure, or a cancel (`POST /replay-jobs/{id}/cancel`), ends the replay (`failed`/`cancelled`) and goes straight to deleting the bank. Only a deletion that fails on the job's last attempt leaves a bank behind, and the replay then says so (`bank_delete_error`, no `bank_deleted_at`).
- **Not verified live:** bank deletion, explicit consolidation in a new bank, and the whole replay have run only against the recorded fake (see the fake's derivations above). The live run is opt-in (`docs/runbooks.md`, "Replay banks").


## 2026-09-30: Measure the real system first (the Codex review)

An independent review of the source (Codex, 2026-09-30) found that research quality was less proven than evidence traceability, and named four mechanisms in the code that kept it that way. The owner's direction: act on it, before the derivation-graph and ontology work the earlier ChatGPT review proposed. The four changes, each small:

- **Triage reads the whole section, in windows.** The Triage role saw a section's first `triage_excerpt_chars` (1,500) characters only, so a capacity or customer detail later in a long Item was invisible to retention. Now a section is read in windows of that size, at most `ATLAS_TRIAGE_WINDOWS_PER_SECTION` (default 6) of them: the first always, the rest spread evenly over a section that has more, each window one entry of the request (`part` of `parts`, anchor `<anchor>~<part>` past the first) and one low-trust quoted text, `triage_sections_per_call` windows to a call. A section is retained when any window is (that window's category and reason, marked `part p/n`); skipped when every window read is. The rubric is `triage.v2` (the prompt describes windows; the categories are unchanged). Decisions stay one per section. Cost: more triage items for long sections only (MiniMax tokens), no more retains than before.
- **Precision counts every verified edge.** `relationship_precision` and `relationship_recall` were pass rates over the expectations a case listed, so an extra wrong edge escaped scoring unless the case had foreseen it. Every case whose pipeline produces edges now also scores `relationships.unexpected`: no machine-reviewed or approved edge that neither a `relationships` nor a `claims` entry names. Edges in `needs_human_review` are the owner's queue and don't fail it. The gold cases are unchanged (they are immutable); the check is emitted by the scorer.
- **Live evaluation uses the real Hindsight.** `atlas evaluate --live` stubbed Hindsight with an empty recall, so the memory-and-retrieval contribution, the reason Hindsight is in the architecture, had never been measured. Live mode now needs `ATLAS_HINDSIGHT_URL` (refused without it, like the model), gives each case a throwaway bank `atlas-eval-<case>-<random>` on it, retains the case's sources into that bank through triage (`retention_triage=auto`) and drains those jobs before the pipeline runs, and deletes the bank when the case ends (a failed deletion is recorded as a case error). Fake mode is unchanged: the stub, no retains.
- **Interactive work before backfill.** The job selector ordered every runnable job by creation time, so the owner's own ingest or investigation waited behind a backfill enqueued hours earlier. `claim` now orders by `(job_class = 'backfill')` first, then age: the oldest interactive job, else the oldest backfill. Budgets, the pause and the window are unchanged.

Not changed here, on purpose: the Skeptic's independence rule (Evidence Family ≠ source origin; needs a source-origin concept, a later ticket), the decisions-first landing page (after the pilot: five real investigations will show what the daily view needs), and the reflect timeouts (Atlas uses the shared cluster Hindsight, whose reflect and mental-model refresh timeouts were raised on 2026-09-28).


## 2026-09-30: Measuring what triage misses (ticket 33)

The Codex review (item 3) asked for triage's recall to be measured before its cost is tuned further. Rules:

- **Windows overlap.** Consecutive triage windows overlap by `ATLAS_TRIAGE_WINDOW_OVERLAP_CHARS` (default 200, less than the window): a window starts `excerpt - overlap` characters after the previous one, and the last ends at the section's end (`atlas.retention.triage.window_spans`). A sentence at a boundary is read whole in the next window. A section's window count grows slightly (stride 1,300 instead of 1,500); the windows chosen for a long section are still the first, the last and evenly spread ones.
- **What is audited.** Every section whose **effective** triage decision is `skip`, whatever decided it (role, rule, inherited), optionally of one company. The audit reads the decision's own offsets in the **archived parse** (never refetches), and a text whose SHA-256 no longer matches the decision's `content_sha256` is recorded as an error, not judged.
- **Sampling.** Strata are form (the document type, else the form, else the source type) × length band (`under-2k`, `2k-10k`, `10k-50k`, `50k-plus` characters). Each stratum is sorted, then shuffled by `random.Random("triage-audit:<seed>:<form>:<band>")`, and the sample is drawn round-robin over the strata in (form, band) order: **equal allocation**, so the long sections triage reads least are represented however rare. The drawn sample (`plan`) and each stratum's population and draw are stored when the audit is created, so a resumed job judges the same sample and the same seed over the same decisions draws the same sample. Because allocation is equal, the plain miss rate is the sample's; the summary also gives a **population-weighted** rate (each judged stratum's rate weighted by its share of the skipped sections).
- **The judge** is its own role, `triage_judge` (prompt `triage_judge.v1.md`): the criteria of triage rubric v2 applied to the whole section, one section per call, told nothing about triage's decision. A section longer than `ATLAS_TRIAGE_JUDGE_MAX_CHARS` (60,000, about 15,000 tokens) is read in chunks of that size, overlapping like the windows, one call each; it is `retain` when any chunk is, and reading stops at the first retaining chunk (its reason is marked `chunk c/n`). A quarantined or refused judge call leaves the sample unjudged, with its error; it is counted as `unjudged`, not in the rate.
- **Windows read** per sample: none for a rule; as the decision's reason records it for a multi-window reading (`none of N windows read (of M)`); else one (a section that fits one window, a one-window reader, or the opening-only reader before windows), of as many windows as the audit's window size and overlap make.
- **A miss** is a sample triage skipped and the judge would retain (`agreement` false). The miss rate's 95% interval is **Wilson's** (it stays in [0, 1] and is informative at 0 of n; exact at 0 and n). The summary breaks it down by length band, triage category, triage method and form, counts the misses by the judge's category and lists up to 10 misses with their anchor, heading and the judge's reason.
- **Pacing.** The `triage_audit` job is `minimax`-budgeted and pausable. One attempt judges at most `ATLAS_TRIAGE_AUDIT_SAMPLES_PER_ATTEMPT` (3) samples and is requeued (no attempt used), so an attempt stays inside the job lease and the rolling budget is checked between attempts; a spent run budget requeues it the same way. A retain-or-skip judge answer costs at most 512 output tokens; a 40-sample audit of Lumentum and Coherent is roughly 40 to 60 calls.
- **Storage (a deviation in naming):** the ticket named one insert-only `triage_audit` table. The per-sample rows are that table (insert-only, trigger-enforced); the audit itself (parameters, strata, plan, status, the summary stored at completion) is a separate `triage_audit_run` row, like `evaluation_run`/`evaluation`. Creation and completion are audited (`triage_audit.created`, `triage_audit.completed` with the summary's hash). The API computes the summary from the rows on every read, so a running audit shows its progress.

## 2026-09-30: Triage tuning from the first live audits

Two live triage audits (`docs/implementation-log.md`, 2026-09-30) found 3 misses in 97 skipped sections (about 3%): audit 1 (Lumentum and Coherent, 40 of 42 skipped sections) 1/40, audit 2 (a census of Lumentum, Coherent, AXT and Applied Optoelectronics) 2/57. Each miss had a different cause, so each gets its own change.

- **Windows: 6 → 10** (`ATLAS_TRIAGE_WINDOWS_PER_SECTION`). Audit 1's miss was a Lumentum Q4 FY26 EX-99.1 chunk of 11,948 characters: triage read 6 of its 10 windows and missed a footnote on a $7.8B non-cash loss from equitizing convertible notes (`dilution_financing`). With 1,500-character windows overlapping by 200, ten windows read a section of up to about 13,300 characters in full; longer ones are still sampled. Cost: more MiniMax tokens on sections of 7 to 10 windows, and more sampled windows on longer ones; no more retains than before unless a window is retained.
- **Rules `triage-rules-v2`: exhibit indexes are no longer boilerplate.** Audit 2's miss (a) was a 10-Q "Item 6. Exhibits" section (2,878 characters) skipped by rule while its index named "Exhibit 10.3: Master Development and Supply Agreement with Coherent Corp". The headings `Exhibit Index`, `Index to Exhibits` and `Item … (Financial Statements and) Exhibits` (10-Q Item 6, 10-K Item 15, 8-K Item 9.01) now go to the role; they are short. The other rules (forward-looking statements, signatures, mine safety, `[Reserved]`, Form 10-K summary, no content past the heading) stay.
- **Rubric `triage.v3`** (judge `triage_judge.v2`, the same criteria applied to the whole text, so the audit measures against the rubric triage uses). Framed around multi-hop supply-chain bottleneck discovery (the owner's direction, after `docs/research/serenity-skills-alignment.md` M2, M5, M6, M10, V11 on `research/serenity-skills-alignment`): from AI and datacenter capex to hardware, components, sub-components, feedstock and process steps, retain what says who supplies whom at any layer and where qualified supply may fall short. The categories are unchanged; their descriptions now say explicitly that (i) an exhibit index listing a supply, purchase, development, license or capacity agreement with a named counterparty is `retain` (`supplier_customer`), (ii) buying, leasing or building out a manufacturing, assembly, test or warehouse facility is `retain` (`capacity`; audit 2's miss (b), an 8-K of 2,136 characters on a 153,928 sq ft Houston property for office, warehouse, light manufacturing and assembly, which the role read in full and called real estate), and (iii) financial statements and footnotes disclosing a financing event (converts, equitization, ATMs, debt extinguishment, share issuance) are `retain` (`dilution_financing`). They also name single- and sole-source statements, qualification cycles, feedstock and materials (InP and GaAs substrates, indium, gallium, germanium, specialty gases), process equipment (MOCVD), export controls, tariffs and geographic concentration, and technology transitions (EML, VCSEL, silicon photonics, co-packaged optics). An exhibit index listing only standard documents stays `skip`/`boilerplate`.
- **Recorded:** every new decision carries `rules_version` `triage-rules-v2` and `rubric_version` `triage.v3`; audits carry `triage_judge.v2`. Earlier decisions keep their versions (insert-only) and are not re-triaged: an unchanged section of a later version still inherits its predecessor's decision. Whether the changes help is for the next live audit to measure, not these fixture tests.
## 2026-09-30: Research roles follow the supply-chain bottleneck method

The owner's direction (2026-09-30): Atlas hunts supply-chain bottlenecks. The method is the one the serenity-skills alignment note describes (branch `research/serenity-skills-alignment`, `docs/research/serenity-skills-alignment.md`: M2 multi-hop mapping, M5 qualification over trailing revenue, M6 dilution and financing as a standing falsifier, M8 customer concentration, M10 the bear checklist, M11 change detection). Atlas adopts the **method only**, in its own words: no text, template or data from that unlicensed repository is copied, ingested or installed.

New prompt versions, same request and response schemas (the bottleneck layer, chokepoint and mechanism fit the existing fields, so no field was truly needed):

- **Scout `scout.v2`.** Queries trace demand → hardware → components → sub-components → feedstock and process equipment, and ask who chokes each layer (single or sole source, capacity, lead times, qualification, scarce materials such as InP/GaAs substrates, indium, gallium, germanium, MOCVD tools, export controls) and what would relieve it. Generic company-news queries are ruled out; deeper layers are favoured; `purpose` names the layer.
- **Investigator `investigator.v2`.** Priorities, not new predicates: who supplies whom at any layer, single/second sourcing, capacity, allocation and lead times, qualification and design wins, feedstock and equipment dependencies. The whitelist, the direction rule and the exact-quote rule are unchanged; a lead time or qualification whose quote names no whitelisted relation is not a Claim, and a supplier missing from a list is not evidence of exclusivity (M11 stays an inference trap). Assertions it creates carry `extractor_version` `investigator.v2`.
- **Skeptic `skeptic-plan.v2`, `skeptic.v2`.** The bear checklist (its six items and their cues in `atlas.roles.skeptic`) is unchanged; the prompts spell out how to read each item: `substitutes` includes technology transitions (EML vs VCSEL vs silicon photonics, co-packaged and linear-drive optics, other substrate materials), `capacity_additions` includes new entrants, and `dilution_financing` is a standing falsifier the plan always searches for and the reader checks every passage for. Counterevidence Assertions carry `skeptic.v2`.
- **Editor `editor.v3`, `editor-hypothesis.v3`.** The research card is organised around the bottleneck the Claims point at (layer, chokepoint, mechanism); its top-level open questions begin with the bottleneck question, then its falsifiers, always including capacity relief and dilution or financing. The Hypothesis draft names the layer and chokepoint in its thesis, writes `mechanism.possible_constraint` as "<bottleneck layer>: <chokepoint> - <mechanism>", and its falsifiers always include capacity relief (new capacity or entrants, a qualified second source, a substitute or technology transition) and dilution or financing. Findings still say only what their cited quotes say.
- **Financial Analyst:** unchanged (`financial-analyst.v1`). It already sizes the BOM share (M4) and diluted shares; nothing in the method needed a new instruction there.
- **Theme config.** The photonics theme's `description` (sent to the Scout and to triage) now states the bottleneck hunt.

A unit test (`tests/unit/test_bottleneck_prompts.py`) checks that each role's current prompt carries the method's key sections and that both Skeptic prompts address every bear-checklist item by name. Whether a model follows the prompts is for the evaluation set and the live runs, not that test. Not changed: the checklist's cues (a passage about, say, silicon photonics alone isn't selected as `substitutes` counterevidence), since widening them changes which passages the Skeptic reads and should be measured first.

## 2026-09-30: The document budget split and the card with no finding (pilot fix 01)

Pilot investigation 1 (`.scratch/pilot/results.md`) ended with no output: the first Investigator (Coherent) took all 25 documents and the second (Lumentum) read none, and with no accepted Claim the Skeptic, Analyst and Editor were skipped, so there was no card.

- **The document budget is split across a round's Investigator tasks.** When a task chooses its Source Versions (once, under the investigation's lock) it may take what is left of `max_documents` less an equal share held back for each of the round's other Investigator tasks that hasn't chosen yet (pending, queued or running, no `documents` artifact): with `L` left and `k` waiting, `L − k·⌊L/(k+1)⌋`. So with 2 seeds and 25 documents the first to choose takes at most 13 and the second at least 12, and every task gets at least `⌊budget / n⌋`. A task records `documents` and `documents_dropped` in its artifacts as it chooses, so what it leaves unused passes on to the tasks that choose after it. The Investigator jobs are queued together and the queue orders ties at random, so "after it" is the worker's order, which is plan order only when the worker takes them in that order; a share is never passed back to a task that already chose. A cancelled task holds nothing back.
- **The Editor always writes a card.** With no accepted Claim (including when every accepted Claim came from a task whose premise was disproven), the Editor is still called (prompt `editor.v4`): the card has `findings` `[]`, `verdict` `needs_review`, and open questions for the next round. The investigation still stops `no_new_independent_evidence` (stop detail "no new independent Evidence: the Investigator accepted no Claims"), so the stop reason, the metrics and the chained relationship review are unchanged. A follow-up round whose accepted Claims bring no new Evidence Family still skips the Editor and keeps the earlier card.
- **What was searched and read is code's, on every card.** `searched`: each round's Scout, its queries in order, how many leads they found and the leads taken. `read`: each Investigator task, its Source Versions with the sections of the passages it was sent (the batches done, across a budget-exhausted extraction and its continuation) and how many passages, the documents the budget left out, and its extraction's outcomes (Claims proposed, accepted, rejected by reason code), or why it read nothing (the task's detail). The Editor is sent the same (`queries`, `read`) so its open questions can name what the next round should read; `editor.v4` tells it to reason from them and to claim nothing about documents it hasn't seen. Cards drawn before this have neither field (both default to empty).
- **A card with no finding can't be saved as a Hypothesis** (409: nothing to save), but its open questions can start the follow-up round, which is how the next round acts on them.
- Not changed: the Skeptic and the Financial Analyst are still skipped without an accepted Claim (nothing to challenge or quantify), and the Claim schema (the pilot's third defect is pilot fix 03).
## 2026-09-30: Lead ranking and English searches (pilot fix 02)

Pilot investigation 1 kept 10 junk leads of 100: dictionary entries for "coherent", Wikipedia, and Swedish pages (Avanza, bab.la). SearXNG answered in its instance's locale, and the investigation kept the first leads found.

- **English searches.** Every SearXNG search (the Scout's and the Skeptic's) sends `language` = `ATLAS_SEARXNG_LANGUAGE`, default `en` (owner direction: English is the main language everywhere). The setting is validated as a language code (`en`, `en-US`), so it can't be set empty by accident.
- **Ranking** (`atlas.discovery.ranking`, a pure function; config `configs/discovery/lead-ranking.yaml`, versioned). Each lead is scored by its best sighting in the discovery: query terms in the title (1) or snippet only (0.6), as a share of the query's terms (up to 60); purpose terms beyond the query's (up to 10); universe company names, matched case-sensitively as written (8 each, up to 16); the config's product and layer terms (3 each, up to 15). Demoted hosts (encyclopedias, broker and quote pages) and results that don't read as English are multiplied by 0.25 each. Denied hosts (dictionaries, translation sites) and results under `min_score` (15) are never kept. The investigation keeps the top-ranked leads up to its lead budget; the others stay Tier C leads (`/api/v1/leads`), not the investigation's.
- **Recorded.** Each `investigation_lead` row has its `score`, `reasons` (in words), the `query` that scored best and the config's `ranking_version` (migration `0042`); the Scout task's artifacts count `leads_rejected` beside `leads_dropped` (over the budget).
- **English detection** is a heuristic, not a language model: non-ASCII letters over 3% or two foreign function words (sv, de, nl, fr, es, it), unless English function words are at least as many. A headline without function words stays English.
- **Version 2 (pilot fix 08).** Why: the re-run (0.2.3, investigation `4620f8c2`, discovery `e9565f0d-9e28-41b2-bb92-f9dbe0cdd4c5`) kept 10 leads of 100 under version 1, and every one was a seed company's own page or profile: Lumentum's investor relations overview, a Lumentum press release on its own IR site, the homepage, "Company | Lumentum", LinkedIn, coherent.com's homepage, "About Us", "Lasers" and "Company" pages, and one NVIDIA newsroom release. They scored 16.5 to 21.7 on the company alone: the Scout's queries name the company ("Lumentum 200G EML chip allocation …"), so the name counted as a query term (≈ 6.7), again as "names Lumentum" (8), and the snippet's date matched the query's "2026". None of them matched a word of the query's topic. What changed:
  - **The query's topic is what counts.** Query and purpose terms are the query's words less the universe company names it contains (matched case-sensitively, as for "names") and years (a snippet's date matches them). A company the result names now adds 4, up to 8 (was 8, up to 16).
  - **A result naming no topic term is never kept** (reason "not kept without a query term"), whatever it scores: a company name alone never keeps a lead.
  - **The companies' own sites are demoted** (`company_site_factor` 0.25): the hosts of the universe companies' `website`s in the theme config, subdomains included (so `investor.lumentum.com`), plus the ranking config's `company_domains` (`ii-vi.com`). Their filings reach Atlas as Tier A Source Versions anyway.
  - **Profile and social sites are demoted hosts** (like encyclopedias): LinkedIn, Crunchbase, ZoomInfo, Bloomberg's `/profile` pages (an entry may name a path), PitchBook profiles, Glassdoor, X/Twitter, Facebook, Instagram, YouTube, and Wikiwand.
  - **Generic titles are demoted** (`generic_title_factor` 0.25): a part of the title (split at `|`, ` - `, `:` and the like) that is one of `generic_titles` ("About us", "Company", "Home", "Homepage", "Investor Relations", "Careers", "Products", …).
  - Not demoted: industry articles, trade press, regulators and standards bodies; nothing lists them.
  - **Effect on the recorded re-run** (its 90 leads recorded in `/api/v1/leads`, each scored against the query that found it): version 2 keeps none. All 10 version 1 kept now score under 8, and the rest are junk (Swedish municipal pages, Microsoft forum threads, YouTube, crossword sites). The re-run had **no industry article among its results** to promote. Its searches came back off topic (Brave was rate-limited on every query), so the lead ranking was only half its problem. The fixtures `tests/fixtures/searxng/rerun-*.json` are its real results; the articles the tests rank them against are hand-written.
  - `score`, `reasons` and `ranking_version` (now 2) are recorded on every kept lead as before.
- **Version 3** (memory-directed reading, ticket 04; "Specific filing phrases; a filer needs a kept hit"): a web lead scores as in version 2. An EDGAR filing hit is scored against its query and purpose, a hit of a bare vocabulary term is demoted (`bare_term_factor`), and the bottleneck method's vocabulary is the config's `method_terms`.
## 2026-09-30: Company-level bottleneck predicates (pilot-fixes ticket 03)

Pilot investigation 1 got no Claim from 24 passages of Coherent's FY2026 10-K. The replay (`scripts/investigator_replay.py`, report `docs/research/investigator-yield.md`) found two causes: the prompt kept the model to few, cautious proposals, and the statements that locate a bottleneck in a 10-K (sole or limited sourcing with no supplier named, in-house supply of an input, demand beyond supply, customer qualification) had no predicate, so the model forced them into `depends_on` (rejected `missing_object`) or `manufactures` (rejected `no_directional_language`). **The owner authorized extending the domain model** (2026-09-30: "we're at a building phase; I'd rather we experiment and figure out what works than under-express what we need").

- **Four predicates join the whitelist** (13 in all), each with object kind `product`, not symmetric, and its own cue list (`atlas/claims/predicates.py`): `capacity_constrained` (the subject cannot fully meet demand for the product: demand exceeds its supply, allocation, backlog, shortage, lead times), `sole_sources` (it gets the input from a single or a limited number of suppliers, named or not), `vertically_integrates` (it makes the input for its own products: in-house, captive, "our own", vertically integrated) and `qualified_for` (customers qualified it, or chose it in a design win, as a supplier of the product). Glossary: CONTEXT.md, "Bottleneck Predicate".
- **Compact on purpose.** "Demand exceeds supply" is `capacity_constrained`, not its own predicate: both say the company can't meet demand. "Single source" and "sole source" are one predicate. A named counterparty keeps the two-company predicates (`depends_on`/`buys_from` for a named supplier, `supplies` for a named customer), so the new predicates never compete with an edge between two companies.
- **Edges, not company attributes.** Each forms a Relationship from the company to a product node (`object_text`, layer-tagged), exactly like `manufactures`: the same identity (subject, predicate, object key, layer), Evidence counts, exceptions queue and owner review. So they show on the edge table, the Theme explorer and the Company dossier with no new read side, and a Hypothesis can depend on them. Migration `0043` adds them to the `relationship.predicate` check (its downgrade refuses while any Relationship uses one).
- **Checks unchanged and mandatory.** An exact quote located in its passage, the span check, the party check (the filer's "we"/"our" names the subject; there is no object company to name), and a cue for the predicate. Review is as before: a hedge ("may", "could", "expects to") sends a risk-factor statement such as "we may experience shortages" to the exceptions queue, not to a machine-reviewed edge.
- **Investigator `investigator.v3`.** Adds "A company's own statements": the filer as subject and a product object, with a line per product predicate and examples, the mapping rules above, "a list item or a sentence fragment without the company is never a quote" (the relaxed variant showed that dropping "names both parties" yields fragments), and the words a quote must contain. The empty-list instruction now applies only when no passage states any relation or company fact. Assertions carry `extractor_version` `investigator.v3`.
- **Reviewer `reviewer.v2`.** One paragraph: for a product object, confirm when the quote states the fact about the subject company and that product (no second company is needed), and what the new predicates don't cover (growing demand alone, a list of materials, making what the company sells).
- **Triage unchanged.** Rubric `triage.v3` already retains the text these predicates read: single- and sole-source statements (`supplier_customer`), constraints and allocation (`capacity`), qualification and design wins (`qualification_design_win`), feedstock dependencies (`supply_pricing`). **Evaluation scoring unchanged**: it compares (subject, predicate, object or object text) as strings, so a gold case can name the new predicates as they are.
## 2026-09-30: Page artifacts and parser version `text-v3` (pilot fix 04)

- **Why (measured).** Pilot fix 03 ran the Investigator live on Coherent's FY2026 10-K and found true quotes that could not be located: the filing is rendered from a paginated document, and each page break leaves a page number and a "Table of Contents" back-link in the text, often inside a sentence ("… design and manufacture\n9\nTable of Contents\nof transceivers …"). Quotes are located by exact match, with no whitespace folding ("Claim quotes are located, not trusted"), so a sentence that crosses a page break is not a span of the parse. In the recorded Coherent 10-K fixture, 18 of 47 page breaks fall inside a sentence.
- **Parser version `text-v3`** = `text-v2` plus page-artifact rules for HTML. Plain text, PDF and TradingView transcripts are unchanged, and so is any HTML document without a page break (same text, same content hash as under v2). The rules are in `atlas.parsing`'s docstring; in short:
  - **Page break:** a visible block element whose `style` sets `page-break-before`/`page-break-after` to `always` (or `break-before`/`break-after` to `page`). The recorded filings use `<hr style="page-break-after:always"/>`. A document without one is not paginated and nothing is removed from it.
  - **Removed: the back-link.** A page's first line, when the whole line is one same-document link (`<a href="#…">`) reading "Table of Contents".
  - **Removed: the page number.** A page's last line, when it is 1 to 3 digits **and** counts up with the pages (the nearest other page ending in such a number differs from it by exactly the number of page breaks between them).
  - **Joined: a split paragraph.** The last line before a page break and the first after it become one line, with one space, when the first does not end a sentence (`.`, `!`, `?`, `:`, `;`), the second starts with a lowercase letter and is not a list marker, and neither is a table row.
- **Conservative on purpose: a false removal corrupts the text Evidence quotes, a missed artifact only loses a quote.** Every removal needs the markup's page break, not just a text pattern. So these **stay in the text**:
  - a contents heading that is not a link, the contents page's own page references ("SIGNATURES\n135"), a back-link that is not the first line of a page or shares its line with other text;
  - a lone number at a page end, or one out of sequence (a table value, a contents page's last reference);
  - page numbers in other forms ("F-12", "Page 3", "- 3 -", roman numerals) and back-links with other wording ("Index", "Back to top"): no recorded filing has them, so no rule was written blind;
  - running titles (a company name or statement title repeated at the top of pages): in the recorded filings these are the statements' own headings ("LUMENTUM HOLDINGS INC.\nCONDENSED CONSOLIDATED BALANCE SHEETS"), not artifacts;
  - a split paragraph whose second part starts with a capital or a digit ("… Board of Directors of Applied" | "Materials, Inc., where …": 1 of the Coherent 10-K's 18). A capital also starts a heading ("ITEM 6. [RESERVED]" | "ITEM 7. …" in Lumentum's 10-K), and the markup does not tell the two apart (both end in a trailing space), so they are left as two lines. The page number and back-link between them are still removed.
  - PDF page headers and footers, and plain text: not examined in this ticket (a PDF's pages are recorded as page anchors; its running headers and footers stay in the text).
- **Effect on the recorded fixtures** (each changed line checked by hand against the v2 parse): Coherent 10-K: 38 back-links and 45 page numbers removed, 17 paragraphs joined; Coherent 10-Q: 4 back-links, 2 page numbers; Lumentum 10-K: 10 back-links, 41 page numbers; Lumentum 10-Q: 5 back-links, 6 page numbers. The 8-K and the EX-99.1 have page breaks but no artifacts: same hashes as under v2 (`tests/fixtures/parser/golden.json`). The sectioner's anchors are the same for every recorded filing.
- **The archived raw bytes are untouched,** and nothing records what was removed beyond the parser version: the removal is a pure function of the raw bytes and `text-v3`, so it can be recomputed from the archive at any time.
- **Existing Source Versions are not re-parsed.** A version keeps the parse it was recorded with: `parser_version` `text-v2`, its `content_sha256` and its archived parsed object (the trigger on `source_version` refuses to overwrite a recorded parse; the archive is content-addressed). So every Assertion span, triage decision, memory document offset and Research Snapshot made on a v2 parse stays valid. Re-fetching or re-importing the same bytes makes no new version and no new parse. Only new versions (new documents, and revisions of existing ones) are parsed by `text-v3` (`tests/integration/test_parser_versions.py`).
- **What the mix of parser versions means until a re-parse:**
  - A quote across a page break is still not locatable in a version parsed by v2, the pilot's already-ingested Coherent 10-K included. On a development or test database the fix is a fresh database and ingest; **for the pilot database the owner decides** between living with it for the filings already ingested and the re-parse below.
  - Evidence Families match by content hash, then SimHash (threshold 3). A v2 and a v3 parse of the same bytes have different content hashes; their SimHash distance is 0 for the two recorded 10-Ks, 2 for Coherent's 10-Q fixture and 6 for Lumentum's (short documents move more). So a syndicated copy ingested now may found its own family beside a v2-parsed original. Families are never merged; this is left as is.
  - Triage inherits decisions for unchanged sections by content hash: the first v3 version of a document after a v2 one re-triages the sections whose text changed.
  - Proposed updates: a cited quote that contains an artifact, or spans a line break that v3 joins, is "no longer contained" in the next version of its document, and is flagged for the owner like any vanished quote. Quotes inside one paragraph of one page are not affected.
- **Re-parsing, when it is wanted, is a separate parse record, never an overwrite** (as `docs/data-model.md` §2.4 already anticipates). Built by pilot-fixes ticket 11 ("Re-parsing recorded Source Versions", below); not built in this ticket (it needs a migration, a job and a change to every reader of the parse, so it is not trivial). The design:
  1. An insert-only `source_parse` table keyed by (Source Version, parser version): content hash, parsed object URI, status, language, page anchors. The `source_version` parse columns stay what they are: the parse the version was recorded with.
  2. A `reparse` job reads the **archived raw bytes** (never refetches), parses them with the current parser, archives the text and inserts the row; idempotent and audited.
  3. Assertions are never moved. An existing Assertion stays bound to the parse it was made on; extraction on the new parse creates new Assertions (which may supersede old ones through the normal review), and each records which parse it quotes.
  4. Memory is per parse too: retaining the new parse is a `reprocess` of the version's sections (Codex budget), and the provenance resolver must resolve a memory document against the parse it was retained from.

## 2026-09-30: Counterparty companies (pilot-fixes ticket 05)

Supply-chain edges need both ends. Pilot investigation 1 could not turn Coherent's "strategic multi-year supply agreement with NVIDIA" into an edge, because NVIDIA is not one of the 12 universe companies and a Claim's company object had to be one. The same holds for hyperscalers, other suppliers and equipment makers. This extends the domain model under the owner's authorization of 2026-09-30 ("we're at a building phase, I'd rather we experiment and figure out what works than under-express what we need"), like the bottleneck predicates.

- **A company has a role** (`company.role`, migration `0045`): `researched` (the universe: the theme config's companies and committed Candidates) or `counterparty`. A **Counterparty** (CONTEXT.md) exists only so a Relationship has its other end. It has an identity and nothing else: no source path (a check constraint), no layer, no theme membership, no `universe_company` row.
- **Created only by an accepted Claim.** The Investigator (`investigator.v4`) gives a company object either as `object_company_id` (a known company) or as `object_name`: the name of a company outside the list, exactly as the quote writes it. Extraction resolves the name (`atlas.counterparties.resolve_named_company`) and, only when the Claim has passed every check, creates the counterparty in the Claim's transaction, before its Assertion (audited `company.counterparty_created`, actor `atlas-investigator`; one savepoint, so a refused Assertion leaves no company). The ticket also names the Reviewer; the Reviewer proposes no company, so only the Investigator creates counterparties.
- **Resolution is deterministic and exact.** First the companies Atlas has (researched or counterparty), by normalized name or alias, with no network call. Else `resolve_mention` by name: SEC's ticker file and submissions, then GLEIF. The name must answer to **exactly one** real company: one SEC registrant whose name equals it after `normalize_name` (an unsponsored-ADR shell never counts); or, when SEC has none, one GLEIF record one of whose names (legal, other, transliterated) equals it. Never a similarity match: "Sumitomo Electric" does not resolve to "Sumitomo Electric Industries, Ltd.". No match is `unresolved_company`, several are `ambiguous_company`, and the Claim is rejected with that reason. Without `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT` there is no resolver, so a name that is none of Atlas's companies is `unresolved_company` and nothing is created; a registry outage rejects the Claim the same way instead of failing the extraction.
- **The checks stay in force.** The quote must contain `object_name` as written (whole words, case-sensitive; `party_not_in_quote` otherwise, checked before any registry is asked), then the exact-quote placement, the span check, the subject's party check and the directional cue, as for any Claim. A name that resolves but whose quote states no relation creates no company.
- **Identity.** Display name: the name as quoted. Legal name: the registry's. A SEC-identified counterparty has its CIK and no LEI (a CIK↔LEI link stays the owner's to confirm); a GLEIF-identified one has its LEI. Country: `US` for a US state of incorporation, GLEIF's jurisdiction, else unknown, so `company.country` is now nullable for a counterparty (SEC gives a foreign registrant's country only as an EDGAR code, which Atlas doesn't map); a researched company always has one. `company.counterparty_resolution` keeps how it was identified (the name as quoted, the registry, its URL and time, the whole resolution); the API shows the first four as `Company.counterparty`. The ID follows the universe's rule (from the CIK, else the slug), and the slug comes from the quoted name.
- **Never researched.** `atlas ingest` and the `ingest` job refuse a counterparty by name; an investigation refuses one as a seed (422 `invalid_seed_companies`); `extend_universe` never includes one; it is in no theme, so retention tags, lead ranking and the Scout never see it. It is a known company to the Investigator from the next batch on (listed by ID and names, its mentions tag passages), so it can also be a Claim's subject.
- **Included in the reads.** The edge table and the Company dossier need no change (any company can end an edge; the dossier shows `role` and no themes). The theme map now holds the edges between a theme company and a counterparty, and lists those counterparties (`counterparties`); they are never among the theme's companies, so layers and coverage gaps don't count them. An edge to a *researched* company outside the theme stays off the map, as before. `GET /api/v1/companies?role=` filters by role.
- **Promotion** is the owner's. Adding the company to the theme config (same CIK, or same slug for a company without one) and seeding makes the same row `researched` (its ID, Assertions and Relationships stay). Or through Candidates: a counterparty is not in the universe, so `propose_candidates` resolves a lead naming it like any unseeded company (`resolve_mention(..., researched_only=True)`) and proposes a Candidate; committing a Candidate whose CIK (or LEI, for one without a CIK) is a counterparty's promotes that row in place (audited `company.promoted`) instead of refusing `already_in_universe`. There is no separate promote endpoint.
- **Not done:** a subject outside the known companies (`subject_name`); only the object can be named, which covers a filer's customers, suppliers and owners once they are counterparties. Aliases for a counterparty's other spellings. The evaluation gold format has no `object_name` yet (scripted answers send null).

## 2026-09-30: Claim precision: the layer from the quote, one clause (pilot-fixes ticket 09)

In pilot investigation 1's re-run (0.2.3), 4 of 14 accepted Claims were wrong or worthless, and one became a `machine_reviewed` edge. There were two shapes. **Layer over-tagging:** generic Lumentum risk-factor sentences ("some of our suppliers are our sole sources for certain materials, equipment and components"; "we purchase raw materials, packages and components from a limited number of suppliers") became `sole_sources` with layer `substrate`, the question's layer, which the text never names; so did a Coherent sentence about the Industrial segment's "exotic materials, crystals, and optics". **A cue in the neighbouring clause:** "We continue to expand our global 6-inch InP manufacturing capacity …, while also operating multiple 6-inch GaAs VCSEL manufacturing facilities" became `expands_capacity_for` → the VCSEL facilities, and `reviewer.v2` passed it (fix 03 predicted this case).

- **Cue proximity (deterministic, `atlas.claims.object_clause_cue`).** For a product object the predicate's cue must lie in a clause of the quote that names the object. The quote is cut only at a short boundary list (`CLAUSE_BOUNDARY`): `;`, `, while`/`, whilst`/`, whereas`/`, but`/`, although`/`, though` (each optionally followed by "also"), `, and also`, ` whereas`, ` while also`. **A bare comma or ", and" is never a boundary**: lists, places and appositives put commas inside one clause ("in Sherman, Texas, to address …", "including exotic materials, crystals, and optics, from sole-source … suppliers"). The object's clause is where its whole text occurs (case and spacing aside), else the clause holding most of its distinctive words (so "GaAs VCSELs" still finds the VCSEL clause); a quote naming none of the object can't be judged this way and keeps its first cue, as before. Rejected at extraction as **`cue_in_other_clause`** (the Claim's recorded cue is now the one in the object's clause) and, for any Assertion (a researcher's included), **`not_eligible` `cue_in_other_clause`** at review, so no edge forms. Tested with the VCSEL sentence (negative), "we continue to expand our 6-inch InP capacity" (positive) and real Coherent and Lumentum 10-K sentences with commas inside the clause. Company objects are not checked this way: their object is a name, and the existing party check already requires it in the quote.
- **Named, specific objects for the four bottleneck predicates (deterministic).** The object text must occur in the quote (**`object_not_in_quote`**) and must not be only generic words (materials, components, equipment, parts, packages, suppliers, raw, key, certain, several and the like: **`generic_object`**, whose reason names the layer the Claim was tagged with). Only the bottleneck predicates: the older product predicates keep their behaviour, and the prompt carries the rule for them. "Exotic materials, crystals, and optics" is not generic, so a wrong layer there is the Reviewer's (`layer: wrong`), not a rule's. **Amended 2026-10-01** ("A company-level constraint needs no named product", memory-directed reading ticket 09): for `capacity_constrained` alone, an object that is missing or names no product no longer rejects the Claim; it becomes the filer's company-level constraint, with no object and no layer. The other three predicates keep this rule.
- **Investigator `investigator.v5`.** `layer` is the layer of the object the quote names, never the question's or the company's; a quote that names nothing of a particular layer is no Claim; the four bottleneck predicates need the input or product named in the quote; generic materials/components sentences are not Claims (the pilot's two sentences as examples, with "We purchase InP substrates from a limited number of suppliers" as the specific counterpart); and "One clause", with the VCSEL sentence. The `sole_sources` example no longer uses a generic sentence. Schema unchanged; Assertions carry `extractor_version` `investigator.v5`.
- **Reviewer `reviewer.v3`.** Names both failure shapes with the pilot's sentences: generic risk-factor language for a bottleneck predicate is `rejected` (layer `wrong` or `unclear` when the tagged layer isn't what the quote names; the layer is never the research question's), and a cue belonging to another clause is `rejected` with direction `not_stated`. Schema unchanged.
- **No migration.** Reason codes are free text. Existing Assertions are not re-judged; the new eligibility reason applies to Assertions reviewed from now on (a machine review is never repeated).
- **Not measured live.** Whether MiniMax follows v5/v3 is for the next pilot re-run or `scripts/investigator_replay.py` live, with the owner's go-ahead.
## 2026-09-30: The passage budget is spread over the documents (pilot fix 10)

**Replaced on 2026-10-01** by "Passage selection: pointers and search, alternately (memory-directed reading ticket 05)": the equal deal, the `recall` selection and the Investigator's own recall are gone. What stays from this entry: the `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_PASSAGES` setting, the lead windows and their order, and `passages_by_document`.

In pilot investigation 1's re-run (0.2.3) each Investigator took 12–13 documents but was sent only 24 passages, all from its company's 10-K. The cause is the selection, not the document budget: the extraction put **every** entity-tagged window of **every** document ahead of any recall-only window, walked the documents in order (an investigation's are newest first, and the 10-K is the newest long filing), and kept the first `investigator_max_passages` (24). A 10-K has dozens of windows naming another universe company (competitors, customers, the peer group) and its Items 1 and 1A are what recall resolves to, so it alone filled the budget; results press releases (EX-99.1) and 10-Qs, which rarely name another universe company, could only enter as recall-only windows, after all of that.

- **A setting for the Investigator's budget:** `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_PASSAGES` (default 24, the old total) is each Investigator task's passage budget. The standalone `extract_claims` job and the Skeptic keep `investigator_max_passages`.
- **Dealt across the documents.** Each document offers its candidates in its own order: entity-tagged windows, then recall-only ones (preferred as before). Passages are dealt one per document per round in the documents' order until the budget is spent or no document has a candidate left. So each document gets an equal share, the remainder goes to the earlier (newer) ones, and a share a document can't use passes on to the others, the way fix 01 shares the document budget. The stored passage order is the dealing order, so a token-budget stop part-way still has read every document, and a continuation sends the rest (unchanged). A single-document extraction selects exactly as before.
- **Lead windows** for a document with no tagged and no recalled window (a press release that names no other known company, recall that resolved elsewhere): its windows in text order, its results sections first (a 10-Q's `part-i-item-2` and a 10-K's `part-ii-item-7` MD&A; an 8-K's Items 2.02, 7.01, 8.01), the cover last. They are `selected_by: ["lead"]`, dealt like the others, and not counted in `passages_dropped` (which still counts the tagged and recall-only windows not sent). Without this, fair shares alone would not read an unmatched press release.
- **Recorded per document.** The extraction's (and the Investigator task's) artifacts have `passages_by_document` (Source Version ID → passages); the card's `read` gives each document's `passages` sent, next to its sections (0 on cards stored before). The Editor's request is unchanged.
- **Measured on the recorded fixtures** (every retained section a recall hit, as the Hindsight fake derives): Lumentum's four filings split 10-K 8, 8-K 3, EX-99.1 8, 10-Q 5 (the 8-K and 10-Q have only 3 and 5 windows with text); before, 24 of the 10-K. Coherent's 10-K and 10-Q split 21 and 3 (before 24 and 0); the 10-K's supply-agreement window is still its second tagged one, so still sent.
## 2026-09-30: Companyfacts normalized whenever pending (pilot-fixes ticket 07)

Pilot investigation 1's re-run sent the Financial Analyst `figures: []` for Lumentum and Coherent, so every input was `missing`. Production had no financial observation at all (`atlas_financial_normalizations_total` 0 for both outcomes): both companies' `xbrl_companyfacts` Source Versions were recorded before normalization existed and nothing normalized them since.

- **An ingest normalizes the company's latest companyfacts Source Version whenever the current normalizer hasn't** (`atlas.financials.latest_companyfacts`: the newest version of the company's companyfacts document, and whether a `financial_normalization` row exists for it under `NORMALIZER_VERSION`), whether or not this run fetched it. Before, only the version the run's own companyfacts fetch returned was a candidate, so a run whose companyfacts request failed, or any path that didn't list companyfacts, left a pending version pending. The submissions index for the facts' acceptance times is the one the run already fetched, re-requested conditionally (a 304 in steady state). Bumping `NORMALIZER_VERSION` therefore renormalizes every company at its next ingest.
- **`EdgarAdapter.updates()` always lists companyfacts**, as `discover` already did: SEC revises it without a new filing, and a conditional request for unchanged content records only a fetch observation. Before, it was listed only when a new filing was selected.
- **`atlas financials normalize [--company slug]`** is the backfill without an ingest: per company, the same normalization and failure record as the ingest's (`financial_normalization` + `financial_normalization.created`, or `financial_normalization_failure` + `financial_normalization.failed`; the job ID is null), one company's failure never stopping the others. A submissions request that fails is reported (`submissions_unavailable`) and records nothing: no normalization was attempted, so it isn't a normalization failure and doesn't trip the alert. Exit 1 if any company didn't end normalized. Runbook: "XBRL normalization backfill".
- **The Analyst's context was checked and needs no change.** `scenarios/analyst.py` selects as of the investigation's `as_of` (`select_as_of`), sends the metrics `revenue`, `operating_income`, `cash`, `total_debt`, `debt_current`, `debt_noncurrent`, `diluted_shares`, `shares_outstanding` under those names (the prompt's inputs `reported_revenue`, `cash`, `total_debt`, `diluted_shares` accept an XBRL source; `operating_margin` is a ratio, estimated with a basis from the figures), and filters nothing by currency or unit before sending (the currency check applies to the proposal). With Lumentum's companyfacts normalized, the test at the investigation seam sees FY2026 revenue, cash, total debt and diluted shares sent, and all four proposed inputs stand as `sourced`.
- **Where the diagnosis differed from the code:** the ingest job never called `updates()` (it uses `discover`, which always listed companyfacts and normalized an unnormalized version it re-fetched as `unchanged` or `not_modified`). So on the code as deployed, any successful SEC ingest of Lumentum or Coherent after 0.2.0 would have normalized them; zero normalizations means none such completed its companyfacts fetch in production (not verifiable from the repository). The fix covers every path regardless, and the backfill doesn't wait for an ingest.
## 2026-09-30: Re-parsing recorded Source Versions (pilot-fixes ticket 11)

Fix 04's `text-v3` parses new versions only, so the pilot's filings kept their `text-v2` parse, and the re-run lost a true Lumentum `capacity_constrained` quote to a page break (`quote_mismatch`). This builds items 1 to 3 of fix 04's re-parse design and designs item 4 without building it.

- **A re-parse is its own record** (migration `0046`). `source_parse` holds one row per (Source Version, parser version): status (`parsed`, `incomplete`, `failed`, `unsupported`), error, content hash, parsed object URI, language, page anchors, the `reparse` job, the time. Insert-only (a trigger refuses UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE, ENABLE ALWAYS), unique per (version, parser version), and never under the parser the version was recorded with (an insert trigger). The `source_version` parse columns are untouched: they stay the parse the version was recorded with, under its trigger. The view `source_version_parse` lists both kinds (`recorded` true for the version's own), so a reader asks for a parse by (version, parser version).
- **The `reparse` job** (`atlas.ledger.parses`; not pausable and in no provider budget: no LLM, no Hindsight). It reads each version's **archived raw bytes** (the archive checks their hash; nothing is refetched), parses them with the current parser, archives the text (content-addressed) and inserts the row with an audit event (`source_parse.created`, the configured actor), one version per transaction, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a retried or repeated job inserts nothing twice. It selects the versions whose recorded parse has text (`parsed`, `incomplete`) under an **earlier version of the same parser** (`atlas.parsing.PARSER_LINEAGE`: `html-text-v1`, `text-v2`, `text-v3`) and that have no row for the current parser yet, optionally one company's or a list of versions. So TradingView transcripts (their own parser), unparsed formats and failed first parses are not re-parsed. A re-parse whose text equals the recorded one (a document without page artifacts) is still recorded (`same_text_as_recorded` in the job's artifacts), so "has a `text-v3` parse" is a fact about every selected version. A re-parse that raises is `failed`, one without usable text `unsupported`, as for a first parse; the row is final for that parser version.
- **The language** of a re-parse is the recorded one (an adapter's declaration, such as EDGAR's `en`, or the recorded parse's): the language rules did not change between v2 and v3, and the ledger does not keep the declaration apart from the parse. The new parse's own guess is used only when the version recorded none.
- **Evidence Families are unchanged.** A version's family was assigned from its recorded parse, and a re-parse does not re-assign it (families are never merged or moved).
- **`atlas ledger reparse [--company slug] [--parser-version text-v3] [--key K]`** enqueues the job (the key defaults to a timestamped one, so a later run picks up versions recorded since); only the current parser can re-parse (exit 2 otherwise, and for an unknown company). `GET /api/v1/source-versions/{id}` lists the version's `parses` (recorded first) and its `current_parser_version`; `GET .../content?kind=parsed&parser_version=` serves one parse's text (the recorded one without the parameter, as before).
- **Readers choose the parse explicitly; an Assertion never moves.**
  - `assertion.parser_version` (NOT NULL, a statement column, so immutable like the others): the parse the span is in. Every Assertion recorded before `0046` was backfilled with its version's recorded parser version (the review trigger is disabled for that one statement). An insert without one gets the recorded parse's; one naming another must name a parse of that version with text (insert trigger). `POST /api/v1/assertions` takes an optional `parser_version` (default: the recorded parse) and checks the span against that parse only (`no_parsed_text` for a parse the version doesn't have). A quote on a re-parse is a **new** Assertion; an older Assertion stays bound to the parse it was made on and may be superseded through the normal review.
  - **The Investigator's extraction reads the current parse**: the version's `source_parse` row for the current parser when it has text, else the recorded parse. Each passage records its `parser_version` in the stored extraction, a resumed or continued extraction reads the parse its passages name (a passage stored before `0046` names none: the recorded parse), and each Claim (`claim.parser_version`, null before `0046`) and its Assertion record the parse. The passage selection and the checks are otherwise unchanged.
  - **Checked against the Assertion's own parse:** the Relationship review's verbatim-span check reads the Assertion's parse (`source_version_parse` by its `parser_version`), and so does the evaluation runner's span resolution. The proposed-update rules that match "the same span" of two Assertions also require the same parse (offsets in different parses are not the same span). A Research Snapshot's Assertions carry `parser_version`.
- **Which readers use which parse after this ticket:**
  - **The current parse:** the Investigator's `extract_claims` (also inside an investigation's Investigator task).
  - **The Assertion's own parse:** Assertion creation and its span check, the Relationship review's verbatim check, the evaluation runner, the source viewer when it highlights an Assertion (it loads that Assertion's parse and says which parse it shows; a new Assertion made there quotes the parse shown).
  - **The recorded parse, unchanged:** retention (sections, memory documents and their offsets), retention triage and the triage audit, the provenance resolver (memory → Source Version section: memory was retained from the recorded parse), recall and reflect citations, the Skeptic (it reads the versions it chooses in their recorded parse, and its counterevidence Assertions record it), proposed-update detection of a vanished quote in a new version, replay banks, Evidence Families, the Company dossier and the source viewer's default text. With no `source_parse` row, every reader behaves exactly as before.
- **Designed, not built: retaining a re-parse (item 4).** Memory stays on the recorded parse in this ticket. To retain the new parse:
  1. A `reprocess` of the version's sections under the re-parse, as a **new set of memory documents** keyed by the parse (document ID `<version id>:<parser version>:<section anchor>`, or a `parser_version` column on `memory_document` with the ID including it), never an in-place re-retain of the recorded parse's documents, whose offsets other records cite.
  2. Under the Codex budget (Hindsight operations submitted, `codex` provider), pausable like `retain`, backfill class by default, and per company (`atlas ledger reparse --retain`, or `atlas jobs enqueue reprocess_parse`).
  3. Triage first: the new parse's sections inherit the decisions of sections whose text is unchanged (content hash, as for a new version), and only changed sections go to the Triage role.
  4. The provenance resolver resolves a memory document against the parse it was retained from (its `parser_version`), and the old documents are forgotten (Hindsight `DELETE`) only once the new ones completed, so recall never loses a section in between.
  5. Until then, recall and reflect cite the recorded parse, and an extraction's recall hits map to a section anchor, which the sectioner gives identically in both parses of the recorded filings (fix 04's measurement), so passage selection still works on the current parse.
## 2026-09-30: The Skeptic reads the archive: a deterministic fallback (pilot fix 06)

In pilot investigation 1's re-run (0.2.3), the Skeptic searched (10 queries, 59 new leads) but read 0 documents and proposed no counterevidence. Its plan call (`skeptic-plan.v2`, role call `b1948992-…`) was sent a `catalog` of the seed companies' archived Source Versions (both 10-Ks, the 8-Ks and their exhibits, the 10-Qs) with `max_documents` room, and answered good checklist queries and `"documents": []`. So the step was empty by the model's choice, and no code noticed. Its search couldn't make up for it: a lead is never read; only a result that is itself a catalog document's URL is (and none was).

- **Why a fallback rather than trusting the plan.** Counterevidence can only come from archived documents (leads are Tier C and never witnesses), so a Skeptic that chooses none cannot do its job, and the bear checklist's standing items (customer concentration, second sources, dilution) are answered in every filer's own 10-K and 10-Q. A better prompt makes an empty choice less likely but can't rule it out; a rule in code makes "the Skeptic read each seed company's filings" true whatever the model answers, and is visible when it applies. The model's choice still comes first; the fallback only fills what it left out.
- **When.** After the Skeptic's search (its step 2): for each seed company that has a parsed Tier A Source Version available as of the investigation's time, if neither the plan nor the search's catalog matches chose any document of it. (The minimum is therefore "at least one document per seed company that has one", not a count.) A plan that covers every seed company gets no fallback.
