- **What, in reading order** (after the plan's and the search's documents): for each uncovered seed company, in seed order, its latest 10-K and its latest 10-Q (a filing's own document, not an exhibit), or, when it has neither, its latest two primary documents of any form; if none of those is one the investigation already read, also the newest of its documents the investigation did read (free: the Investigators usually read the latest filings, and this keeps the seed company read when they spent the budget). Then the latest 10-K of each of the theme's other companies. XBRL companyfacts is never chosen. New documents count against `max_documents` as before (dropped beyond it, `document_budget_reached`), so the other companies' 10-Ks come only while the budget has room. A follow-up round's fallback leaves out what an earlier round's Skeptic read, as a follow-up Investigator reads only what is new.
- **Recorded.** Each chosen document is `selected_by` `fallback`; the round's `skeptic_documents_fallback` event names the uncovered seed companies, how many documents the plan proposed and how many the fallback added; the Skeptic task's artifacts have `documents_fallback` and `documents_from_fallback`. The passages are then picked by the checklist's cues as for any document.
- **The card says what the Skeptic read.** The research card's `read` gains a row per Skeptic task (`role` `skeptic`, after the round's Investigators): its documents with who chose each (`plan`, `search`, `fallback`) and the sections of the passages it was sent, the passages, whether the fallback applied, and its counterevidence items proposed, accepted and rejected by reason code (in `claims_proposed`, `claims_accepted`, `rejected`); or why it read nothing: skipped with no Claim to challenge, the budget spent, nothing archived, or no passage matching a checklist item. Cards drawn before this have Investigator rows only (`role` defaults to `investigator`). The Editor is still sent only the Investigators' rows; it gets the Skeptic's result as its counterevidence.
- **Prompt `skeptic-plan.v3`** (same response schema): says how the plan is used (queries are searched and their results are leads Atlas never reads; only the chosen documents are read and are Evidence; an empty `documents` list reads nothing), asks for at least one document of each seed company, and names what to choose for the checklist: every seed company's risk factors, the 8-K results releases and their EX-99.1, older filings, the other companies' filings, S-3 and 424B for financing. Whether the model now chooses documents is for the next live run to show; the fallback doesn't depend on it.
- **Amended** ("The Skeptic reads where Memory points", 2026-10-01): it never did (0.2.5: an empty plan once, then queries and no document in four runs), so the plan no longer chooses documents at all. The Skeptic's documents are the ones its own reading pointers lead to; the fallback stays, for each company the Claims name that Memory points at nothing of (its latest 10-K and 10-Q, as here), without the last step (the theme's other companies' 10-Ks); the passages are chosen by the Investigator's selection, not by the checklist cues in text order.
## 2026-09-30: EDGAR full-text search as a discovery channel (pilot fix 12)

**Why a primary-source channel.** Web discovery through the owner's SearXNG returns nothing usable. Probed on 2026-09-30 with the pilot query "InP wafer substrate supplier capacity EML laser": Bing answered with unrelated results (Swedish news, a Swedish health-portal login, YouTube help) for `language` en, en-US, en-GB and all alike; Brave "too many requests"; DuckDuckGo a CAPTCHA; Startpage and Qwant parsing errors; Mojeek and Presearch access denied; Google, Yahoo and Wikipedia no results. Ranking v2 (pilot fix 08) rightly keeps none of it. SEC EDGAR full-text search answers the same research need from primary sources, keyless, under SEC's fair-access rules: `"InP substrates"` in 10-Ks filed 2025-06-01 to 2026-09-30 returns AXT (10-K 2026-03-17), Aeluma (10-Ks 2026-09-16 and 2025-09-09) and Coherent (10-K 2026-08-14). So it becomes discovery's second channel, beside SearXNG, not instead of it.

- **The client** (`atlas.discovery.edgar_fts`): `GET https://efts.sec.gov/LATEST/search-index?q=&forms=&dateRange=custom&startdt=&enddt=` (base `ATLAS_SEC_EFTS_URL`) through the SEC HTTP client (the SEC User-Agent with its contact email, the process-wide 10 requests/s limiter, retries with backoff; 3 attempts and a 30 s Retry-After cap here, so a discovery never waits minutes). `q` is each phrase in double quotes (an exact-phrase match). Fields read (the EDGAR full-text search FAQ, https://www.sec.gov/edgar/search/efts-faq.html, and the answer recorded on 2026-09-30 in `tests/fixtures/edgar-fts/`): `hits.total.value`, and per hit `_id` (`<accession>:<file name>`) and `_source.ciks`, `display_names` (`AXT INC  (AXTI)  (CIK 0001051627)`: name and first ticker parsed from it), `form`, `file_type`, `file_date`, `period_ending`, `adsh`. EDGAR returns no text snippet. Tests never call it: the fake serves the recorded answer at the transport.
- **Settings.** `ATLAS_DISCOVERY_EDGAR_FTS` `auto` (default: on when `ATLAS_SEC_USER_AGENT` is set), `on` (refused without the user agent) or `off`; `ATLAS_DISCOVERY_EDGAR_MAX_HITS` (default 10) documents kept per search (EDGAR pages 100 at a time; the first N in EDGAR's relevance order are kept, `total_hits` records how many matched).
- **Which phrase.** Only an explicit `filing_phrase` is searched: the Scout's schema gains it (`scout.v3`) and so does the Skeptic's plan (`skeptic-plan.v3`), nullable. A web query's keywords are not sent to EDGAR (full-text search matches wording, and keyword soup from a web query finds boilerplate). A phrase with double-quoted parts is several phrases (all must match). The schema sends `filing_phrase` as required (strict mode) while an answer without it, recorded before v3, parses as null.
- **Forms and window.** 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, 20-F, 6-K, 40-F; filing dates from 18 calendar months before the as-of time to it: an investigation's `as_of` (Scout and Skeptic), else now (a `discover` job).
- **Storage** (migration `0050`). `discovery_query.filing_phrase` as written; `edgar_search`, one per query with a phrase while the channel is on (the `q`, forms, dates, status, `total_hits`, kept, new leads, error). Each hit is a lead sighting of that query (`lead_sighting.channel` `edgar_fts`), creating an `edgar_fts` lead per new canonical URL: URL the document in the EDGAR archive, built as the EDGAR adapter builds it (`https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/<CIK>/<accession without dashes>/<file>`), so an ingested filing has the same canonical URL; title `<filer> <form> filed <date>` (`, <file_type>` for an exhibit); published date the filing date; snippet `EDGAR full-text search matched "<phrase>" in this <form> of <filer> (CIK …, period ending …).` (metadata Atlas writes, since EDGAR sends none). `edgar_filing` holds the filing (CIK, filer, ticker, form, dates, accession, document), the researched company with that CIK, the latest archived Source Version of that document, and `ingestable` (a universe company's filing Atlas hasn't archived). The Investigator's document choice is unchanged: it reads the archive; `ingestable` is a pointer for the owner or a later ticket. Leads stay Tier C metadata: nothing is fetched from the archive, nothing is retained.
- **Failures.** A failed EDGAR search is recorded on its search and the rest proceed; a discovery attempt fails only when some search failed and no search of either channel succeeded (the retry reuses the queries). A search that returns nothing is not a failure. So SearXNG answering nothing, or failing, doesn't stop an investigation that EDGAR serves. EDGAR, like SearXNG, never pauses the queue.
- **Ranking.** An EDGAR hit is ranked by ranking v2 against the phrase EDGAR matched (the `edgar_search.q`), not the web query, with the query's purpose; the recorded `investigation_lead.query` is that phrase. sec.gov is neither a demoted host nor a company site, so a filing is not demoted as a company's own page (a company's own website still is).
- **Candidates by CIK.** A filing lead names its company exactly, so `propose_candidates` examines it without the mention extractor (`lead_examination.method` `filer_cik`, no role call): the filer's CIK goes through `resolve_mention` (the universe first, then SEC's submissions and GLEIF by name), and a filer outside the universe becomes (or joins) the Candidate keyed `cik:`, with the lead as its mention (name and ticker as EDGAR displays them). The `discover` job's proposal does this for its filing leads and runs the mention extractor on the rest; an investigation's Scout, which proposed no Candidates before, now enqueues `propose_candidates` with `filers_only` (filing leads only: no LLM call) when entity resolution is configured.
- **The Skeptic** uses the same two channels: its planned queries' filing phrases are searched in its own discovery, and a filing hit on an archived catalog document is read (`selected_by: search`), as a SearXNG result with that URL was already.
- **Not done:** fetching an `ingestable` filing (the Investigator still reads only the archive); a filing phrase inferred from a query without one; EDGAR's own paging beyond the first 100.

## 2026-10-01: TradingView through the cluster's MCP proxy; what the override covers now

The owner put TradingView's hosted MCP server in the cluster behind a read-only proxy on their account (home-ops `kubernetes/apps/llm/toolhive/tradingview-mcp`: ToolHive runs `mcp-remote`, which holds the sign-in; 25 read-only tools; watchlist and alert writes blocked twice) and told the lead: "I've made a broader tradingview mcp server for you to make use off". This widens how the 2026-09-30 override is reached, not what Atlas stores.

- **In the cluster Atlas reaches TradingView only through the proxy** (`http://mcp-tradingview-mcp-proxy.llm.svc.cluster.local:8080/mcp`). The proxy owns the OAuth session, so the Atlas pods hold no TradingView token. On 0.2.5 this is configuration only: `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_ENABLED`, `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_MCP_URL`, and a placeholder `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_ACCESS_TOKEN`, because the client always sends a bearer header; the proxy ignores it. A setting that sends no header replaces the placeholder in a later release. `atlas tradingview login` stays for running Atlas outside the cluster.
- **Atlas still calls three tools:** the document catalog, transcript views and news headlines. Transcripts (earnings calls and conference appearances, transcribed by Quartr) are Tier B Source Versions; headlines are Tier C leads; every stored item records the override. The nightly CronJob enqueues one `tradingview_catalog` job per universe company, backfill class.
- **Why transcripts matter now:** the Serenity method reads what management says about allocation, capacity and qualification, and that is said on calls and at conferences more plainly than in filings. Lumentum alone has 25 transcripts in the two-year lookback (8 results calls, 17 conference appearances). Once retained they are reachable by the reading pointers of the memory-directed reading spec.
- **Allowed next, each needing its own ticket and decision entry:** quotes, fundamentals, analyst consensus and the earnings calendar, as scenario inputs marked `estimated` with TradingView as the basis, and for scheduling. They are never Evidence and never make a Claim.
- **Still out:** technical ratings, the screener, alerts and watchlists. They serve the trading lenses the spec excludes (§1.4) and the Serenity alignment disregarded.
- **Limits:** the account allows about 100 calls a minute, shared with the owner's other agents. Atlas keeps its own pace: at most 0.5 calls a second, 20 calls a job, 200 calls per rolling 5 h.
- **A new sign-in is the owner's act** (the README beside the proxy's manifests). A catalog job that fails with an authorization or connection error fails like any job and blocks nothing else.
- **Verified live on 2026-10-01** from the owner's PC through a port-forward: `tools/list` (25 tools, the names Atlas's client uses); one `mcp-tv-get-documents` call for NASDAQ:LITE; and Atlas 0.2.5's `atlas tradingview check --symbol NASDAQ:LITE` with the three settings (51 documents, 25 transcript views). Not yet verified: a catalog job in the cluster.
## 2026-10-01: Memory as the reading index (memory-directed reading ticket 01)

**Why.** Pilot investigation 1 on 0.2.5 read only its seed companies, an equal share of each document, and asked Memory one multi-clause question per seed company. A recall probe on production (no LLM call) showed that Memory answers the Scout's kind of query, asked across the theme, with the sections the investigation never opened: AXT's 6-inch InP supply agreement with Coherent and its export-permit backlog, Lumentum's allocation statement (`.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md`). So Memory becomes the investigation's index of where to read. This ticket records the index; tickets 05 to 07 read by it.

- **What Memory may do: choose what is read.** After the Scout has its queries, its task asks Memory the round's question and each query: one scoped recall each (`atlas.research`, budget `mid`), scope the investigation's theme (`theme:<slug>`, `any_strict`), so facts from any company of the theme answer, not only the seeds'. A recall makes no LLM call and is counted against no budget (neither Codex nor MiniMax); a round makes at most the query budget plus one (≤ 11). Every recalled memory goes through the provenance resolver, and a **reading pointer** is stored for each Source Version section a **resolved** memory leads to: a world fact to its section, an observation to the sections of the facts it was consolidated from (one pointer per section, the observation's text and rank on each). An unverified or broken memory makes none. A section of a Source Version whose effective `available_at` is after the investigation's as-of time makes none either, so a replayed or back-dated investigation is not pointed at what it couldn't have known.
- **What stays forbidden.** Memory is never Evidence and never a witness (CONTEXT.md), and a pointer changes none of that. A pointer's memory text is never quoted, never becomes or supports a Claim, an Assertion, counterevidence or a finding, and is **never sent to a role as a statement**: no role request or retrieved item carries it (the Investigator's request is unchanged; whether the text helps as a hint is a later experiment with its own measurement). Every Claim is still checked against the archived text it quotes. Nothing Atlas concludes is retained into Hindsight (threat model T3). On the page a pointer's text is labelled Memory, not Evidence.
- **The record** (migration `0051`, `reading_pointer`, **insert-only**: triggers refuse UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE): the investigation, round and task (the Scout's; the Skeptic's in ticket 07), the query (`query_index` 0 for the question, else the Scout query's position, with its `discovery_query_id`, and the text), the memory's rank in the recall's results (from 1, counting unresolved memories too, so it is the recall's own rank), the memory's ID, type and text as returned, the Source Version, the section's anchor, heading and offsets, the company, the `available_at` the as-of check read, and the citation state (always `resolved`; the column keeps the rule visible). A task's pointers are stored in one transaction with the task's counts (artifacts `pointer_recalls`, `pointer_recalls_failed`, `memories_recalled`, `memories_unresolved`, `sections_after_as_of`, `pointers`, `pointers_by_company` by slug), a `pointers_recorded` event (the same counts and `queries_without_pointers`) and one audit event `investigation.pointers_recorded` (actor `atlas-scout`, entity the task, `new_hash` over the rows). One audit event per task, not per pointer: a round can store a few hundred pointers, and the audit chain is appended under a lock. A later attempt of the task finds the counts in its artifacts and asks nothing again.
- **Failures.** Recall is LLM-free but still a Hindsight call. A recall that fails for a reason of its own (an HTTP error that is neither a quota nor an outage, an answer off the contract) is recorded as a `pointer_recall_failed` event naming the query, and the other queries proceed: the Scout succeeds with fewer pointers. A quota or outage failure (HTTP 429/502/503/504, an unreachable server: `classify_failure`) is re-raised before anything is stored, so the task pauses with the queue like any Hindsight-backed job, and the attempt that resumes asks every query again; the recalls run before the leads are taken, so the resumed attempt takes the leads once. A recall that returns nothing is not a failure: the event names the query among `queries_without_pointers`.
- **Read side.** `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` returns `pointers` (by round, query and rank, with the document's title and the company's name); the investigation page lists them by query under the plan. The Research Snapshot of a Hypothesis built on the investigation carries them (`memory.reading_pointers`, the rows as stored; `memory.used` is true when there are any), so a published version says what Memory returned at the time. Snapshots written before this have no such key; the format stays `atlas.research_snapshot.v1` (the key is additive).
- **Coverage caveat.** Recall sees only what was retained: a company whose filings wait in the backfill has no pointer. `GET /api/v1/ingest-plans` shows each company's retained share.
- **Fake, not recorded.** A failed recall was never recorded, so the Hindsight fake gains `fail_recalls(where, status=, times=)`: an unrecorded strict recall whose query matches answers that HTTP status with a hand-written body (only the status is relied on). The fake recalls every in-scope memory whatever the query, so the tests show scope, rank, resolution and the as-of rule, not relevance; relevance is the pilot's to measure.
- **Not done here:** reading by the pointers (document choice, passage selection, Investigators for non-seed companies, the Skeptic's recalls: tickets 05 to 07); reflect inside an investigation; a pointer for an unresolved memory.
- **Changed since** ("The Skeptic reads where Memory points", 2026-10-01): the Skeptic's task records pointers too (`query_kind` `bear_checklist`, with its `checklist_item` and `query_company_id`; migration `0059`), by `atlas-skeptic`; an Investigator reads by the Scout's only.
## 2026-10-01: Claim checks: the filer as the unnamed party, the typographic fold, constraint and ownership cues (memory-directed reading ticket 02)

Pilot investigation 1 on 0.2.5 (`.scratch/pilot/results.md`, "Investigation 1, third run") lost its most direct evidence to two checks and accepted two wrong Claims through two cues: 9 of 15 rejections were `party_not_in_quote` (the NVIDIA purchase-commitment sentence of Coherent's and Lumentum's 8-Ks, proposed four times; Coherent's slide bullets), 2 were `quote_mismatch` over a non-breaking hyphen, an expansion plan was accepted as `capacity_constrained` through "capital allocation", and Lumentum was recorded as owning NVIDIA from a sentence in which it issues shares to NVIDIA. Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (the spec, `.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md`); this supersedes pilot-fix tickets 13, 14 and 19.

- **The filer as the unnamed party.** The party check still asks that the quote name both parties: a company by one of its names (case-sensitive, whole words), or the filer in the first person ("we", "our", "the Company"). New: when the party the quote doesn't name is **the company that filed the document**, the Claim is accepted on these conditions, all deterministic:
  1. the quote is from the filer's own document (the Source Document's company is that party; a third party's report or another company's filing never qualifies);
  2. the quote names the other end: for a company object, the other company by name (never the first person, which could only be the filer); for a product object, the product (`names_object`: the object text whole, or at least half of its particular words);
  3. the quote carries the predicate's cue (the directional-language check, unchanged, and for a product object the cue in the object's clause);
  4. the quote names **no other company** that could be the unnamed party (`atlas.claims.stray_companies`): none of the companies Atlas has, universe or counterparty, besides the Claim's two; and no name written with a corporate legal form (Inc., Incorporated, Corp., Corporation, Co., Ltd., LLC, plc, N.V., S.A., AG, GmbH, K.K.) that is not the named party's.
  Option (a) of ticket 13, for every document of the filer (not only slides and press releases: the sentence that exposed it is an 8-K's body). Recorded on the Claim: `claim.party_basis` `filer`, else `named` (migration `0052`; null on rejected Claims and Claims before it), on `GET /api/v1/claims[/{id}]`.
- **The co-mention rule holds.** Two companies named in one sentence carry no relation without a directional cue (the gate of ticket 10, unchanged), and the filer rule adds no exception to it: a sentence that names two other companies and not the filer proves nothing about the filer ("NVIDIA also entered into a multi-billion-dollar purchase commitment with Lumentum …" in Coherent's document is no Claim about Coherent, with either company as the object: `party_not_in_quote`, the reason naming the other company and "co-mention"); the same impersonal sentence in another company's document is no Claim about a company it doesn't name; and a company that is not the filer is never an unnamed party. **Limits:** an unknown company written without a legal form ("Broadcom supplies …") is not seen as a stray, and an impersonal sentence about a group ("Suppliers may extend lead times for InP substrates") passes these checks if the model proposes it with the filer as subject; for both, the Reviewer's direction and hedge checks are the remaining guard, as for every edge.
- **The typographic fold.** A quote that has no exact occurrence in its passage is placed through a fold of typographic variants, **one character to one character**, so offsets in the folded text are offsets in the archived text:

  | Folded to | Characters |
  |---|---|
  | `-` (U+002D) | U+2010 hyphen, U+2011 non-breaking hyphen, U+2012 figure dash, U+2013 en dash, U+2014 em dash, U+2015 horizontal bar, U+2212 minus sign |
  | `'` (U+0027) | U+2018, U+2019, U+201A, U+201B (single quotation marks) |
  | `"` (U+0022) | U+201C, U+201D, U+201E, U+201F (double quotation marks) |
  | space (U+0020) | U+00A0 no-break space, U+2007 figure space, U+2009 thin space, U+200A hair space, U+202F narrow no-break space |

  Nothing else is folded: not case, not runs of whitespace, not accents, not a word (a quote that differs by a word is still `quote_mismatch`). Order: exact at the model's offsets (`model`), one exact occurrence (`located`), then the folded quote at the model's offsets or at its one folded occurrence (`claim.offset_source` `folded`, added to the column's check by migration `0052`); several folded occurrences are `quote_ambiguous`, and Atlas never picks one. An exact match always wins, so a quote that is exact is never `folded`.
- **The stored quote is the archive's.** Once a quote is placed, the Claim's `quote` and its Assertion's `quote` are the archived text at the span, never the model's spelling (which stays in `claim.proposed`). So "an Assertion's quote is exactly `parsed[span_start:span_end]`" holds as before and `check_quote` is unchanged; the fold decides only where a quote is, never what is stored. The later checks read the archived quote through the same fold (cues, names and object words match "in‑house" and "6‑inch"), and a cue is recorded as the quote writes it. The Relationship review's verbatim and language checks therefore pass on a folded Claim with no change to the review. This supersedes rule 2's "no folding" in "Claim quotes are located, not trusted". Researcher-created Assertions and the Skeptic's counterevidence still need exact text (the Skeptic's own placement is not changed here).
- **`capacity_constrained` needs language of constraint.** Its cues are now: "constrain…", "shortage(s)", an **allocation of supply** ("supply/capacity/product/customer allocation", "allocation of … supply/capacity/output/production/products", "allocate(s/d/ing) … supply/capacity/output/production/products/shipments/inventory", "on allocation"), demand exceeding or outpacing supply ("demand [is/has/…] exceed…/outpac…/outstrip…/outgrew", "exceed…/outpac…/outstrip… [our/current/…] supply/capacity"), "unable/not able to meet/satisfy/fulfill", "backlog(s)", "lead time(s)", "tight/limited supply/capacity", "sold out". The bare stem "allocat…" is gone: "capital allocation", "allocate capital to …" and "purchase price allocation" are no cue, and "expand manufacturing capacity" never was one. The same list serves the Relationship review's language check.
- **Why fix 09's `generic_object` let "manufacturing capacity" through, and the gap closed.** Its generic words were the materials-and-components vocabulary only ("materials", "components", "equipment", "suppliers", …); "manufacturing" and "capacity" were not among them, so the object counted as a particular product. The capacity vocabulary (capacity, capacities, manufacturing, production, output, facility, facilities) is now generic: such an object alone is `generic_object` for a bottleneck predicate, and a quote naming only those words no longer names "EML manufacturing capacity" (`object_not_in_quote`). "6-inch InP manufacturing capacity" and "indium phosphide capacity" stay particular.
- **Direction read from the wording** (`atlas.claims.direction_refusal`, rejection **`wrong_direction`**, after the cue check). `owns` runs from the holder to the issuer: when the quote says shares (stock, securities, warrants) were issued or sold **to** a company, or purchased, acquired, bought or subscribed for **by** it, that company is the holder, and a Claim with it as the object is rejected with a reason naming both directions. Judged only when the wording makes exactly one of the two parties the holder; "X made an investment in Y" is not judged. By the same reading, **the company whose purchase commitment or order it is, is the buyer** ("an NVIDIA multi-billion-dollar purchase commitment", "purchase orders by X"): `buys_from` with that company as the object, or `supplies` with it as the subject, is rejected. This second rule is not in the ticket; it is there because the filer rule would otherwise newly accept the pilot's `buys_from` proposals for the NVIDIA sentence, which name NVIDIA and carry "purchase". "A purchase commitment with X" names no buyer and is left to the Reviewer.
- **Investigator `investigator.v6`** (schema unchanged; `party_basis` is derived, not proposed): "Impersonal sentences and slide bullets" with the NVIDIA sentence (`supplies`, never `buys_from`) and the slide bullet (`manufactures`), the co-mention and third-party limits, "a list item or a sentence fragment that states no relation is never a quote"; `capacity_constrained` "must use language of constraint", with the allocation statement as the example and the expansion sentence as the counter-example; "manufacturing capacity" names no product; `owns` from the holder to the issuer with the share-issuance sentence. The `owns` definition sent in the request says the same. Assertions carry `extractor_version` `investigator.v6`.
- **Not changed:** the layer rules and the Reviewer (ticket 08), passage selection (ticket 05), the Skeptic. Existing Claims are not re-judged.
- **Not measured live.** Whether MiniMax follows v6, and how many `filer` and `folded` Claims a run yields, is for the pilot's re-run of investigation 1 on this release.
## 2026-10-01: Contradiction or bear context (memory-directed reading, ticket 03)

In pilot investigation 1 on 0.2.5 (`.scratch/pilot/results.md`, "Investigation 1, third run"; the evidence is pilot-fix ticket 16), 29 of the Skeptic's 30 items were accepted as counterevidence and none contradicted a Claim: Lumentum's risk factor "for certain components we have sole or limited source supply arrangements" stood as independent counterevidence against Coherent's vertical integration, balance-sheet rows ("Inventories 2,126,823 1,437,636") against capacity Claims, and 12 items named no Claim. The card marked every finding contradicted and the run stopped "4 findings are contradicted". The items themselves were useful (the Editor's open questions used them well); what was wrong was calling them contradictions.

- **Two kinds, never both.** Accepted counterevidence is a **contradiction** of named supporting Claims or **bear context** about a company (`counterevidence.kind`; glossary: Counterevidence, Bear Context). A contradiction says how it contradicts (`how`: it `denies` the Claim's statement, `limits` it, or `dates` it, meaning it held at another time). Bear context is a checklist item, a company and a span, attached to no Claim (`contradicts_claim_ids` empty). Both are Assertions (predicate `counterevidence`, `value_json.kind`), with exact quote spans, so both stay citable.
- **The Skeptic proposes the kind; code decides it** (`skeptic.v3`: `kind`, `how`, `figure_name`, `figure_period` on every item; the schema is strict, so each is required and the last three nullable). A proposed contradiction is kept as one only when it names at least one supporting Claim, says how, and **its quote names that Claim's subject company or its object**: a company by one of its names (display, legal, legal without corporate suffixes; whole words, case-sensitive, as the Investigator's party check), or the Claim's object text as the Investigator's object check reads it (`names_object`: the whole text, or at least half of its particular words). An object text of generic words only ("components", "materials") names nothing.
  - **The filer's "we" is not a name here**, unlike in the Investigator's party check. A risk factor in the subject's own filing ("we have sole or limited source supply arrangements") would otherwise pass against every Claim about that company, which is the pilot's defect. The cost is a real contradiction written wholly in the first person with no object named ("we no longer face a shortage"): it is stored as bear context with the reason, still on the card, and the researcher can see it. A missed contradiction that stays visible is cheaper than a false one that discredits a finding.
  - **Another company's statement** can contradict a Claim when its quote names the Claim's subject or object (a competitor's new capacity "for 6-inch InP wafers" against a shortage of them); a competitor's boilerplate that names neither cannot. This is the rule ticket 16 asked for.
  - A contradiction listing several Claims is kept against the ones its quote names; the others are left out (the item as proposed stays in `proposed`).
- **Demoted, not rejected.** A proposed contradiction that fails those conditions is accepted as bear context, with the reason in `kind_reason` (it names no Claim; it doesn't say how; its quote is a table row; its quote names neither the subject nor the object of the Claims it lists). The quote is still a verified span about a company on the bear checklist, which is what bear context is. The Skeptic task's artifacts count them (`contradictions_accepted`, `bear_context_accepted`, `contradictions_demoted`).
- **Table rows.** A quote that is a label followed only by figures, at least two cells of them ("Inventories 2,126,823 1,437,636", "Diluted 96.2 69.3 87.4 68.8"; `is_table_row`), states nothing by itself. It is accepted only as bear context and only when the item states the figure's name and its period (`figure_name`, `figure_period`, both non-blank); otherwise it is rejected `table_row_without_figure`. A table row proposed as a contradiction is bear context whatever it names. Code doesn't verify the name or period against the table's heading (the heading is often outside the quote's window); they are the Skeptic's reading, shown as such beside the quote.
- **Independence stays a property of contradictions.** A contradiction is independent when its Evidence Family is none of the supporting Claims' (unchanged). Bear context records its Evidence Family and no independence (`independent` NULL): it contradicts nothing, so there is nothing for it to be an independent witness against. Only an independent contradiction marks a finding (`counterevidence_ids`, `needs_review`), counts in the stop detail ("N findings are contradicted by independent counterevidence"), enqueues `check_contradictions` for a published Hypothesis version, or disproves a company premise. Bear context disproves no premise: a `disproves_premise` on it is left out, and `kind_reason` says so.
- **The research card** gains `bear_context`: groups by checklist item (in checklist order) and company (in the order found), each item with its statement, span, figure name and period, and the reason when code made it bear context. `contradictions` now holds contradictions only, each with `how`. The Editor (`editor.v5`) is sent the two lists separately (`contradictions`, `bear_context`, replacing `counterevidence`) with every quote as low-trust data; it writes open questions from both, and its prompt says bear context is no limitation of a finding and never a reason for `needs_review`. The investigation page shows the contradictions table and, on the card, a Bear context section.
- **Hypotheses are unchanged in shape:** a version carries the card's `contradictions` (now only real ones); bear context stays on the investigation's card and isn't copied into Hypothesis versions, the dossier export or the Research Snapshot. The Hypothesis Editor's request is byte-for-byte what it was (`how` goes only to the card's Editor).
- **Migration `0053`:** `kind`, `how`, `kind_reason`, `figure_name`, `figure_period` on `counterevidence`. **Rows recorded before it are backfilled** `contradiction` when they name a Claim (`contradicts_claim_ids` not empty), else `bear_context`, and nothing else about them changes: a backfilled bear-context row keeps the `independent` it was recorded with, `how` stays NULL. The table is insert-only (ENABLE ALWAYS triggers), so the no-update trigger is disabled for the one backfill statement and enabled again. The old rule "accepted = `independent` set" becomes: rejected has none, an accepted contradiction has one, bear context may have either (the old rows) and new rows have none. Research cards are stored JSON and are not rewritten: a card drawn before this lists every accepted item under `contradictions`, as it was shown then. The backfill is by the rule the lead gave, not by re-running the deterministic check: an old row that names a Claim stays a contradiction even where the new check would demote it (as it would the rows of pilot investigation 1 that name a Claim).
- **Not changed:** which documents and passages the Skeptic reads (ticket 07), its plan prompt (`skeptic-plan.v3`), the bear checklist and its cues.
- **Changed since** ("Specific filing phrases; a filer needs a kept hit", 2026-10-01): which phrases are searched, how a filing lead is ranked (version 3: against its query and purpose, not the phrase), and that a filer outside the universe needs a kept hit to become a Candidate.

## 2026-10-01: Specific filing phrases; a filer needs a kept hit (memory-directed reading, ticket 04)

**Evidence.** Pilot investigation 1 on 0.2.5 (`ead2b86e-…`; discovery `ca235cf4-e60c-408e-a8a8-83fc6f43dd43`, `propose_candidates` job `4c4ccf3c-7aa1-5070-961b-1bb19da76a0f`): the Scout (`scout.v3`) wrote a filing phrase for each of its 10 queries, and EDGAR full-text search proposed production's first 16 Candidates. About 5 are on the theme: Aeluma, Veeco, Tower Semiconductor, Axcelis and Semilux, found by `"InP substrates"`, `"200G EML"`, `"EML chip"`, `"epiwafer"` and `"GaAs substrates"`. The rest came from `"VCSEL"`, `"CW laser"`, `"MOCVD"` and `"export controls"`: lidar makers (Hesai, Ouster), medical lasers (IRIDEX), Applied Energetics, Mesa Laboratories, NOVONIX, ECARX and Super Micro; NVIDIA, already a counterparty company, was proposed too. (`.scratch/pilot/results.md` names the phrases and the filers, not which phrase found which filer.) No EDGAR lead made the investigation's top 10, so the Candidates were the channel's only visible output. Two causes: any phrase was searched, and every hit proposed its filer. Ranking v2 could not have told them apart either: it scored a filing lead against the phrase EDGAR matched, and the lead's snippet is Atlas's own sentence naming that phrase, so every hit matched all of its "query" and was kept.

- **Which phrase is searched** (`atlas.discovery.edgar_fts.filing_phrase_skip_reason`). A filing phrase is searched only when it has at least two words (a hyphen divides words: `at-the-market`) or is a product or layer term of the lead-ranking config (`terms`; case and a plural `s` ignored: `VCSELs`). Of a filing phrase's several quoted phrases, one such is enough (EDGAR must match them all). Any other is not sent: the query's `edgar_search` is recorded with status `skipped` and `skip_reason` (migration `0054`; `GET /api/v1/discoveries/{id}`, each query's `edgar`), and the job's artifacts count `filing_searches_skipped`. The rule is the client's (`EdgarFullTextSearch.skip_reason`, with the config's terms), so the Scout's and the Skeptic's discoveries share it. The config's bottleneck vocabulary ("capacity", "supply", "customer", "export controls") moved to its own list, `method_terms`: it scores a lead as before, but it is no product or layer, so `capacity` alone is not searched.
- **How a filing lead is scored** (lead ranking **version 3**). Like a web lead: against the query whose filing phrase found it, and that query's purpose (the recorded `investigation_lead.query` is now that query). EDGAR returns no text, so the lead's title is `<filer> <form> filed <date>` and its snippet Atlas's sentence naming the matched phrases; what is scored is therefore how much of the query's topic the filing is known to contain, plus the filer's name. That alone leaves a one-word phrase under the threshold (36 / the query's topic terms + 3) but not a two-word bare term on a short query, and "CW laser" and "export controls" are two words. So one demotion was added, the smallest change that separates the pilot's phrases: a hit whose search matched **one phrase that is itself a vocabulary term** (`terms` or `method_terms`: "VCSEL", "CW laser", "MOCVD", "export controls") is multiplied by `bare_term_factor` (0.25), with the reason `matched only the vocabulary term "…"`. Every industry that uses the thing writes the bare term; a phrase that says more than a term ("InP substrates", "200G EML") and a search of several phrases (`"CW laser" "silicon photonics"`: both had to match) are not demoted. A web lead scores exactly as in version 2.
- **Which filer becomes a Candidate** (`atlas.candidates.proposals`). A filer outside the universe is proposed only when at least one of its filing leads in the discovery is kept by ranking (at or above `min_score`, 15, and naming a topic term of its query). Otherwise the filer is not resolved (no SEC, GLEIF or OpenFIGI request), nothing is proposed, and its leads stay unexamined, so a later discovery whose query they answer better can still propose it; the `propose_candidates` job's artifacts list each such filer (`filers_below_threshold`: CIK, filer, leads, best score, the query, the reasons). A filer with one kept hit brings all its filing leads of the discovery to its Candidate. A filer in the universe is recorded (`in_universe`) whatever its leads score. No schema change: `lead_mention.outcome` has no new value.
- **A filer that is already a counterparty company** is proposed as a Candidate, once, by the same rule: it is outside the universe, so a kept hit proposes it; the Candidate is keyed by its CIK, so further filings and later discoveries join the same Candidate (also a rejected one: it is not proposed again). That is the promotion path of "Counterparty companies", not a duplicate: the read now says so, `Candidate.counterparty_company_id` (the counterparty with the Candidate's CIK, else its LEI: the row a commit promotes; null once promoted). The pilot's NVIDIA Candidate is this case. A counterparty gets no exemption from the threshold: a bare-term hit proposes it no more than any other filer.
- **The pilot's phrases under the rules** (unit tests `tests/unit/test_edgar_fts.py`, `test_lead_ranking.py`; the pilot's own queries were not recorded in the repo, so the queries are the re-run's and hand-written ones):

  | Phrase | Searched | A hit's score | Proposes its filer |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `InP substrates` | yes | 16.5 on the recorded fixture's query ("indium phosphide substrate capacity expansion 2026") | yes: Aeluma, as before |
  | `200G EML`, `EML chip` | yes | 16.3 on the re-run's "Lumentum 200G EML chip allocation qualified second source 2026" | yes |
  | `GaAs substrates` | yes | 20.4 on "GaAs substrate supply for VCSEL wafers" | yes |
  | `epiwafer` | no: one word, not in `terms` | none | no |
  | `VCSEL` | yes: a product term | 1.8 (bare term) | no: the lidar makers |
  | `CW laser` | yes | 4.1 (bare term) | no: the medical lasers |
  | `MOCVD` | yes: a layer term | 2.2 (bare term) | no |
  | `export controls` | yes | 3.0 (bare term) | no |

- **Prompts** `scout.v4` and `skeptic-plan.v4` (same response schemas): ask for a specific phrase of two to four words naming the query's own product, material or tool; show good (`InP substrates`, `200G EML`) and bad (`VCSEL`, `CW laser`, `MOCVD`, `export controls`) phrases and why; say that a single word is searched only when it is a theme product term; and ask for a second quoted phrase beside a broad term (`"CW laser" "silicon photonics"`).
- **Limits, known.** (1) All hits of one search score alike, apart from the filer's name: the score judges the phrase against its query, not the filing, because EDGAR gives nothing else. A specific phrase still finds off-theme filers (GaAs substrates are used outside photonics); the owner's review of Candidates stays the judge. (2) The threshold is the web leads', and a filing lead shows only its phrase: a two-word phrase of two terms scores 72 / N + 6 for a query of N topic terms that names both words, so it is kept only for N ≤ 8, and a phrase in other words than its query scores less: `InP substrates` on the re-run's "indium phosphide substrate export control China gallium germanium 2026" scores 13.0 and would not propose Aeluma. Such filers are listed in the job's artifacts, not lost; `scout.v4` asks for the query's own words in the phrase. Whether this costs on-theme Candidates is for the pilot re-run to show (compare `filers_below_threshold` with the Candidates). (3) `epiwafer`, which found an on-theme filer in the pilot, is no longer searched; `InP epiwafers` is. (4) Mentions in web leads (the mention extractor) propose Candidates as before, with no threshold.
- **Not done:** a signal about the filer itself (EDGAR's hits carry its SIC code; unused); a threshold of its own for filing leads; adding terms to the config's vocabulary.

## 2026-10-01: A layer only when the quote supports one; an edge is subject, predicate and object (memory-directed reading ticket 08)

In pilot investigation 1 on 0.2.5 (`.scratch/pilot/results.md`, "Investigation 1, third run"; the evidence is in `.scratch/atlas-pilot-fixes/issues/18-layer-not-named-in-quote.md`) 7 of 10 accepted Claims carried a layer their quote doesn't support. Two harms: Coherent's InP device capacity became two `machine_reviewed` `substrate` edges (one fact had edges in three layers across runs), and four right edges (NVIDIA `owns` Coherent among them) went to the exceptions queue with `reviewer_rejected`, `direction_not_confirmed` and `layer_not_confirmed`, because `reviewer.v3` rejected on the layer and the answer was recorded as a failed direction too. Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (the spec, `.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md`, "Layer"); this supersedes pilot-fix ticket 18, "An edge is (subject, predicate, object, layer)" in "Relationships and review", and `investigator.v5`'s "if the quote names nothing of a particular layer, propose no Claim".

- **A Claim's layer is optional, and kept only when the text names it** (`atlas.claims.layer_term`, deterministic). The Investigator may propose no layer. A proposed layer is kept when a **term of that layer** occurs in the object text, else in the quote; otherwise the accepted Claim and its Assertion have no layer. Not a rejection: the Claim is accepted either way. Recorded on the Claim (migration `0055`): `layer` (null when unsupported), `layer_term` (the words that support it, as written) and `layer_reason` `layer_unsupported` (a layer was proposed and dropped; the proposal stays in `claim.proposed`). A proposed layer outside the taxonomy is still rejected `unknown_layer`. Atlas never adds a layer the model didn't propose.
  - **The term table** (`LAYER_TERMS` in `atlas/claims/predicates.py`, the single source; the lead-ranking config's `terms` are one flat vocabulary for scoring leads, not a layer table, so they are not used):

    | Layer | Terms |
    |---|---|
    | `substrate` | substrate, bare wafer, boule, ingot |
    | `epi` | epitaxy, epitaxial, epi, epiwafer, MOCVD, MBE |
    | `chip-laser` | laser, EML, DML, VCSEL, DFB, photodiode, photodetector, PIC, photonic integrated circuit, photonic chip, optical chip |
    | `dsp` | DSP, digital signal processor, TIA, transimpedance amplifier, laser driver, driver IC, retimer, serdes |
    | `module` | transceiver, optical module, module, pluggable, transponder, AOC |
    | `contract-manufacturing` | contract manufacturer, contract manufacturing, electronics manufacturing services, EMS, outsourced manufacturing, outsourced assembly, OSAT |
    | `system` | optical transport, transport system, line system, network switch, ethernet switch, data center switch, switching system, router, server, AI cluster, networking equipment |

  - **A term shared by several layers supports none** (`SHARED_LAYER_TERMS`): InP, indium phosphide, GaAs, gallium arsenide, SOI, silicon photonics, wafer, chip, optics; and the feedstock indium, gallium and germanium, which the taxonomy has no layer for. So "InP substrates" keeps `substrate` (for "substrates"), "epitaxial wafers" keeps `epi`, "EML lasers" keeps `chip-laser`, and "expanding our indium phosphide capacity in Sherman, Texas" proposed as `substrate`, an ownership stake proposed with the question's layer and "germanium" proposed as `substrate` have no layer. No term is in two layers (a unit test checks the table).
  - **Matching:** whole words, through the typographic fold, the last word optionally plural, words joined by spaces or a hyphen ("contract-manufacturers"); a term written in upper case is an acronym and matches only as written ("PICs", never "a pic"), the others whatever their case. A term inside a longer term of another layer is not one: "laser drivers" is `dsp`, not `chip-laser`.
  - **For a product object the quote is read in the object's clause** (the clauses that name the object, as the cue is read since fix 09; the whole quote when it names none of it): in "We continue to expand our global 6-inch InP manufacturing capacity ..., while also operating multiple 6-inch GaAs VCSEL manufacturing facilities", "VCSEL" supports no layer for the InP capacity. For a company object the whole quote is read. **Limit:** a term of the proposed layer anywhere in a company-object quote (or in the object's clause) keeps it, even when the link is about something else in the sentence; the Reviewer's layer check is the remaining guard.
- **The same rule at review, for any Assertion.** `review_relationships` proposes an edge with the Assertion's `value_json.layer` only when `layer_term` supports it, so a researcher's Assertion, and an Assertion recorded before this rule with a layer its quote doesn't name, also form a layerless edge. An Assertion with no layer at all is eligible (it was `not_eligible` `unknown_layer` before); only a layer that is not in the taxonomy is still `unknown_layer`. Existing Claims, Assertions and Relationships are not rewritten: those that have a layer keep it.
- **An edge's identity is (subject, predicate, object).** The object is the company, or the object text casefolded with whitespace collapsed (`object_key`), as before. `relationship.layer` is nullable and outside the identity: migration `0055` replaces the unique constraint on (subject, predicate, object key, layer) by the unique index `uq_relationship_identity` on (subject, predicate, object key).
  - **Existing duplicates are left as they are.** Edges recorded before `0055` that share an identity and differ only by layer are neither merged nor deleted. The migration marks all but the oldest of each such group `legacy_layer_duplicate` (the one thing it writes to an existing row: the index leaves the marked rows out, so it can be unique for every other edge and no new duplicate can be made). **The owner's report:** `GET /api/v1/relationships/layer-duplicates` lists the groups (subject, predicate, object, the layers, each edge with its review state, reasons and counts), read-only. Whether to reject the wrong ones is the owner's (`POST /relationships/{id}/review`).
  - **The layer an Assertion brings** (`atlas.relationships.checks.brings_layer`): its supported layer, unless the Reviewer answered and did not confirm its direction or its layer. An Assertion the Reviewer wasn't asked about (hedged, Tier B) or gave no answer for brings the layer its quote names.
  - **A new edge** takes the layer its first Assertion brings, or has none. **An edge with no layer takes the first layer later Evidence brings** (`relationship.layer_set`; the identity trigger lets the layer change only from null, once), when the edge is still in the exceptions queue or that Assertion passed its machine review: a machine-reviewed or owner-decided edge never gets a layer from a witness that failed.
  - **Evidence that brings another layer than the edge has is a `layer_conflict`, not a second edge.** The Assertion joins the edge, its machine review is recorded `needs_human_review` with the reason `layer_conflict` (and `supported_layer`, the layer it brought), and a `machine_reviewed` edge goes to the exceptions queue (`relationship.layer_conflict`). This is the one case where new Evidence demotes a machine-reviewed edge. An owner's decision stands (the reason shows in `review_reasons`). A conflicted edge is not lifted by a later passing witness: only the owner settles it. The edge keeps its layer; a review can't change a layer.
  - **Legacy duplicates and new Evidence:** it joins the edge of the group that has its layer, else the oldest (with `layer_conflict` when it brings a layer neither has).
- **The Reviewer answers each check on its own** (`reviewer.v4`, a new response schema): `direction` (`as_proposed`, `reversed`, `undirected`, `not_stated`: does the quote state this relation between these parties, and which way), `hedge` (`none`, `hedged`) and `layer` (`correct`, `wrong` with `suggested_layer`, `unclear`, or `not_proposed` for an item with no layer). There is **no overall `verdict`** any more: it was the field through which a doubted layer became a rejected edge. The prompt says the three are separate questions and that a wrong, unclear or missing layer never changes `direction` or `hedge`; the two failure shapes of fix 09 (generic risk-factor language, a cue from another clause) are now `direction: not_stated`.
  - **Reasons:** `direction_not_confirmed` (direction not `as_proposed`), `reviewer_hedged` (new), and `layer_not_confirmed` only when the Assertion has a layer and the answer is not `correct`. For an Assertion with no layer the layer answer is not a reason, whatever it is. So **direction confirmed, no hedge and no layer is `machine_reviewed` with a null layer**, and an unsupported or unconfirmed layer never records `direction_not_confirmed`. An Assertion with a supported layer the Reviewer doesn't confirm is `needs_human_review` `layer_not_confirmed` and brings no layer to its edge. `reviewer_rejected` and `reviewer_uncertain` are no longer produced; reviews recorded under `reviewer.v3` keep them.
  - **Recorded** (`relationship_review`, migration `0055`): `reviewer_hedge`, `supported_layer`; `reviewer_layer` gains `not_proposed`; `reviewer_verdict` stays for the earlier reviews and is null from `reviewer.v4` on ("answered" now means a direction was answered).
- **Investigator `investigator.v7`** (the response schema's `layer` is now `string | null`): give a layer only when the quote names it, with examples per layer; leave it null and still propose the Claim for an ownership stake, a company-level capacity statement and a material several layers share (the Sherman sentence); Atlas drops a layer the quote and `object_text` don't name. Both the Investigator and the Reviewer are sent each layer's terms with the taxonomy (`request.layers[].terms`), so the model knows which words keep a layer. The Editor and the Financial Analyst are sent a Claim's layer as null when it has none (their request models; their prompts are unchanged).
- **Read side.** `Relationship.layer` is nullable. The edge table's `layer` filter takes `none` (the edges with no layer), and sorted by layer those come first, then upstream to downstream. The Company dossier lists a layerless edge like any other. The theme map's `relationships` hold them first; the Theme explorer lists them under each theme company they name ("Relationships with no layer: <company>"), never under a layer, and its by-layer table holds only the edges that have one.
- **Evaluation.** The gold cases are unchanged (they are pinned by SHA-256). Their scripted Reviewer answers are written with `reviewer.v3`'s `verdict`; the script stub turns each into the per-check answer (`uncertain` is a hedge; `rejected` with the direction as proposed is `not_stated`; an item with no layer is answered `not_proposed`). The layer-conflation case (EV-LAY-001) still fails a wrong layer: the `epi` proposal is refused by the Reviewer (`layer_not_confirmed`) and brings no layer, the `substrate` one gives the one edge its layer, and no `epi` edge exists.
- **Not measured live.** Whether MiniMax follows `investigator.v7` and `reviewer.v4`, and how many edges end with no layer, is for the pilot's re-run of investigation 1 on this release. The term table is a first cut from the taxonomy's own words; the pilot's `layer_unsupported` Claims are where to look for missing terms.
## 2026-10-01: Passage selection: pointers and search, alternately (memory-directed reading ticket 05)

Replaces "The passage budget is spread over the documents (pilot fix 10)". Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (the spec, `.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md`, "Passage selection"); this supersedes pilot-fix ticket 15, which keeps the evidence. **Amended the same day by the lead** (first built as "pointers and search, best first", every pointer window before any search window): pointer and search windows are now taken alternately, and results-call transcripts have the floor; see "Two channels" below.

**Evidence** (pilot investigation 1 on 0.2.5, `ead2b86e-3556-42ba-9f3e-08660a938f98`; `.scratch/pilot/results.md`, "Investigation 1, third run"). Fix 10 dealt each Investigator's 24 passages one per document per round, so every one of the 25 documents was read and the best finding was lost:

| Document | Passages | What was read |
|---|---|---|
| Lumentum's FY2026 10-K (Source Version `e256983e-…`, 511,261 characters) | 3 | characters 8,628–13,178 of Item 1 and a tax note in Item 8; the allocation statement ("This demand is outpacing our current supply which has required us to make decisions on supply allocation") is at character 15,113, about 2,000 past the last passage |
| 8-Ks with Items 5.02 (officer changes), 3.02 (share sales), 9.01 (exhibit lists) | 1 each | nothing on the question |
| Coherent's FY2026 10-K | 2 | |
| Coherent's results slides (EX-99.2) | 2 each | |

The archive-search baseline found the same from the other side: all 20 of its hits lay in sections recall resolved to, and the card covered 2 of the 10 on-question ones. Memory finds the right sections; the equal deal read the wrong windows of them.

- **Windows and candidates** (`atlas.claims.selection`, pure code with no database, so the Skeptic can use it in ticket 07). A document's parsed text is cut as before: the retention sectioner's sections, each into windows of at most 3,000 characters at line breaks. A window is a candidate when a selection chooses it:
  1. **`pointer:<query_index>`**: for each of the round's reading pointers into the document (whichever task recorded them), the window of the pointed section that shares most terms with the pointer's Memory text (stopwords dropped, plurals stemmed; the earliest window on a tie, also when none shares a term). The section is found by its anchor in the parse the extraction reads (the current parse; the pointer's offsets are in the recorded parse and are not used); a pointer whose anchor that parse lacks chooses among all the document's windows. A pointer chooses one window, not its whole section: the old `recall` selection offered every window of a recalled section in text order, which is how the 10-K's Item 1 was read from its start and never reached the statement.
  2. **`search`**: every window containing a term of the round's question or of the Scout's queries (the terms of all of them together, each once), scored by Okapi BM25 (k1 1.5, b 0.75) over all windows of the extraction's documents. Outside an investigation the terms are the extraction's own question's; with no question there is no search.
  3. **`entity:<company_id>`**: a window naming a known company other than the document's own, as before. It has no score of its own: it ranks by its search score, 0 when it matches no term.
  4. **`lead`**: only for a document none of whose windows is chosen by the above; its windows in lead order as before (a 10-Q's or 10-K's MD&A or an 8-K's Items 2.02, 7.01, 8.01 first, the cover last).
  A window several selections choose is **one** passage whose `selected_by` lists them all (`pointer:0`, `pointer:3`, `search`, `entity:<id>`). The section-wide `recall` value is retired; extractions stored before this keep it.
- **Two channels, each with its own order; neither ranked above the other** (the amendment). Recall and the term search are independent ways to a window: Memory finds retained facts however they are worded (the spec's production probe found the allocation statement for the focused query "demand outpacing supply allocation decisions laser chips"); the search covers every archived document, retained or not, by its exact words. Scores of the two are never compared.
  - **Pointer candidates**, by their best pointer's rank in its recall (1 is best; then the lower query index, so the question's before a query's; then the earlier document, the documents being newest first; then the earlier window).
  - **Search and entity candidates**, by BM25 score (ties: the earlier document, the earlier window). A pointer window that also matches the search or names a company is in both lists and is still one passage, taken once.
  - Lead windows follow both, by their place in their document's lead order (so lead-only documents take turns).
  **Why not pointers first** (as first built): by the time this was integrated production had gained 43 earnings-call and conference transcripts (provider `tradingview`, parser `tradingview-transcript-v1`, Tier B, document types "Call transcript" and "Event transcript"). They are archived but not yet retained (the Codex budget is held), so they have no memory and no pointer: only the search reaches them. They hold the pilot's best statements ("our EML capacity, which is completely sold out", "the industry-wide constraint in indium phosphide"), and a re-run of investigation 1 took 23 of its 32 accepted Claims from transcripts. With production's 72 passages and pointers plentiful, pointer-first would have read unretained documents only from leftover budget.
- **Dealing the budget** (`deal`):
  1. **The floor.** Each periodic report (the primary document of a 10-K, 10-Q, 20-F, 6-K or 40-F, amendments included), each results release (an 8-K whose filing lists Item 2.02: the 8-K itself and its EX-99.1 and EX-99.2; the Items are in the Source Version's `metadata.items`) and each **results-call transcript** (a Source Document of provider `tradingview` whose `document_type`, the catalog's category title, is "Call transcript", compared without case; a conference's "Event transcript" has no floor) that has a candidate gets its best one: a pointer window, else its best search or entity window, else a lead window. If the budget is smaller than the number of such documents, the best of those candidates are taken.
  2. **Alternately, under the ceiling.** One pointer candidate, then one search or entity candidate, and so on, each list in its order, passing over a window already taken and a document that already has its ceiling (which counts its pointer and search passages together). When one list runs out, the other goes on. With more pointer windows than the budget, about half of what the floor leaves still goes to the search's best windows.
  3. **The ceiling yields before the budget is stranded.** If passages are left (every document is at its ceiling or out of such candidates), the candidates passed over are taken, alternately again. A lone 10-K is still read with the whole budget, and a short filing's unused share goes to the best windows left, as fix 01 and fix 10 passed unused shares on.
  4. **Lead windows last** (under the ceiling, then the rest). So a document with only lead windows and no floor (an 8-K with Items 5.02 and 9.01) gets a passage only when no other document has a pointer, search or entity candidate unread.
  The passages are stored in reading order (the pointer windows taken and the other search and entity windows taken, alternately, each in its order; then the lead windows), so a run whose token budget ends part-way has sent the best of both channels; the continuation sends the rest (unchanged).
- **The ceiling is a setting:** `ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGE_SHARE_PER_DOCUMENT` (`investigator_max_passage_share_per_document`, default one third, 0 < share ≤ 1): one document's passages are at most that share of the extraction's passage budget, rounded down, never less than one (24 → 8, 5 → 1; production's 72 passages per Investigator → 24; a hundredth of a passage is forgiven, so a share written `0.3333` is a third). It applies to every extraction, an Investigator's (`ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_PASSAGES`) and the standalone job's (`ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGES`); 1 turns it off.
- **The Investigator asks Memory nothing.** Its own recall (one company-scoped recall with the whole question, reduced to a set of sections) is removed, inside an investigation and in the standalone `extract_claims` job: the Scout's theme-wide recalls are the pointers, and the standalone job has search, entity and lead selections. `ClaimExtractor` no longer takes a recall; an investigation passes it the round's pointers into the task's documents and the Scout's queries (`Reading`, read by `atlas.investigations.pointers.round_reading`). The Memory text only picks a window: the Investigator's request and its retrieved passages are unchanged in shape, and no role is sent the text. Two opt-in tools that stubbed the recall now use the search selection: `scripts/investigator_replay.py` (so its passages are no longer those `docs/research/investigator-yield.md` was measured on) and the live extraction smoke test.
- **The search code is shared.** The tokenizer, the passage splitter and BM25 moved from `atlas.evaluation.baseline` to `atlas.research.search`; the baseline keeps its names and results (its unit tests pass unchanged), so the pilot's baseline and the selection rank by the same terms. The selection ranks its own 3,000-character windows, not the baseline's 300–1,500-character passages.
- **Recorded, for the pilot's count.** No migration: `claim_extraction.passages` is JSON and only its `selected_by` values changed. The extraction's and the Investigator task's artifacts gain `passages_by_selection` (passages per kind; a passage counts under each kind that chose it). `GET /api/v1/claims[/{id}]` gives each Claim's `passage_selected_by` (its passage's `selected_by`; empty when it names no passage sent), and so does each entry of the investigation's `evidence`. The card's `read` gives each document's `selections` (passages per kind; empty on cards stored before). The investigation page shows both. `passages_dropped` counts the pointer, search and entity candidates not sent. The Research Snapshot's `memory` gains `pointer_selections` (the passages a reading pointer chose, as `recall_selections` listed a recall's; that key stays for extractions made before this and is empty otherwise; the format stays `atlas.research_snapshot.v1`).
- **Measured on the recorded fixtures** (question "Who supplies the lasers in AI data-center optics, and to whom?" and the tests' three Scout queries, budget 24, ceiling 8). Lumentum's four filings with no pointer: 10-K 8, EX-99.1 8, 10-Q 5, 8-K 3 (all their windows with text), and the allocation statement's window is the third passage, by search (fix 10's equal deal: 8, 8, 5, 3 too, but the 10-K's eight were its first windows in text order). With one fact in Memory that paraphrases the statement (the acceptance test, budget 30): its window is the first passage, the only `pointer` one, and the hand-shaped administrative 8-K gets none. Under the Hindsight fake's recalls (a fact for every retained section, every query, so each section's opening window is a pointer window): Coherent's 10-K and 10-Q give 13 pointer windows and 11 search windows, in turn, and the supply-agreement window is among the search's; Lumentum's four filings give 13 pointer and 11 search windows, the 10-K's eight being 5 pointer and 3 search windows (pointers first, as first built: all eight were pointer windows, and the 10-K's search windows were read only from leftover budget).
- **Limits, known.**
  - Half the budget (after the floor) is the pointers' while they last, whatever their rank: rank 40 of a generic recall still takes a turn. A rank cut-off is the obvious next knob; whether Memory's facts or the search's windows yield the accepted Claims is what the pilot measures (`passages_by_selection`, `passage_selected_by`).
  - The alternation is by turns, not by merit: a strong search window waits its turn behind a weak pointer window and the reverse, and the two channels' shares are fixed at about half each while both have candidates.
  - The transcript floor rests on the provider's category title ("Call transcript", as production records it). The synthetic TradingView test fixtures call their category "Earnings call", so a fixture transcript has no floor; another title for a results call needs adding to `RESULTS_CALL_TYPES`.
  - Term overlap can pick the wrong window when the fact's words are spread over the section, and nothing checks the chosen window against the fact. A retained boilerplate section (triage off) gets a pointer window too.
  - The search has no relevance bar either: a window sharing one common word with a query is a candidate, ranked low. Company names and years in a query are terms like any other.
  - The same text in two documents (a 10-Q repeating the 10-K's risk factors) is two candidates; the baseline drops repeats, the selection does not.
  - Among filings only primary documents of periodic forms and results releases have the floor: a 6-K's press-release exhibit has none, and neither have exchange announcements or manual imports (no form).
- **Not done here:** Investigators for the companies the pointers name and document choice by pointer (ticket 06); the Skeptic's recalls and reading (ticket 07; `atlas.investigations.skeptic` still cuts its own windows); sending Memory text to a role.

## 2026-10-01: A company-level constraint needs no named product (memory-directed reading ticket 09)

"This demand is outpacing our current supply which has required us to make decisions on supply allocation." (Lumentum's FY2026 10-K, Item 1) is the pilot's most valuable kind of statement, and the checks lost it: in breadth investigation 5 on 0.2.5 (`.scratch/pilot/results.md`, "Breadth runs on 0.2.5") it was read, proposed as `capacity_constrained` with object "our products" and rejected `object_not_in_quote`; with ticket 02's wider generic-word list it would be `generic_object`. The sentence names no product because the constraint is the company's own supply. Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (the spec's note of 2026-10-01); this amends "Claim precision: the layer from the quote, one clause" (the named-object rule of the four bottleneck predicates) and "Company-level bottleneck predicates" ("each forms a Relationship from the company to a product node").

- **`capacity_constrained` may be company-level; the other three may not.** `atlas.claims.company_level(predicate, object_text)`, deterministic: the predicate is `capacity_constrained` (`COMPANY_LEVEL_PREDICATES`), and the proposed object text names no product: it is missing, or holds only generic words (fix 09's and ticket 02's list: "our products", "manufacturing capacity") and the words of the constraint itself (allocation, available, backlog, business, constraint, current, customer, demand, existing, lead time, overall, own, shortage, total: "our current supply", "supply allocation", "customer demand", "component parts shortages"). Such a Claim has **no object and no layer**. So for this predicate `missing_object`, `object_not_in_quote` and `generic_object` no longer arise from an object that names no product: the object is dropped instead of the Claim. **With a named, specific object the rule is as before** ("EML laser chips" proposed for the allocation sentence is still `object_not_in_quote`; "200G EML lasers" in a quote that names them is a product edge with its layer). `sole_sources`, `vertically_integrates` and `qualified_for` keep the named-object rule: a company-level `sole_sources` is the risk-factor boilerplate fix 09 refuses.
- **Accepted only as the filer's own statement**, in this order, all deterministic:
  1. the subject is the company whose document is quoted (`company_level_not_filer` otherwise: the allocation sentence proposed for Coherent from Lumentum's 10-K, and equally a third party's report that names the company: name the product, or quote the company's own document);
  2. the quote identifies the filer by name, in the first person, or leaves it unnamed (ticket 02's filer rule, here with no other end to name; `party_basis` `filer`);
  3. the quote names **no other company** (`atlas.claims.stray_companies`: a company Atlas has, or a name written with a legal form). The ticket says "no other company as the constrained party"; who the constrained party is can't be told deterministically, so any other company named refuses the Claim: `other_company_in_quote` when the filer is named ("Our contract manufacturer Fabrinet is capacity constrained ..."), `party_not_in_quote` (co-mention) when it is not ("Fabrinet remains supply constrained ...");
  4. the quote carries a constraint cue (ticket 02's list; `no_directional_language` otherwise: "prioritizing investments to expand manufacturing capacity" is still no Claim, with a product or without).
- **Recorded on the Claim** (migration `0057`): `claim.company_level` (boolean, false by default; true only on an accepted company-level Claim, enforced by a check). Its `object_text` and `layer` are null and its `layer_reason` is null (it has no layer because it has no object, which `company_level` says); what the model proposed for them stays in `claim.proposed`. Its Assertion's `value_json` has `object_text` and `layer` null and `company_level: true`. `GET /api/v1/claims[/{id}]` returns `company_level`.
- **The Relationship is the company's own edge with no object and no layer.** `relationship.object_company_id` and `object_text` are both null, and `object_key` is the **reserved empty string**: a product's key is its text (never blank) and a company's its ID, so no other edge has it, and the identity index of ticket 08 (`uq_relationship_identity`: subject, predicate, object key) gives **one company-level `capacity_constrained` edge per company**. Every further company-level Assertion of that company, from any filing, joins it as Evidence (`relationship.evidence_added`); nothing is merged by wording. Migration `0057` replaces 0021's two unnamed checks ("exactly one of company and text", "the key is not empty") by three named ones: at most one of the two (`relationship_object_check`), the key empty exactly when there is no object (`relationship_object_key_check`), and an edge with no object is `capacity_constrained` with no layer (`relationship_company_level_check`), so it can never take a layer. No existing row changes.
- **The same rule at review, for any Assertion** (`review_relationships`): a `capacity_constrained` Assertion with no object text, or one that names no product, proposes its subject's company-level edge when the subject is the company whose document is quoted, and is `not_eligible` `company_level_not_filer` otherwise (before, `missing_object`). The other product predicates without an object are still `missing_object`. The other-company check is not repeated at review (a researcher's Assertion is not party-checked there either); the Reviewer's direction answer covers it.
- **The hedge check is unchanged and decides machine review.** A risk factor's conditional ("we could experience supply constraints"; in Lumentum's 10-K, "our business and customers' businesses are experiencing and could, in the future, experience supply constraints ...") is accepted as a Claim, and its machine review is `needs_human_review` `hedged_language` without the Reviewer being asked. The allocation sentence has a cue and no hedge, so in a Tier A filing it goes to the Reviewer and, confirmed, is `machine_reviewed`. Because the edge is one per company, the existing rules apply across its witnesses: an edge whose first witness was hedged is in the exceptions queue until a passing witness lifts it (`relationship.machine_reviewed`), and a later hedged witness doesn't undo a passing one (its reason stays visible on the edge).
- **Prompts.** `investigator.v8` (v7 kept): "A constraint on the company's own supply": propose it with no object, `object_text` and `layer` null; never fill `object_text` with words that name no product; only from the company's own document, about its own supply against demand, as a fact about now, naming no other company; a risk factor's conditional and an eased past constraint are not it; only `capacity_constrained` may have no object. The `reads` of `capacity_constrained` sent in the request says the same. `reviewer.v5` (v4 kept): "A company-level constraint": the item has `company_level` true (a new request field) and no object on purpose; `direction` is `as_proposed` when the quote says the company itself cannot meet demand with its supply, `not_stated` when the constraint is someone else's or the constraint words are about something else (regulatory constraints, a backlog that is only an order book); `hedge` is `hedged` for a conditional, a plan or a past constraint that has ended; `layer` is `not_proposed`. Response schemas unchanged.
- **Read side.** `Relationship.company_level` (true: no object company, name or text). The edge is in the edge table (`layer=none` holds it; sorted by object it comes first), on the company's dossier (`relationships_out`) and among the theme map's edges, where the Theme explorer lists it under its company with the other layerless edges. The frontend names the missing object "company-level" (`frontend/lib/relationships.ts`: `objectLabel`, `edgeLabel`; the Evidence tray uses the same label for a Claim with no object). A proposed update about a rejected company-level edge names it by subject and predicate. The Editor, the Skeptic and the Financial Analyst are sent an empty object for a company-level Claim; their prompts are unchanged.
- **Limits.** The cue list is wide ("backlog", "lead times", "constraints"), and with no object to anchor it a sentence such as "our backlog at any particular date is not necessarily indicative of actual revenue" passes the deterministic checks if the model proposes it; the Reviewer's direction answer is the remaining guard, and the pilot's review will show how often it is needed. A sentence that states the filer's own constraint and names a customer ("demand from NVIDIA is outpacing our supply") is refused (`other_company_in_quote`); it can still be a `supplies` Claim or a constraint of a named product. An evaluation gold case can't yet require a company-level edge specifically: a gold relationship with neither `object` nor `object_text` matches any edge of that subject and predicate (unchanged; the gold cases are pinned).
- **Not measured live.** Whether MiniMax follows `investigator.v8` and `reviewer.v5`, and how many company-level Claims a run yields, is for the reviewed pilot runs on this release.
## 2026-10-01: Atlas's retains on MiniMax by metadata routing (memory-directed reading ticket 11)

Decided by the owner on the lead's prototype (`.scratch/atlas-pilot-review/issues/18-retains-on-minimax-by-metadata-routing.md`). It amends "No model switching, ever" (queue pause and pacing) in one respect only: which extractor a retain asks for is now a setting the owner switches; Atlas still never changes a model in response to a failure.

**Why.** Memory's intake was limited by the ChatGPT subscription: the shared Hindsight extracts on Codex, a quota shared with hermes, the Codex CLI and LiteLLM's ChatGPT alias, and Atlas rationed itself to 40 operations per 5 h. On 2026-10-01 production had 473 sections retained, 44 retains held by that cap and none of the 43 new transcripts in Memory. The MiniMax subscription is Atlas's own.

- **Metadata routing, not failover or round-robin.** Hindsight (0.10.0 and later) can hold several LLM chain members and a strategy. `failover` and `round-robin` are server-wide: other banks' content (hermes') would sometimes be sent to MiniMax, which the standing rule forbids (`.scratch/atlas-pilot/map.md`, "LLM failover for Hindsight"). The `metadata` strategy (retain only) sends an item to the member its own metadata selects and everything else to the primary, so only the items Atlas marks go to MiniMax, with no dedicated Hindsight. LiteLLM can't do it: Hindsight calls ChatGPT through its own `openai-codex` provider.
- **The key.** With `ATLAS_RETAIN_EXTRACTOR=minimax` every retain item Atlas builds carries `extractor: minimax` in its metadata, beside the provenance keys (`atlas.retention.service.retain_item`, used by retention and by replays; evaluation runs retain through the same jobs).
