The shared server's route is `{"key": "extractor", "value": "minimax", "member": 1}` (home-ops PR #7180: the second chain member, MiniMax-M3 through LiteLLM, and the strategy; inert until a client sends the key). Unset (the default), no key is sent and the request is what it was before. A Hindsight without the route ignores the key, so replay banks on the local Hindsight and throwaway evaluation banks send it too. All retains are routed, interactive and backfill alike: one setting, no per-job choice.
- **What stays on the primary.** Recall, reflect, consolidation and mental-model refresh use Hindsight's primary LLM whatever the items say. Routing chooses the model that extracts a document; it is not a data boundary. So consolidation of the facts MiniMax extracted still spends the ChatGPT quota, uncounted by Atlas as before.
- **A routed item does not fall back.** Verified on Hindsight 0.10.2: when the routed member fails, the item's extraction fails after its retries and its retain operation ends `failed`; nothing goes to the primary, and an unrouted item of the same batch is still extracted. Atlas treats that operation like any other failed one (below).
- **Two extractors in one bank; Atlas records which it asked for.** The research bank now holds facts extracted by Codex and by MiniMax (the bake-off measured MiniMax alone at 16 of 17 expected facts; the two were not compared on the same bank). Hindsight stores nothing about the route, so Atlas does (migration `0060`): `memory_document.extractor` is the extractor asked for when the section was last submitted (a reprocess or a resubmission records its own; null: the primary, and for a linked or never-submitted section and every row before `0060`), and `hindsight_operation.extractor` the same per operation. `GET /api/v1/source-versions/{id}/memory` shows both. It is what Atlas asked for, not proof of what Hindsight did: that is in Hindsight's LLM request log, kept one day. Sections retained before the switch are not re-extracted.
- **Budgets** (`atlas.jobs.budget`). With the extractor set, `retain`, `poll_operation` and `reprocess` are kinds of a new provider, **`hindsight_minimax`**, counted like `codex` in Hindsight operations submitted: `ATLAS_RETAIN_BUDGET_OPERATIONS` (default 200) per rolling window, with the same interactive reserve, backfill rule and `poll_operation` exemption. `codex` then holds `reflect`, `refresh_mental_model` and `replay` only (a replay's operations stay `codex`: it runs on the local Hindsight). Unset, the mapping and the queue view are what they were, and `hindsight_minimax` isn't listed. The name says whose quota (MiniMax) and through what (Hindsight), apart from `minimax`, the role calls' token budget: both spend the MiniMax subscription, but Atlas sees tokens only for its own calls and operations only for Hindsight's, so they can't share a unit.
  - **An operation counts against the budget of the extractor it asked for**, not against whatever the setting is when the queue counts it. Counting by the setting would have re-counted every recorded operation under the other provider, as spent now, the moment the setting changed. So switching the setting on or back never counts an operation twice, and operations before `0060` stay `codex`.
  - 200 is a first value, not a measurement: the prototype's batches took 12 to 21 seconds, and what a MiniMax window bears beside the research roles is for the owner to tune (`docs/runbooks.md`).
- **Failures.** A routed operation's error is the member's, wrapped by Hindsight (`RuntimeError: Fact extraction failed: 1/1 chunks failed. First failures: chunk 0: …`). A relayed rate limit (`RateLimitError: Error code: 429 … litellm.RateLimitError`) already classified as `quota`. The prototype's failure, `BadRequestError: Error code: 400 … There are no healthy deployments for this model`, is LiteLLM having no deployment to serve the model (all cooling down, or the route misconfigured); it was `permanent` and is now `unavailable`, as is "no deployments available". Either pauses the pausable kinds with backoff, leaves the sections `pending` and resubmits them, still asking for the extractor in force then. A relayed 502/503/504 was and is `unavailable`; a relayed 500 stays `permanent`, as before (it can be the document's fault). Zero-fact and permanently failed sections are handled as before.
- **Not verified.** A chain whose primary is `openai-codex` (it can't run locally); the route on the cluster (PR #7180 isn't merged); MiniMax's extraction against Codex's on the same sections. Atlas's side is tested against the recorded fake only, which accepts any metadata and routes nothing.
## 2026-10-01: Multi-hop Investigators: the plan grows after the Scout (memory-directed reading ticket 06)

Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (the spec, `.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md`, "The plan grows after the Scout" and "Document choice"). It amends "The plan is fixed and visible from the start" in "Investigation orchestration" and "One Investigator task per seed company": the plan is still visible from the start, and it now grows once per round.

**Evidence.** Pilot investigation 1 asked about InP feedstock with Coherent and Lumentum as seeds, so only their filings were opened. The spec's recall probe (production, no LLM call) shows Memory answering the Scout's kind of query, asked across the theme, from AXT's filings: its 6-inch InP supply agreement with Coherent and its backlog of substrate orders waiting for export permits. AXT is in the universe and was never read. Ticket 01 records where Memory points; this ticket reads the companies it points to.

- **Seeds are where the reading starts, not where it ends.** Every seed company has an Investigator, as before, whatever Memory says. When a round's Scout task has succeeded (its reading pointers are stored), the companies the pointers name are ranked, and each **researched** company that is not a seed gets an Investigator, in rank order, while the company budget has room.
- **The ranking weight** (`atlas.investigations.companies.rank_companies`, pure). A company's weight is the sum, over the round's Scout pointers that name it, of **1 / rank**, the pointer's rank being its memory's place in its recall's results (1 is the top). So one pointer at the top of a recall weighs 1, one at rank 10 a tenth; five pointers at rank 10 (0.5) lose to one at rank 1, three at rank 2 (1.5) beat it, and a company that several queries point to adds its pointers up. Ties go to the company with the better best rank, then by slug. It is the simplest weight that is "count weighted by rank"; it has no cut-off and no per-query normalisation, and is recorded per company (`pointers`, `score` to four places, `best_rank`) so the pilot can judge it.
- **Who is ranked.** The company of each pointer (the pointed Source Document's company), when its role is `researched`. A **counterparty** is never ranked and never gets an Investigator: it has no archive (it can't be ingested or imported, so no pointer names it), and the ranking filters on the role besides. Memory naming a company in its text counts for nothing: only where a pointer leads does. A pointer with no company names nobody. The ranking is not limited to the investigation's theme config: the pointers come from theme-scoped recalls, which is the limit.
- **The company budget.** `max_companies` Investigators a round, **the seeds counted**: setting `ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_COMPANIES` (`investigation_max_companies`, default 6, 1–25), which a request may lower or set (`budgets.max_companies`, 1–25, like the other budgets); stored on the investigation (`investigation.max_companies`, migration `0058`; investigations recorded before it take 6). A seed is never dropped: a budget below the number of seeds only adds nobody (the default seeds are the theme's 12 companies, which fill it). The added Investigators share the round's document budget with the seeds', split as before (each task holds back an equal share for those that haven't chosen yet), and each has its own passage budget (`ATLAS_INVESTIGATION_MAX_PASSAGES`), so a round with six Investigators can spend three times the extraction tokens of a two-seed round: the run's token budget is the bound, and a `budget_exhausted` stop is resumable as before.
- **A company whose premise was already disproven** (a follow-up round, or the researcher disproving it while the Scout runs) is ranked, gets no Investigator and takes no room (`premise_disproven`); a seed in that state keeps its task, which is cancelled at once, as before.
- **How the plan grows** (`Investigations._grow`, called at the top of `advance`, under the investigation's lock). The first advance after the round's Scout has succeeded, which is the one in the transaction that records the Scout's outcome, and so before any Investigator is enqueued. For each company to add: a `company:<slug>` premise (created unless an earlier round left one), and an `investigator:<slug>` task depending on the Scout, with premises `question` and `company:<slug>` and artifacts `added_for_pointers` (`pointers`, `score`, `best_rank`). The new tasks take the positions after the round's other Investigators, in rank order; the later tasks move down, and every task that waits for an Investigator (the Skeptic, the Financial Analyst, the Editor) now waits for all of them, the added ones included. Then `advance` goes on as before: it enqueues every Investigator whose dependencies are done. No migration for the task table: an added Investigator is a row like a seed's.
  - **Once per round.** The Scout task's artifacts record the ranking (`pointed_companies`: each ranked company with its weight, its `outcome` `seed`, `added`, `no_room` or `premise_disproven`, and its `task_key`; `company_budget`; `investigators_added`; `companies_not_read`), and their presence is how a later advance knows the round's plan has grown. So a **resume** changes nothing (a budget-exhausted Scout grows the plan when it succeeds after the resume; a resume later in the round finds the ranking recorded), and a **follow-up round** grows the same way from its own Scout's pointers, which are its own question's: a company added in round 1 is in round 2 only if round 2's pointers name it and there is room. One `companies_ranked` event per round (no task key; `max_companies`, `investigators`, `added`, `not_read`, `companies`) records it in the log, also when nothing was added.
  - **An investigation running across the upgrade** keeps its plan: the plan grows only while every task after the Scout is still pending (or cancelled or skipped).
  - A Scout that fails or is cancelled grows nothing: the round has no pointers to rank.
- **Document choice** (`TaskRunner._documents`). An Investigator, a seed's or an added one, takes first the Source Versions the round's Scout pointers into its company name: one per Source Document (the latest version a pointer names), by that version's best pointer (rank, then the question before a query), then, as before, the latest parsed version of each of its other Source Documents, newest first; all available by the as-of time, none already read by the investigation, up to its share of the document budget. Its artifacts count `documents_pointed`. So with room for one document, an Investigator reads the older filing Memory points to rather than the newest one. **Limit:** when a pointer names a version that is no longer the latest of its document (the newer one is not retained yet), the pointed version is read and the newer one is not, this round: the pointer's section and window are in the version Memory was made from.
- **What does not change.** The seeds (`seed_company_ids`) are the request's: the Financial Analyst still proposes scenario inputs for the seed companies an accepted Claim names, a Hypothesis's related companies are still the seeds, and the Skeptic's code fallback is still for the seed companies (its reading is ticket 07). An added Investigator's accepted Claims are the investigation's like any other: in the Evidence tray, sent to the Skeptic, the Analyst and the Editor, reviewed into Relationships after the stop. No prompt changed and no role request changed; no Memory text is sent anywhere new.
- **Read side.** `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}`: `budgets.max_companies`, `usage.companies` (the current round's Investigators), `pointed_companies` (every round's ranking, best first: company, `pointers`, `score`, `best_rank`, `outcome`, `task_key`), the added tasks among `tasks` (with `added_for_pointers`) and their premises among `premises` (the question, the seeds in order, then the added companies'). The research card's `read` has a row per Investigator, the added ones included, and the card gains **`not_read`**: the pointed companies that got no Investigator, with their pointers and the reason ("the company budget (N Investigators a round) had no room", "its premise was disproven"), so the next investigation can seed them. It is written by code and not sent to the Editor (its request is unchanged). The investigation page shows the budget row, a note on each added Investigator in the plan, a table of the companies the pointers name under "Reading pointers", and "Companies not read" on the card.
- **Tested with a hand-shaped document, not a recorded one.** The recorded EDGAR fixtures hold filings of Coherent and Lumentum only (Nokia's are XBRL facts), and neither company's filings state a relation to the other, so no recorded filing of a third universe company can give the acceptance test its edge. The test's AXT document is a hand-shaped note, labelled so (`tests/fixtures/investigations/axt-supply-agreement.txt`), stating the probe's fact (a supply agreement with Coherent for 6-inch InP substrates) beyond its first 3,000 characters; the Memory text is the test's paraphrase (`script_fact_text`). The ticket-01 test, on the recorded filings, shows Lumentum gaining an Investigator that reads its recorded filings when Coherent alone is the seed.
- **Limits, known.**
  - The weight is a first cut: no rank cut-off (a company with one pointer at rank 40 is still ranked, and read if there is room), no normalisation by how much of a company is retained (a company with many retained sections collects more pointers), and the question's recall counts like a query's.
  - Recall sees only what was retained: a company whose filings wait in the backfill has no pointer and is not added (`GET /api/v1/ingest-plans` shows each company's retained share). The term search covers unretained documents of a chosen company, not unchosen companies.
  - The document budget is not raised with the companies: six Investigators share the 25 documents (at least four each), so a seed reads fewer documents than in a two-seed round.
  - The Hindsight fake recalls every in-scope fact for every query, in retain order, so the tests show the ranking's arithmetic, the budget, the plan and the reading, not relevance.
- **Not measured live.** Whether real recalls rank AXT above the other non-seeds for the pilot's question, and what the added Investigators cost in tokens and yield in Claims, is for the pilot's re-run of investigation 1 on this release (`pointed_companies`, each added task's `accepted`).
- **Not done here:** the Skeptic reading by pointers (ticket 07); raising the document budget; a rank cut-off; proposing a pointed company outside the universe (pointers only reach archived documents, so there is none).
## 2026-10-01: The Skeptic reads where Memory points (memory-directed reading ticket 07)

Amends "The Skeptic reads the archive: a deterministic fallback (pilot fix 06)" (the fallback stays, per company; the plan no longer chooses documents) and the Skeptic's part of "Memory as the reading index". Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (the spec, `.scratch/atlas-memory-directed-reading/spec.md`, "The Skeptic"); this supersedes pilot-fix ticket 17, which keeps the evidence.

**Evidence.** The Skeptic's plan call never chose a document on production. On 0.2.3 `skeptic-plan.v2` wrote 10 queries and `"documents": []`; on 0.2.5 `skeptic-plan.v3` answered `{"queries": [], "documents": []}` in investigation 1 (role call `21b98d30-…`) and 10 queries with no document in each of the four breadth runs (`.scratch/pilot/results.md`, "Breadth runs on 0.2.5"). So everything the Skeptic read came from the fallback: the latest 10-K and 10-Q of the seed companies, and of those the first checklist-matching windows in text order, whatever the Claims were about.

- **The rule changes from "No Memory is sent or read" to "no Memory is sent to the Skeptic; Memory chooses what it reads".** Before its plan call, the Skeptic's task makes one scoped recall per bear-checklist item for each company the supporting Claims name, and what resolves is stored as the task's reading pointers. A pointer is used exactly as an Investigator's: its Memory text finds a window of the pointed section and goes no further. **What stays forbidden** is everything "Memory as the reading index" forbids: no role request or retrieved item carries Memory text (the Skeptic's requests are the plan's fields and passages of archived Source Versions), Memory is never a witness (an item citing anything but a passage sent in that call is still rejected `not_a_witness`), and nothing the Skeptic finds is retained into Hindsight.
- **Which companies.** The companies the supporting Claims name: each Claim's subject, and its object company when that is a researched universe company (`company.role = 'researched'`). A counterparty is never asked about: it has no archive. Order: the seed companies among them first, in seed order, then the others in the Claims' order. The supporting Claims are the ones the Skeptic is sent (the run's accepted Claims, every round's, minus those under a disproven premise), so a follow-up round's Skeptic asks about the earlier rounds' companies too.
- **The query is deterministic text** (`atlas.roles.skeptic.bear_query`; no LLM call): `<company's display name>: <the item's words>; <object>; <object>…`. The item's words are a keyword phrase per checklist item (`ChecklistItem.recall`: "customer concentration, largest customers, share of revenue from a few customers"; "second source, alternative or additional qualified suppliers, sole or single source supply"; …), written as the spec's recall probe found Memory answers best: focused terms, not a sentence. The objects are those of the Claims that name the company: for its own Claims the other party's name (or the object text) and the product; for a Claim it is the object of, the subject's name and the product; each once, in the Claims' order, at most 4. Example: `Coherent: customer concentration, largest customers, share of revenue from a few customers; NVIDIA; advanced lasers`.
- **Scope and bound.** Each recall has the investigation's theme as its scope (`theme:<slug>`, `any_strict`), like the Scout's, so another company's filing that names the company can answer. At most `MAX_RECALL_COMPANIES` (6) companies are asked about, each for the 6 checklist items: **at most 36 recalls per Skeptic task** (the Scout makes at most 11). Recall makes no LLM call and is counted against no budget; each recalled memory is resolved to its section through Hindsight, which is the cost. The task's artifacts say how many companies were asked about and how many the cap left out (`recall_companies`, `recall_companies_dropped`), beside the pointer counts (`pointer_recalls`, `pointers`, `pointers_by_company`, …). The recalls run first, before any LLM call, so a Hindsight quota or outage pauses the task with nothing spent; a recall that fails for its own reason is a `pointer_recall_failed` event and the others proceed (the Scout's rules, the same code: `record_pointers`).
- **The record** (migration `0059`, three columns on `reading_pointer`, which stays insert-only): `query_kind` (`scout` for the question and the Scout's queries, the default for rows recorded before; `bear_checklist` for a Skeptic's), and for a bear-checklist query its `checklist_item` and `query_company_id` (the company asked about, which need not be the company the pointer leads to, `company_id`). A check constraint ties them together. A Skeptic's `query_index` is the query's position among its task's queries, from 1 (companies in order, each in checklist order); with `task_id` it identifies the query, and it is never read as a Scout query's number: `atlas.investigations.pointers.round_reading`, which an Investigator reads by, returns only `query_kind = 'scout'` pointers (before this it returned every pointer of the round, whichever task recorded it); the Skeptic's are read by `skeptic_pointers`. The audit event `investigation.pointers_recorded` of a Skeptic's task is by `atlas-skeptic`.
- **Documents**, in reading order, each once, with who chose it:
  1. `pointer`: the Source Versions the task's pointers lead to, best pointer first (its rank in its recall, then the earlier query), each for the checklist item of its best pointer. **Only a parsed Tier A version is followed**: the Skeptic's witnesses were Tier A archived documents (its catalog), and this ticket doesn't widen that; a pointer into another tier's document (a transcript, once retained) is stored and not read by the Skeptic.
  2. `search`: a search result whose canonical URL is a catalog document's, as before (the catalog is now only that: the latest parsed Tier A version of each document of the investigation's and theme's companies).
  3. `fallback`: for each company the Claims name (all of them, also beyond the recall cap) that **no pointer document was chosen of** and that has a document archived: its latest 10-K and latest 10-Q (its latest two primary documents when it has neither), and, when none of those is one the investigation already read, the newest document of it the investigation read (free of budget), as fix 06 chose them. A company whose filing only the search matched still gets its 10-K and 10-Q: the standing checklist items are answered there. Fix 06's last step (the theme's other companies' latest 10-Ks while the budget has room) is gone: the theme-wide recalls reach them. The `skeptic_documents_fallback` event now names `company_ids` (it had `seed_company_ids` and `plan_documents`) and how many documents were added; the task's `documents_fallback` and `documents_from_fallback` are as before, with `documents_from_pointers` beside `documents_from_search`.
  A document the investigation hasn't read yet counts against `max_documents` as before; a follow-up round's pointer and fallback documents leave out what an earlier round's Skeptic read (a whole document, not its unread windows: a known limit).
- **Passages** come from `atlas.claims.selection.select`, the Investigator's selection (the Skeptic's own copy of the window cutter and its round-robin over checklist cues are gone): the windows the pointers lead to, by rank, and the windows a term search ranks highest, by BM25 score, alternately, within `ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGES`, with the floor (each periodic report and results release keeps a passage) and the ceiling per document (`ATLAS_INVESTIGATOR_MAX_PASSAGE_SHARE_PER_DOCUMENT`); lead windows only for a document with neither. **The search's terms are the bear-checklist queries'** (the companies' names, the items' words and the Claims' objects, all of them together); there is no question term and no entity selection. So a fallback document, which has no pointer, is read at the windows that match the checklist's words best, not at its first windows; and a window no longer needs to match a checklist cue to be read. Each passage records every selection that chose it: `pointer:<checklist item>` (the selection's `Pointer` gained an optional `label`, so a Skeptic passage's tag is `pointer:customer_concentration`, never `pointer:6`), `search`, `lead`. The checklist cues remain as the reading request's `checklist_items` hint (the items whose cues the passage's text matches, now possibly none); the reading prompt (`skeptic.v3`) and its request are unchanged.
- **The plan keeps the web and EDGAR queries** (`skeptic-plan.v5`; v4 stays in the repo). Its request no longer carries the `catalog` or `max_documents`, and its answer is `{"queries": […]}`: `documents` is gone from the strict schema, so an answer that still has it is off the schema. The prompt says what the queries are for (leads, and sources the archive lacks) and asks for at least one per checklist item the Claims touch. An empty answer searches nothing and changes nothing else; an answer quarantined after its repair is treated the same (`skeptic_plan_quarantined` event with the role call; before, it failed the task), because the plan no longer decides whether the Skeptic reads.
- **Independence is unchanged.** A contradiction is independent only when its Evidence Family is none of the supporting Claims'. Memory pointing at the same filing the Investigator quoted changes nothing about that: such a contradiction is recorded and not independent.
- **Read side.** `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}`: `pointers` include the Skeptic's (`task_key` `skeptic`, `query_kind`, `checklist_item`, `query_company_id`, `query_company_name`), after the Scout's of the round; the investigation page lists them under "The Skeptic's", one group per checklist item and company. The card's Skeptic `read` row gives each document's `selected_by` (`pointer`, `search`, `fallback`; `plan` on cards stored before) and, like an Investigator's, its `selections` (passages per kind). The Skeptic task's artifacts gain `passages_by_selection`. The Research Snapshot's `memory.reading_pointers` carry the three new columns and `memory.skeptic_pointer_selections` lists the Skeptic's passages a pointer chose (additive; the format stays `atlas.research_snapshot.v1`).
- **Fixtures.** The recorded Coherent and Lumentum 10-Qs hold a cover and financial statements only, so the acceptance test's customer-concentration memory is derived from the recorded Coherent **10-K**'s risk factors (Item 1A, its fifth window), not a 10-Q. The Hindsight fake recalls every in-scope memory whatever the query, so under it every checklist item of every company points at every retained section; the tests show the wiring (scope, storage, where a pointer leads, the fallback), not relevance. Tests that need one document read and not another shape Memory with the fake's `report_zero_facts` and `script_fact_text`; the Evidence Family test's byte copy of the 10-K has no fact of its own, so the Skeptic reaches it through a hand-shaped search result (the mirror's URL) instead of a plan.
- **Limits, known.**
  - Whether real recalls of these keyword queries return bear-checklist facts, and at what rank, is unmeasured: the pilot's re-run shows it (`pointers` by checklist item, `passages_by_selection`, the bear context found).
  - Theme scope means a recall about Coherent can return only other companies' facts; Coherent is then read through the fallback, at the search's best windows.
  - 36 recalls resolve up to 36 × the recall's result size memories; on the shared Hindsight that is the Skeptic's load. The cap is a constant, not a setting.
  - A pointer window is read whatever its rank, half the passage budget while pointers last (the selection's known limit).
  - A follow-up round's Skeptic skips whole documents an earlier round's Skeptic read, also when new Claims would point at windows it did not read.
  - Tier B documents (call transcripts) are not read by the Skeptic.
- **Not done here:** Investigators for the companies the pointers name (ticket 06); sending Memory text to a role; reflect inside an investigation.

## 2026-10-02: A cut-off answer is not a schema failure; the card never goes missing (memory-quality ticket 16)

Decided by the lead under the owner's delegation (`.scratch/atlas-memory-quality/issues/16-the-editors-card-fits-a-multi-hop-investigation.md`); a production blocker, released alone as a patch. It amends "repair then quarantine" in the role-call entry and the research card's Editor.

**Evidence.** Investigation `fcb1ef32-…` on 0.3.0 (six Investigators, 89 accepted Claims) ended with no card: the Editor task failed after 3 attempts, and each of its six answers (role calls `a6313db9-…`, `60b0c792-…`, `4e76a562-…`) used exactly 4,096 output tokens and stopped inside a list of Claim IDs ("Invalid JSON: EOF while parsing a string"). The repair was sent with the same cap and was cut the same way. The request was about 38,000 tokens; investigations before 0.3.0 had 10 to 31 accepted Claims.

- **Truncated is its own outcome, for every role.** When an answer fails validation and the model stopped at the output cap (the completion's `finish_reason` is `length`, or its `completion_tokens` reach the request's `max_tokens`), the role caller records the call `truncated` (migration `0061` widens `role_call.status`) and raises `RoleOutputTruncated`; no repair is sent, since it would be cut at the same cap. An answer that validates is used whatever its token count. `RoleOutputTruncated` is a kind of `RoleOutputQuarantined` (its output is never used), so every role's existing failure path handles it unchanged: the Investigator's extraction, the Reviewer, triage and its audit, the Skeptic still catch it where they caught a quarantine, and a Scout or Financial Analyst attempt fails as before. Only the status, and the repair no longer sent, differ. `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}/role-calls` shows `truncated`. The other roles keep their caps.
- **The Editor's cap follows the work.** The research card's Editor calls with the role's cap (now 8,192); a truncated answer is asked once more with the cap doubled, up to `ATLAS_EDITOR_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (default 16,384, the bound). The caller may give one call a cap of its own (`call_recorded(..., max_output_tokens=)`); each request's `max_tokens` is still the smaller of that cap and the run's token budget left.
- **Claims are cited by a short reference** (`editor.v6`). The Editor is sent each accepted Claim as `ref` `c1`, `c2`, … in the request's order (the claims' quotes in `retrieved_data` carry the same `id`), a contradiction names its Claims by reference (`contradicts_refs`), and a finding cites `claim_refs`. Code maps each reference back to its Claim; a finding citing a reference it wasn't sent is dropped as unsupported exactly as an unknown Claim ID was ("cites what isn't an accepted Claim of this investigation: c99"), its `claim_ids` recording the IDs of the references it was sent and the rest as written. A 36-character ID per citation was most of what overflowed. The Hypothesis draft (`editor-hypothesis.v3`) has its own request and is unchanged: it still cites Claim IDs. Only the short form is accepted (not both): every scripted Editor answer in the tests, the evaluation stub, the e2e seeds and the live-verify rehearsal was moved to it.
- **An investigation never ends card-less.** If the Editor is cut off at the bound too, or quarantined, the Editor task still succeeds with a card that is code's alone: no finding, `editor_verdict` `needs_review`, `editor_failure` (the reason), `editor_role_call_id` the failed call, `claims_by_company` (each subject company's accepted Claims: predicate, object, layer, Source Version), and, as on every card, the contradictions, bear context, `searched`, `read` and `not_read`. The investigation stops `needs_review` ("the Editor failed, so the card has no finding: …"; `no_new_independent_evidence` when there was no accepted Claim, as before). The Evidence tray is unchanged. The investigation page shows the reason and the Claims by company. A Hypothesis can't be saved from such a card (it has no finding), as with any card without one.
- **Not covered.** An Editor failure of another kind (an HTTP error that is neither quota nor outage) still fails the attempt and, after the job's attempts, the task, with no card, as before; quota and outages still pause. The cap doubles once; a request so large that 16,384 tokens of short references overflow ends with the fallback card.
- **Not measured live.** Whether MiniMax cites `ref`s faithfully, and how many output tokens a card for 89 Claims takes, is for the re-run of investigation 1 on this patch.
## 2026-10-02: Hindsight 0.10.2 recorded (memory-quality ticket 01)

Evidence: `docs/hindsight-feature-matrix.md` (headed 0.10.2), recordings under `spikes/hindsight/recordings/` and `rerun-0.10.2/`. What changes the memory-quality spec's design:

- **The backfill cannot rely on "same document ID, so Hindsight replaces the document's memories".** A retain of unchanged content under the same `document_id` re-extracts nothing (0 LLM requests: unchanged chunks are skipped by content hash, also in `replace` mode); the stored facts keep their old context and get none of the newly given entities; only their tags are replaced. `reprocess` takes no body and replays the stored retain parameters (it picks up bank-config changes only, such as `entity_labels`). So a section is brought to a new retain profile (context, entities) by **deleting its document and retaining it again**; that path is expected from the docs, not recorded, and the backfill ticket checks it on one document before running. Explicit scopes sent with a same-content retain were the ones the next consolidation used in a bank with nothing consolidated yet; re-scoping facts consolidated before is untested.
- **Given entities don't merge a company's names.** With `resolve_entities: false` Hindsight adds the given names to every fact of the item as written; the extractor's short forms ("Aurora", "Aurora Optics") stay entities of their own. The entity hop (spec, "The entity hop") lists facts by the entity of the canonical name Atlas sends, which every fact retained at the new profile carries; facts retained before carry only extracted names until re-retained.
- **Explicit observation scopes cost one consolidation request per scope.** Consolidation makes one LLM request per scope a run touches (one-scope item: 1; two-scope item: 2), on the cluster's primary (Codex) chain. A company scope plus a theme scope doubles consolidation for a section; the backfill's pacing and the `codex` budget must count it.
- **An observation's sources come back only with `include.source_facts`** (`source_fact_ids` is null otherwise); with it they are in the same answer, as the spec assumes.
- **A chunk is a verbatim slice of the retained content**, without offsets in the API: chunk-exact pointers place a chunk by searching the section text in `chunk_index` order. Chunks are cut at paragraph breaks and the separators between them belong to no chunk.
- **Reflect citations of world facts now carry `document_id`, `chunk_id`, `metadata` and `tags`** (0.10.2); an observation is still resolved through its sources.
- **The recorded fake gains no derivation.** The 57 new recordings are served exactly as recorded and replayed by `tests/unit/test_hindsight_contract.py` through the fake's transport. They are bank-scoped to the spike's own banks, so they never answer an Atlas test's request; the unscoped `GET /version` of 0.10.2 was kept out of `recordings/` (it would answer the gateway's version check after the 0.10.1 one) and lives in `rerun-0.10.2/monitoring/`.
- **The docs skill is re-vendored at `v0.10.2`** (`.agents/skills/hindsight-docs/`).
## 2026-10-02: A section's outcome comes from its own document (memory-quality ticket 03)

Production on 2026-10-02 had 1,907 sections recorded `failed`; every one sampled carried "Hindsight reported the operation cancelled with no error message": the owner cancelled those operations early in the rollout (study `docs/research/hindsight-memory-use.md`, finding 1). This entry refines "retention into memory" (its "Failures stay visible" bullet) and "queue pause and pacing".

- **Cancelled is not failed.** An operation Hindsight reports `cancelled` leaves each section it did not store in the retain state **`cancelled`**, with class `cancelled` and the operation's error text (or "Hindsight reported the operation cancelled with no error message"). The operation is classed `cancelled`. `atlas_retained_sections_total{outcome="cancelled"}` counts them apart from `failed`; `GET /api/v1/source-versions/{id}/memory` counts them under `cancelled`. A cancelled version is not a retained twin (a byte copy of it retains on its own). Migration `0068` moves the sections recorded `failed` with exactly that error to `cancelled`; the move is not in the audit chain (a migration), and the rows keep their error text.
- **The outcome rule.** Once an operation has ended, whatever its status, each pending section's outcome is read from **its own document** (`GET …/documents/{id}`): stored with facts is `completed` (its memory IDs listed), stored with none gets the one `reprocess` and is `zero_fact` after it, and absent (404) is decided by the operation: completed → `failed`, class `missing`; `cancelled` → `cancelled`; `permanent` → `failed` with the operation's error, class `permanent`. Before, a failed operation failed every pending section without reading any document.
- **Failure classes.** A section that is not in memory records why (`memory_document.error_class`): `cancelled`, `permanent`, `transient` or `missing`. An operation's class (`retain_error_class`): `cancelled` by status first; then `quota` and `unavailable` as before (pausing the queue, never bounded); then **`transient`**: a timeout ("timeout", "timed out") or a relayed server error ("Error code: 5xx", "HTTP 5xx", "internal server error", "server error"); anything else, including no message, `permanent`. Mental-model refreshes and replays keep the old three classes (`operation_error_class`).
- **Transient failures are bounded.** The sections a transient operation did not store with facts stay `pending`; each counts the failure (`transient_retries`); the poll raises `TransientFailure` as an outage does, so the queue pauses with its backoff (60 s, doubling) and the next attempt resubmits them under the same document IDs. A section whose count passes `ATLAS_RETAIN_TRANSIENT_RETRIES` (default 3) is `failed` with class `transient`, and that failure does not pause the queue. **Why pause:** "with the queue's backoff" is the only delay the queue has; a timeout from the model usually is the provider's, and the bound keeps a section that times out on its own from pausing the queue more than three times.
- **Partial extraction.** The operation's `result_metadata.extraction_errors_count` (Hindsight's `HINDSIGHT_API_FAIL_ON_EXTRACTION_ERRORS` is off on the cluster, so a retain with failed chunks still ends `completed`) is kept on each section it stored (`extraction_errors`). A section stored with facts by an operation reporting errors, never reprocessed, is retried once as part of the operation's `reprocess` (the same once-only counter as a zero-fact section); after it, it is completed with the new operation's count, and **partial** when that count is above zero (`partial` on the memory read's documents and its count; gauge `atlas_partial_sections`). The count is per operation, not per document, so every section of an operation with errors is retried. A section completed by a quota, outage or transient operation keeps the count but is not retried.
- **Retrying by class and company.** `atlas retention retry-failed` resets `failed` and `cancelled` sections; `--error-class` (repeatable) and `--company <slug>` narrow it, and it now enqueues **backfill** class by default (`--interactive` for the old behaviour; `--backfill` is accepted and is the default). It resets each section's class and transient count, and prints the sections by class. The backfill of memory-quality ticket 12 takes the cancelled backlog through this command.
- **What the health read (ticket 02) should add:** sections by state including `cancelled`; failed and cancelled sections by `error_class` (`atlas_sections_not_in_memory{state,error_class}`); partial sections (`extraction_errors > 0` on a completed section); operations by `error_class` including `transient` and `cancelled`.
- **Long Items:** not split. The error grouping (every failed section sampled was a cancellation) shows no section failing by size, so the spec's split is not built.
- **Fake, not recorded:** what a failed or cancelled operation leaves stored, a cancelled status and a non-zero `extraction_errors_count` were never recorded; the Hindsight fake derives them (listed with its derivations under "code review fix-up (spec axis)"). Not verified against Hindsight 0.10.2: whether a failed or cancelled batch stores some documents at all, whether a cancelled operation carries an error message, and whether `extraction_errors_count` counts chunks or items.

## 2026-10-02: What a retained section says (memory-quality ticket 04)

A retained section used to reach Memory with the context `<title>: <heading or anchor>` ("Q4 2026: chunk-004" for a transcript on production), no entities and only the provenance metadata (study, findings 4 and 5). Now each retain item says whose document it is, what kind, for which period and who speaks, and names the companies Atlas knows are in it. `atlas.retention.context`; profile `retain-v2`.

- **The context** is built by one function (`section_context`) from the Source Version and the section, as short plain sentences an extraction model reads before the section: (1) whose document and what kind: "Lumentum's annual report on Form 10-K for the period ended 2026-06-27", "exhibit 99.1 to Lumentum's current report on Form 8-K dated 2026-08-11", "the transcript of Lumentum's earnings call "<event>" for fiscal Q4 2026", "<company>'s document "<title>", imported by hand from <publisher>", "<company>'s announcement "<title>" on <publisher>"; the period is EDGAR's report date (the period's end for 10-K, 10-Q, 20-F and 40-F; the report's date for the others) or the transcript's fiscal period, and is left out when the source gives none; (2) "It was made public on <available_at's UTC date>."; (3) the section: its heading, "the cover page", or "part N of the document" for a chunk; (4) the speaker by source type: a company's own filing or announcement, "<company> is speaking: this is its own document."; a call transcript, "<company>'s management and analysts speak on the call; an analyst's question is not a statement by <company>."; a manual import, "<publisher> published it and is taken as the speaker." (Atlas doesn't know who wrote a document imported by hand; the publisher is what it knows).
- **What stays out of the context:** every identifier a reader could not use (the version's UUID, the accession, the CIK, the anchor, the file name) and anything Atlas did not read from the source (no layer, no theme, no judgement of the document).
- **`entities`** are the filer's canonical name (its `legal_name`, as written in the universe), then every other company Atlas has (universe companies and counterparties, the `company` table by slug) whose names the section's text mentions, by the matcher passage selection uses for entity windows (`atlas.claims.predicates.company_names` and `mentions`: display name, legal name and its stem, whole words, case-sensitive), each by its canonical name, type `ORG`. **They are sent with `resolve_entities: false`**: they are authoritative names, and ticket 01 recorded that resolution would otherwise be left to fuzzy matching, whose time signal is useless for filings months apart; given unresolved, one company is one entity in Memory under the name the entity hop lists by. The extractor's own short forms ("Coherent") stay entities beside it (ticket 01); a name inside another word ("Incoherent") is no mention.
- **Metadata** keeps the provenance keys unchanged and in their order (`source_version_id`, `section_anchor`, `char_start`, `char_end`, `available_at`) and gains display keys after them, each only when known: `company_name` (display name), `form`, `period` (as above). `extractor` stays last when set. The provenance resolver reads `source_version_id` and `section_anchor` only, so it ignores the new keys.
- **The retain profile:** each memory document records, when it is submitted (a retain, a reprocess or a resubmission), `retain_profile`, `retain_context` and `retain_entities` (the names sent). Migration `0062` sets `retain-v1` on every section recorded before it (a linked section, never submitted, has none) with no context or entities recorded. `GET /api/v1/source-versions/{id}/memory` shows them with `current_retain_profile`, so the backfill (ticket 12) finds the sections below the current profile. Bringing a stored section to the profile is the backfill's, by deleting and retaining (ticket 01): this ticket changes only what new retains send.
- **Replay and evaluation banks send the same:** a replay builds its items with the same `retain_item` and the same companies; an evaluation bank's sources are manual imports retained through the production `Retention`.
- **Room for observation scopes (ticket 06):** the item's fields are now `content, document_id, timestamp, context, metadata, tags, entities, resolve_entities`; `observation_scopes` is ticket 06's.
- **The recorded fake gains no derivation:** it already stores each retain item as sent (`fake.retained()`) and derives its memories with the item's context and metadata.

## 2026-10-02: Observation scopes: one per theme (memory-quality ticket 06)

Until now every retain item went without `observation_scopes`, so Hindsight consolidated under its default (`combined`): an observation's scope was the item's full tag set (`company:…`, `theme:…`, `source:…`, `doctype:…`, `form:…`), and an observation was only ever updated by memories with exactly that tag set. Nothing consolidated across a form, a source or a company (study `docs/research/hindsight-memory-use.md`, finding 2, seen on production).

- **Each retain item sends one explicit scope per theme tag**: a company in one theme sends `[["theme:photonics"]]`, a company in two sends one scope for each (in the tags' order). The company, source, document type and form tags stay on the item for filtering and are no longer a consolidation boundary. One function builds them from the item's tags (`atlas.retention.service.observation_scopes`, used by `retain_item`), so retention, replay banks and evaluation banks (whose live runs retain through the same `retain` jobs) send the same scopes. An item with no theme tag sends none, and Hindsight's default applies; `[[]]` would be the untagged global scope, which no strict recall returns, and `[]` falls back to `combined` anyway (retain docs, "`shared` vs `[[]]` vs `[]`").
- **No company scope.** Ticket 01 measured that consolidation makes one LLM request per scope a run touches (a one-scope item: 1 request; a two-scope item: 2), on the shared Codex subscription. A company scope beside the theme's would double consolidation for every section, and a theme's scope already draws on every document of every company in it. One scope per theme costs what the default cost.
- **What it gives up.** A theme observation carries the theme tag alone, no company tag. So a recall scoped to a company (`company_ids`, `any_strict` on `company:<id>`) returns that company's facts and no observation, and the per-company memory-health read lists no scope. Investigations, the Scout's pointers and the Skeptic recall across the theme, so nothing built today loses by it. A theme-scoped recall returns the theme's observations; each resolves to sections through its source facts as before, which may lie in several companies' documents, and every source is listed.
- **What is stored now keeps its old scopes** until it is retained again: a retain of unchanged content re-extracts nothing and whether facts consolidated before are re-scoped is untested (ticket 01). Re-consolidating the stored corpus is the backfill's (ticket 12).

## 2026-10-02: Recall as a reading index (memory-quality ticket 07)

Spec: `.scratch/atlas-memory-quality/spec.md`, "Recall as a reading index"; study `docs/research/hindsight-memory-use.md`, finding 3; behaviour on 0.10.2 from `docs/hindsight-feature-matrix.md` and `spikes/hindsight/recordings/recall_options/`. A pointer recall is how an investigation finds where to read, so it is asked for what a reading index needs, field by field:

- **`max_tokens`: `ATLAS_POINTER_RECALL_MAX_TOKENS`, default 8,192** (Hindsight's default is 4,096). Recall fills the budget with facts in rank order, counting only their text, so a larger budget is more memories and so more distinct sections per recall (`recall_options/01`: 8,192 returned the whole small bank's 23; `02`: 128 returned 3). Recall makes no LLM call, so the cost is the answer's size.
- **`budget: high`**: a deeper search (more candidates per retrieval arm before reranking). A recall is cheap next to an Investigator's call; depth is worth it when the answer decides what is read.
- **`prefer_observations: true`**: an observation in the results replaces the facts it was built from, and the freed places go to the next results, so no place is spent on the same fact twice (`04` against `05`: 12 results, none a source of an observation in the answer, against 23 with 11 such facts). An observation still points at all its sources' sections, so nothing pointed at is lost.
- **`query_timestamp`: the investigation's as-of time** (a replay's question: the replay's cutoff). It anchors recency and relative dates in the query; it **ranks, it does not filter** (`08` against `09`: the same 23 in another order). The as-of filter stays Atlas's own: a pointer to a Source Version available after the as-of time is never stored.
- **`include.source_facts`**: an observation's sources come in the same answer (`source_fact_ids` is null without it; `06`), with their `document_id`, `metadata` and `chunk_id`. **The provenance resolver uses them** and asks Hindsight for a memory only when a listed source is not in the answer's map: the budget cut it (`source_facts_truncated`) or the fact no longer exists. A missing fact is then asked for exactly as before, so every citation state (resolved, unverified, broken, and their reasons) is the one the per-memory requests give. A recall without the include is resolved as before (the observation looked up for its sources).
- **`types`** is accepted by the gateway and the public recall, and not sent by pointer recalls: every type is recalled by default, and `prefer_observations` needs an observation and a raw type together.
- **Scores and entities are kept; the score is recorded before it is used.** A reading pointer stores the recall's `scores.final` (`score`) and its memory's entity names (`entity_names`), insert-only like the rest of the row (migration `0063`; null for pointers recorded before). The investigation read and the Research Snapshot carry both. Pointed companies are still ranked by reciprocal rank and passages chosen as before: a score is relative to its own recall only (it can exceed 1, and an arm that didn't surface a result has none), so whether it should weight anything is for the pilot review to judge from the recorded values first. The chunk ID and the entity IDs stay in the recall answer (`RecallResult.entities`, each memory's `chunk_id`; the public recall returns them per memory) for chunk-exact pointers (ticket 08) and the entity hop (ticket 09); the pointer row does not copy them.
- **`POST /api/v1/memory/recall`** accepts `max_tokens`, `types`, `prefer_observations`, `query_timestamp` and `include_source_facts`, all optional; without them the request Hindsight receives is the one it always was. Each memory comes back with its `scores`, `entities` (name and entity ID) and `chunk_id`, and the answer with `source_facts_truncated`.
- **Fake:** a derived recall serves source facts when asked (a derivation, listed with the fake's others above); the other fields change nothing in a derived recall, so their effect on ranking is checked only by the recordings and, live, by the probe set the lead runs.

## 2026-10-02: Chunk-exact pointers (memory-quality ticket 08)

Spec: `.scratch/atlas-memory-quality/spec.md`, "Chunk-exact pointers". Precondition settled by ticket 01 (`docs/hindsight-feature-matrix.md`, question (b); recordings `chunks/01`–`06`, `recall_options/07`, `10`): a chunk's text is a verbatim slice of the retained content (the section's parsed text), the API gives no offsets, and a chunk is placed by finding its text in the section in `chunk_index` order, each search starting where the previous chunk ended. Before this, a pointer's window was the one whose words matched the memory's text best, and a long section could point at the wrong one: a fact paraphrases, and the window sharing most of its words need not be where it was said.

- **The placing rule.** Pointer recalls (the Scout's and the Skeptic's) ask for the chunks of their results (`include.chunks`, beside `include.source_facts`). Each resolved fact's chunk is **located** in its section (`atlas.research.chunks`): the chunk the answer carries is used when it settles the place alone (its text whole, not `truncated`, and occurring exactly once in the section, where the ordered search could only find it too); otherwise the document's chunks are listed (`HindsightGateway.document_chunks`, once per document per recall, the conformance ticket's read; no second way was added) and located in order. A pointer whose memory has a fact whose chunk lies in the pointed section is **`placed_by` `chunk`** and records the chunk's ID and its span in the parsed text (`chunk_char_start`, `chunk_char_end`); an **observation's** pointer is placed by its source facts' chunks (the first located in that section). Otherwise (no chunk, a chunk not found verbatim, a document Hindsight no longer lists) it is **`placed_by` `match`** and the best-match window stands, as before. A listing refused with a 4xx other than 429 places nothing; a 429 or 5xx is the recall's failure (a quota or outage pauses the task, as before).
- **Passage selection** (`atlas.claims.selection`): a chunk-placed pointer chooses the window of the pointed section that the chunk's span **starts in**, and its tag is `pointer:<query_index>:chunk` (the Skeptic's `pointer:<checklist_item>:chunk`); a match-placed one keeps `pointer:<query_index>`. The span is used only when the parse read has the section at the offsets the pointer recorded (the chunk was located in the recorded parse; a re-parsed version's sections may lie elsewhere), else the best match places it. **Deviation from the ticket's wording:** the ticket says the window is "extended by the existing rule when the span crosses a window"; selection has no such rule (windows are fixed cuts of `PASSAGE_CHARS`, 3,000, at line breaks), so a span that crosses a window boundary chooses the window it starts in, which holds the chunk's opening; at Hindsight's default chunk size (3,000) a chunk is at most one window long. Whether to read the next window too is for the pilot review to judge from the recorded spans.
- **What says which rule placed a pointer:** each pointer of `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` and of a Research Snapshot (`placed_by`, `chunk_id`, `chunk_char_start`, `chunk_char_end`); each passage's `selected_by` (so the Evidence tray's `passage_selected_by`); the research card's `read`, per document, `pointers_placed_by` (passages per rule). The public `POST /api/v1/memory/recall` accepts `include_chunks` (default false: the request Hindsight receives is unchanged) and then gives each resolved source its `chunk_char_start`/`chunk_char_end`; every source now carries its fact's `chunk_id`.
- **A chunk is located, never quoted.** Its text finds a span of the archived section and goes no further: it is not stored on the pointer (only its ID and span), not returned by the public recall, not quoted and never sent to a role. Memory stays an index: the passage read is archived text chosen by where the fact came from.
- **Stored** insert-only like the rest of the row (migration `0065`): `placed_by` (`match` for every pointer recorded before, which is how they were placed), `chunk_id`, and the span, set exactly when `placed_by` is `chunk` (a check constraint). The downgrade refuses while a chunk-placed pointer exists.
- **Fake:** the chunk listing and `include.chunks` of derived documents are derivations (`derive_chunks`, off by default; listed with the fake's others above). The conformance rehearsal turns it on, and check 7 passes on it; live, check 7 is the measure of the precondition on the owner's bank.

## 2026-10-02: The bank template's missions and the layer label (memory-quality ticket 05)

Template `1.2.0` (`configs/hindsight/bank-template.json`); study `docs/research/hindsight-memory-use.md`, findings 4 and 5; recordings `entity_labels/`, `dry_run_extract/`, `reprocess/03`.

- **`retain_mission` keeps its include list and gains an ignore list and attribution.** Hindsight injects it beside its built-in extraction rules (the docs: it narrows focus, it doesn't replace the logic), and its best-practices page calls the ignore list as important as the include list. Ignored: safe-harbor and forward-looking-statement disclaimers, officer certifications and signature blocks, exhibit lists and indexes, tables of contents and cross-references, generic risk language that names no product, counterparty, site or figure, legal and accounting boilerplate about the filing, and call logistics. Kept from 1.1.0: stated apart from inferred, figures with units and periods exactly as stated. Added: each statement attributed to who made it (the filer, a named executive, an analyst asking, a quoted third party; an analyst's question is not the company's statement), hedged and forward-looking statements kept hedged, and the include list names the supply chain (who supplies whom, sites, lead times, pricing). A risk that names a product, counterparty, site or figure is still extracted: that is where a constraint shows. `retain_custom_instructions` (which replaces the built-in rules) is not used.
- **`observations_mission` stands alone.** Setting it replaces Hindsight's built-in consolidation rules entirely (`developer/observations.md`, `configuration.md`): durable, specific beliefs grounded in their source memories, ephemeral state filtered out, contradictions tracked with temporal markers rather than overwritten. 1.1.0's one sentence had dropped all of that. 1.2.0 restates what Atlas needs of it (durable and specific, grounded, ephemeral and generic material left out, a change recorded with both dates or periods instead of overwriting, a contradiction kept with both sides and who said each) and adds Atlas's own rules: one observation per company and subject, industry-level only from several companies' facts with those companies named, who supplies or depends on whom by name and direction, figures with unit, currency and period, attribution and hedges kept, and repetition across filings or by the same source is one source, never corroboration. Consolidation strategies (a mission per scope, 0.10.2) are not used (ticket 01).
- **One label group, `layer`, `multi-values`, optional, `tag: true`.** Its values and their descriptions are the Claim layer taxonomy's (`atlas.claims.LAYERS`: `name` and `covers`), in its order; `tests/unit/test_bank_template_config.py` fails when they differ, so a change to the taxonomy changes the template (and its version) with it. The group's description says a material named alone (InP, GaAs, silicon photonics) is no layer, as the Claim rule says (`SHARED_LAYER_TERMS`). With `tag: true` each label is also a tag (`layer:<value>`), so a recall can be scoped to a layer (`entity_labels/05`: `any_strict` returned only facts carrying it); labels never merge by name similarity. A layer label is the extractor's classification of a fact, an aid to recall: never a Claim's layer and never Evidence of one (a Claim's layer still needs its quote: "A layer only when the quote supports one", above). A second group for the statement kind is left out until the first is measured (spec).
- **Applying it** is `atlas hindsight apply-template` as before (dry run, import, recorded version `1.2.0`). The research bank's facts retained before carry no labels: `reprocess` applies labels configured after the retain without a new context (`reprocess/03`), and the backfill ticket (12) decides whether to reprocess or re-retain. The dev bank template (`atlas-dev-bank-template.json`) has its own missions and is left alone.
- **The template is checked against 0.10.2's schema.** `tests/unit/test_bank_template_config.py` validates the manifest against the recorded `rerun-0.10.2/bank_templates/01-schema.json` and checks every bank, label-group and label-value field is one the schema names (the schema accepts unknown fields, which Hindsight would ignore silently).
- **Fake, not recorded:** the import of a changed bank config and `script_fact_labels` are derived (the list of the fake's derivations above).
- **Not measured yet:** the old and new missions on real sections. `spikes/hindsight/mission_compare_05.py` runs `dry-run-extract` on the local spike Hindsight for three recorded sections (a Lumentum 10-K Item 1A risk factor, the Lumentum EX-99.1's "About" and forward-looking-statements text, and the synthetic TradingView transcript), old template against new, with at most 12 LLM requests (6 needed); the observations mission can't be compared that way (it acts at consolidation). The lead runs it; the comparison note goes in `docs/research/` and the texts are adjusted once from it.

## 2026-10-02: Reflect and the models: grounded and counted (memory-quality ticket 10)

Spec: `.scratch/atlas-memory-quality/spec.md`, "Reflect and the mental models". Study: `docs/research/hindsight-memory-use.md`, finding 6. Recordings: `reflect_options/01`–`02`, `tagged_mental_model/01`–`03`, the template schema `bank_templates/01-schema` and the 0.10.2 OpenAPI (`MentalModelTrigger`, `BankTemplateConfig`). Bank template **1.3.0** (ticket 05 edits the missions and labels as 1.2.0; this ticket touches only the mental models and the reflect fields of the bank section).

- **A reflect asks at a real depth and reads no mental model by default.** `POST /api/v1/memory/reflect` takes `budget` (`low`/`mid`/`high`, default `mid`; Hindsight's own default is `low`, which every reflect ran at before) and `exclude_mental_models` (default true). Both are stored on the research answer (`research_answer.budget`, `.exclude_mental_models`, migration `0066`; a row recorded before says `low` and false, what it ran with) and returned by `GET /api/v1/memory/reflect/{id}`. A replay's reflects send `mid` and the exclusion too, so a replay asks as production research does.
- **A cited world fact resolves in one hop.** On 0.10.2 a reflect answer's cited world fact carries its `document_id`, `chunk_id`, `tags` and `metadata` (`reflect_options/02`), so the resolver checks it against the ledger from the answer itself, with no memory lookup. An observation still goes through its source facts (its `document_id` is null); a fact cited without a document (a mental model's `based_on`, which carries none, or a fact deleted since) is looked up as before, so a deleted one is still `broken`.
- **A mental model an answer read is reported, not dropped.** `based_on.mental_models` becomes one citation each, kind `mental_model`, state `unverified`, reason `mental_model`, `memory_id` the model's ID, no sources: a model is Hindsight's own synthesis and never resolves to a section. It counts as unverified, so an answer resting on a model shows it.
- **Can a theme-scoped reflect see the models?** Yes, when it doesn't exclude them: `reflect_options/01` (`tags: [company:aurora]`, `any_strict`, no exclusion) cited the model tagged `company:aurora` (`tagged_mental_model/01`). Both Atlas models are now tagged `theme:photonics`, so a reflect scoped to that theme with `exclude_mental_models: false` may read them (and they are reported); a reflect scoped by company tags only matches neither model's tag and, by the docs' rule for strict scopes, sees neither. The models were untagged before, so no strictly scoped Atlas reflect ever saw them (study, finding 6).
- **The models rest on the theme's memories, never on each other.** Each model has `tags: ["theme:photonics"]`: a tagged model's refresh reads only memories carrying every model tag (`all_strict`, the schema's default for a tagged model), and every section Atlas retains is tagged with its themes. Each trigger has `exclude_mental_models: true` (no model reads another) and `keep_trace: true` (Hindsight keeps the latest refresh's trace: scope, retrieval counts, tool and LLM calls), and `budget: "mid"` stated (a refresh's null budget is `mid` on 0.10.2; stated so it's visible).
- **Reflect's own recall returns no raw chunk and shows the facts behind an observation.** The bank section sets `recall_include_chunks: false` (the internal recall of reflect and of a refresh; chunk text has no memory identity, so a citation of it could only be unverified) and `reflect_source_facts_max_tokens: 4096` (off, `-1`, by default; with a budget, `search_observations` returns each observation's source facts). Both are per-bank fields the recorded schema names. The public recall endpoint is unaffected: its `include.chunks` is per request.
- **Atlas's daily job is the only scheduler.** Template 1.1.0 gave Hindsight `refresh_cron: "0 6 * * *"`, half an hour before Atlas's 06:30 job, and Atlas skips a model refreshed inside its 12 h interval: so by the configuration Hindsight made every scheduled refresh, outside Atlas's queue, its pause and its budget. Of the two ways to stop that, the schema's own was chosen: **`refresh_cron: null`** ("null = no schedule", the schema's default; recorded stored as sent in `tagged_mental_model/03-get`) with `refresh_after_consolidation: false`, so Hindsight never refreshes a model on its own. A cron that never fires (e.g. 31 February) was rejected: it relies on a parser's handling of an impossible date and reads as a schedule. The runaway rule stays and tightens: a trigger must say `refresh_after_consolidation: false` and `refresh_cron: null` explicitly, give a positive `min_refresh_interval_seconds`, and say `exclude_mental_models: true`; anything else makes the template invalid before any call (`atlas hindsight apply-template` exits 2). The interval stays in the template: Atlas's job enforces it, and Hindsight would apply it to an automatic trigger if one were ever set.
- **Who refreshed.** `mental_model_refresh.refreshed_by` (migration `0066`): `atlas` on a refresh Atlas's job completed; on a skip, who made the refresh it found: `atlas` when a refresh Atlas completed left that `last_refreshed_at`, `hindsight` otherwise (with no cron, that is a refresh asked for in Hindsight itself: its API, MCP or UI, which no interval holds); null on older rows, submitted or failed ones, and a skip of a model never refreshed. Shown on `GET /api/v1/mental-models[/{id}]` with each refresh record, beside the model's `tags` and its trigger's `budget`, `exclude_mental_models` and `keep_trace`.
- **Every reflect and every refresh Atlas submits is one `codex` unit.** The `codex` sweep (`atlas.jobs.budget`) gains three sources: a `reflect_submission` row (insert-only, migration `0066`; one per attempt of a `reflect` job, recorded just before the request, so a retried reflect counts each time it was sent), a `mental_model_refresh` row that submitted a refresh (has an operation; a skip has none and costs nothing), and a replay's `replay_answer` (its reflect; a replay's reflect that failed is not counted, since no row is written). Rows recorded before `0066` are counted at their own time, as `0028` did for operations. Hindsight reports no tokens for a refresh and Atlas can't see Codex tokens, so the unit is one request, as for a retain batch. `reflect` and `refresh_mental_model` were already `codex` kinds, so a spent window holds them as before; now they also fill it.
- **Not live.** Every behaviour here is tested against the recorded fake (its new derivations are listed in the "recorded fake" entry above). Whether `recall_include_chunks: false` and `reflect_source_facts_max_tokens` change what a refresh reads, and what a `mid` reflect costs on Codex, is for the lead's live run after the template is applied (`docs/runbooks.md`, "Universe rollout and quota budgets").

## 2026-10-02: Memory works as advertised: the conformance check (memory-quality ticket 14)

The owner's requirement (2026-10-02): before Atlas is benchmarked, one command says whether Memory behaves as Hindsight's documentation and the memory-quality spec promise. `scripts/memory-conformance.sh`; `atlas.conformance`; runbook "Memory conformance"; the precondition in `docs/evaluation-methodology.md` §10.

- **Known answers come from the pilot's reviews and follow the research method.** `configs/memory/known-answers.yaml` holds 25 answers over the five probe questions. Each names a sentence a reviewer can check and is labelled with its hop (demand, system, module, chip, substrate, feedstock, equipment) and its part of the bottleneck test (demand against capacity, second source, pricing power, share of BOM, financing or dilution: the Serenity analysis's M1 to M6 and M10), so recall is reported per hop and per test part as well as per question. `basis` says where each comes from: 17 quote a fact a review quotes, 5 are the archive's sentence for a fact a review names as missing (export controls, InP supply concentration, MOCVD/tool capacity, customer concentration), 3 come from the question alone (qualification cycles; hyperscale demand, twice). Anchors were taken only where the sentence was found in the recorded fixture of the same document by Atlas's own parser and sectioner (a unit test checks each); 5 answers name documents the fixtures don't hold (the March 8-Ks, Coherent's August slides, the truncated 10-Q) and have `section: null`, filled from the first live run. Investigations 2 to 5 are unreviewed, so their questions rest on investigation 1's documents; no answer serves share of BOM, and the report shows that row empty on purpose.
- **An answer that does not resolve is an error, not a miss.** Each is resolved against the running Atlas before scoring: the document (URL, accession, or form and filing date within a day, the sentence choosing among a filing's documents) must hold the sentence (typographic quotes, hyphens, dashes and whitespace folded), and a given anchor must be the section the sentence lies in. A question is recalled as an investigation's pointer recalls are (the question and its four Scout-style queries, theme-scoped); the five answers' resolved sections are merged by rank.
- **The behaviours run on a throwaway bank prepared through the real retain path.** A fresh Atlas retains the recorded fixtures of Lumentum (8-K exhibit, 10-Q) and Coherent (10-K, 10-Q; its Item 5 names Lumentum, which gives the entity hop something to find) and the synthetic transcript (TradingView's fake; synthetic content in a throwaway bank) with triage off, so Atlas makes no LLM call; 19 sections, 5 retain operations. The gateway may now delete a bank prefixed `atlas-conformance-` as well as `atlas-replay-`, and nothing else.
- **A check is pending until its ticket is on the branch, read from the public shape the ticket describes**: an OpenAPI property or enum value (`retain_state` `cancelled` for 03, `prefer_observations` for 07, `placed_by` for 08, `exclude_mental_models` for 10), what the retain requests carry (`entities` for 04, `observation_scopes` for 06), the bank template (a `layer` label group for 05) or a setting (`entity_hop_max_companies` for 09). Checks 1 to 9 were written against those shapes before their tickets merged; check 10 (every citation of a recall resolves) runs today. Check 5 tests the theme scope only: ticket 06 dropped the company scope after ticket 01 measured its cost, so "an observation in the company's scope" of the ticket's wording no longer applies. Check 7 tests the precondition of a chunk-placed pointer (every fact's chunk occurs verbatim in its section, found in `chunk_index` order) rather than an investigation's pointers, so it needs no LLM call.
- **The gateway gains two reads for the checks**, each from a 0.10.2 recording: `document_chunks` (`chunks/03-list-chunks`) and `entity_memories` (`entity_memories/01-by-entity-and-tag`). Tickets 08 and 09 may add their own; one of each is kept at integration.
- **Caps** are counting proxies (the live verification's `CappedProxy`), two in a chain: retain operations (10) and reflect, refresh or consolidation requests (6). Hindsight's extraction requests happen inside Hindsight and cannot be capped by Atlas; the live report reads the bank's own LLM request log before the bank is deleted.
- **The fake:** the memory-health listings now serve ticket 01's recordings (the entry under "code review fix-up" above); the hand-written fixtures are deleted. No new derivation: checks 2 to 9 need derivations (given entities on derived facts, layer tags, per-scope observations, the chunk listing and the entity listing of derived documents, a reflect citing derived facts) that their own tickets add when they make the behaviour, and the rehearsal reports them pending until then.
